- **Finding:** Three incompatible tables are in common use: (a) C256-based just series 256 / 288 / 320 / 341.3 / 384 / 426.7 / 480 Hz (root→crown = C D E F G A B; ratios 9:8, 10:9, 16:15, i.e. just intonation, not Pythagorean; 341.3 = 256×4/3, 426.7 = 256×5/3) — popularized by Jonathan Goldman's Chakra Chants and 'harmonic spectrum' fork sets; (b) 'Solfeggio' 396 / 417 / 528 / 639 / 741 / 852 / 963 Hz — derived by Joseph Puleo via numerological reduction of Numbers 7:12-83, published in Horowitz & Puleo, Healing Codes for the Biological Apocalypse (1999); the chakra assignment and 174/285 Hz were added later by vendors; no link to Gregorian chant or Guido d'Arezzo; (c) Cousto planetary: root = Earth-day 194.18 Hz (Cousto's own assignment), heart = Earth-year 136.10, with Moon 210.42, Sun 126.22, Mercury 141.27, Venus 221.23, Platonic year 172.06 Hz assigned to chakras only by later vendors.
- **Usable parameters:** If a chakra ladder is wanted, the C256 just series is internally consonant with a 136.1 Hz C# only if transposed (C#256→ use 136.10×2 = 272.2 as 'root' and build 9:8, 5:4, 4:3, 3:2, 5:3, 15:8 above it: 272.2 / 306.2 / 340.3 / 362.9 / 408.3 / 453.7 / 510.4 Hz). Solfeggio tones are mutually dissonant and clash with a C# drone; use only as isolated episodes if at all.
- **Notes:** All three tables date from 1978-1999; no scripture or controlled study supports any chakra-frequency pairing.
- **Source:** Goldman set https://www.7chakracolors.com/chakra/frequencies/ ; fork set https://soundtherapyshop.com/products/harmonic-spectrum-tuning-forks-set-of-8 ; Solfeggio history https://www.soundmedicineacademy.com/pages/sound-healing-blog/solfeggio-frequencies ; https://muzencosmos.com/en/solfeggio-en/solfeggio-history-origins-en/ ; Cousto table https://www.planetware.de/octave/The_CoOc_Tuning.pdf ; chakra-vs-planet note https://help.soundhealinglab.com/article/42-chakra-vs-planetary-notes

### Hindu/Vedic — Gayatri mantra and Samaveda chant parameters

- **Evidence level:** traditional (a lineage's own teaching, not a tested claim)
- **Finding:** Gayatri (Rig Veda 3.62.10): 24 syllables in three padas of 8 (gayatri metre), preceded by OM bhur bhuvah svah; its petition 'dhiyo yo nah prachodayat' = 'may (Savitr) impel/illumine our intellect' — the classical 'illumination of mind' mantra, recited at the three sandhyas (dawn/noon/dusk), traditionally 108×. Samaveda chant: Vedic recitation uses three accents (udatta high, anudatta low, svarita falling); Sama singers expanded these to a 7-note DESCENDING scale (sources give Ma-Ga-Ri-Sa-Ni-Dha-Pa), with prolonged vowels, inserted stobha syllables (ho, hau, hai) and three main recensions (Kauthuma, Ranayaniya, Jaiminiya).
- **Usable parameters:** Gayatri: 24 syllables at ~1 syllable/0.5 s ≈ 12-15 s per recitation incl. OM; 3 reps per block; use 3-tone contour (e.g. 136.1 / 153.1 / 121.0 Hz = Sa, Re-ish, Ni-ish). Sama-style: descending 7-note melismas on long vowels, tempo ~50-60 BPM.
- **Notes:** The Rig Veda citation 3.62.10 is standard; 10.16.3 cited by one site is wrong.
- **Source:** Gayatri https://yogainternational.com/article/view/the-gayatri-mantra-awakening-to-the-sun/ ; https://shlokam.org/shloka/gayatri-mantra.htm ; Samaveda musicology https://sreenivasaraos.com/2012/09/15/music-in-sama-veda/ ; https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Svara ; Vedic chanting intro https://www.srimatham.com/uploads/5/5/4/9/5549439/introduction_to_vedic_chanting.pdf

### Hindu/Yogic — Yoga Nidra session architecture (Satyananda, 1976)

- **Evidence level:** moderate
- **Finding:** Satyananda's Yoga Nidra is an 8-stage script: (1) preparation/settling, (2) sankalpa (short positive resolve, stated 3×), (3) rotation of consciousness through 61 body points starting right thumb, right side then left, then back/front, (4) breath awareness/counting, (5) feelings & opposites (heavy/light, hot/cold), (6) visualization (rapid images then symbolic scene), (7) sankalpa repeated, (8) externalization. Length 20-45 min; the rotation alone 5-15 min. Origin: tantric nyasa. Stated purpose: conscious deep sleep (hypnagogic state), 'planting' the sankalpa in the subconscious — the yogic ancestor of 'manifestation' practice.
- **Usable parameters:** Track skeleton for 45-60 min: 0-4 settle; 4-6 sankalpa (3×); 6-18 rotation (61 points, ~10 s each, right side first); 18-26 breath count (descending 27→1, exhale-weighted); 26-32 opposites; 32-45 visualization (the 'inner sound' and 'thunder/light' episodes go here); 45-48 sankalpa; 48-52 externalize.
- **Notes:** Structure well documented; clinical trials of Yoga Nidra show relaxation/sleep benefits (not reviewed here); 'manifestation' is the tradition's own framing of sankalpa.
- **Source:** Swami Satyananda Saraswati, Yoga Nidra (Bihar School of Yoga, 1976; 6th ed. 1998); overview https://www.yogapoint.com/mainstory/TopstoryContents/yoga_nidra.htm ; 61-point rotation https://suryachandrayoga.co.uk/rotation-of-consciousness-where-the-mind-goes-prana-flows/ ; study table https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC8175770/table/T1

### OM chanting — what imaging/EEG actually show

- **Evidence level:** weak
- **Finding:** Kalyani et al. 2011 (pilot fMRI, n=12): audible OM chanting vs 'ssss' control produced DEACTIVATION of limbic areas (orbitofrontal, anterior cingulate, parahippocampal gyri, thalami, hippocampi, right amygdala) with no significant activations; authors hypothesize vibration reaching the auricular branch of the vagus (pattern resembles tVNS). EEG studies are mixed: some report theta increase and decreased theta frequency during OM; alpha results inconsistent.
- **Usable parameters:** Audible self-chanted OM (the effect is attributed to the listener's own laryngeal/ear vibration, not to listening). Guided OM: inhale 4 s, 'A-U-M' 12-15 s with M (nasal hum) ≥ 50% of duration, 9-11 rounds.
- **Notes:** Small samples; vagal mechanism is the authors' hypothesis.
- **Source:** Kalyani BG et al. Int J Yoga 2011;4(1):3-6 https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC3099099/ ; EEG review https://dergipark.org.tr/en/download/article-file/4840263

### Tibetan Buddhist — Gyuto/Gyume tantric 'chord' chanting

- **Evidence level:** moderate
- **Finding:** Smith, Stevens & Tomlinson (JASA 1967) measured Gyuto monks producing a chord from one voice: a fundamental in the 60-75 Hz range ('seventy cycles per second', Katy Butler's account of Huston Smith's recording: bass B ≈ 61.7 Hz, with a strongly amplified D# near middle C ≈ 311 Hz = 5th harmonic, and a faint F# ≈ 740 Hz = 12th harmonic). Gyuto emphasises the 10th-harmonic major third; Gyume a different overtone (5th/6th). The chant is 'dbyangs' used in Yamantaka/Guhyasamaja/Chakrasamvara sadhanas — the voice is the deity's voice; it is not for listeners.
- **Usable parameters:** Synthesized or sung low drone at 61.7 Hz (B1) or 68.05 Hz (C#2, sub-octave of 136.1) with boosted 5th harmonic (308.7 / 340.3 Hz) and 10th harmonic (617 / 680 Hz), formant-filtered vowel 'o'→'a'; phrases 20-40 s with breath gaps; tempo ~40 BPM syllabic pulse.
- **Notes:** The exact Hz in the 1967 paper could not be read (paywalled); 60-75 Hz is from secondary reports of that study.
- **Source:** H. Smith, K.N. Stevens, R.S. Tomlinson, 'On an Unusual Mode of Chanting by Certain Tibetan Lamas', J. Acoust. Soc. Am. 41(5):1262-1264 (1967) https://pubs.aip.org/asa/jasa/article/41/5/1262/693779/ ; Katy Butler, 'Vibration' http://www.katybutler.com/author/articles/thegyutomonks/ ; Gyuto Order https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gyuto_Order ; 'Multiphonic vocalizations' JASA 96(5) suppl. 1994 https://pubs.aip.org/asa/jasa/article/96/5_Supplement/3342/741613/

### Tibetan Buddhist — Singing bowls: measured acoustics and the one EEG study

- **Evidence level:** moderate
- **Finding:** Bowls have inharmonic partials; each mode is a near-degenerate pair split by a few Hz by wall asymmetry, producing audible beating of ~2-10 Hz (the 'wobble'), plus beating tied to the puja (striker) rotation rate when rubbed (Inácio, Henrique & Antunes 2006 model; mode frequencies scale ≈ n²). The only EEG study (2023, n=17, F3/F4, 5 min) used ONE bowl with fundamental 482.61 Hz and a 6.68 Hz beat and found delta/theta power rising and alpha/beta/gamma falling over 5 minutes. Fundamentals of common bowls span roughly 100-600 Hz (vendor spectra show e.g. 176.5, 289.1, 482.6 Hz).
- **Usable parameters:** Use real bowl samples or additive synthesis with mode pairs split 3-8 Hz (e.g. 136.1 & 142.5 Hz → 6.4 Hz beat; 272.2 & 278.9 Hz → 6.7 Hz theta-range beat); fundamentals chosen near C#3 (138.6), G#3 (207.7), C#4 (277.2) to sit in the drone; strike every 20-40 s; rubbed sustain 30-60 s.
- **Notes:** Beating in bowls is monaural (acoustic) and so reaches both ears — a different mechanism from headphone binaural beats; EEG evidence is one small study.
- **Source:** Inácio O, Henrique LL, Antunes J, 'The Dynamics of Tibetan Singing Bowls', Acta Acustica 2006 http://projects.itn.pt/VibroImpacts/Ref_A.pdf ; Terwagne & Bush 2011 arXiv:1106.5657 (withdrawn) ; EEG: https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10298245/ ; physical model https://ccrma.stanford.edu/~carmenng/250b/icmc2002.pdf

### Tibetan Buddhist — Dungchen long horns, tingsha, damaru/kangling (Chöd)

- **Evidence level:** traditional (a lineage's own teaching, not a tested claim)
- **Finding:** Dungchen (1.3->3 m) play 2-3 pedal notes; a 1.7 m horn has fundamental ≈ 58 Hz (B♭1); 1-D acoustic models predict its resonances within ±25 cents (Kemp et al.). Tingsha: small bronze cymbal pairs (6-10 cm) with a high pitch (vendors say >2 kHz), usually slightly detuned so the pair beats. Chöd (Machig Labdrön, 1055-1149): practitioner plays damaru (double-headed hand drum, continuous rolling pulse) and bell while chanting, blows the kangling (human-thighbone trumpet) to summon spirits, visualizes offering the body as food to demons in charnel grounds at night, shouts PHAT to 'cut' ego-clinging — the Tibetan practice most explicitly about commerce with 'other realms'.
- **Usable parameters:** Dungchen-like layer: 58 Hz (B♭1) and 68 Hz long tones, 15-30 s swells, slight pitch slide; tingsha accents at 2.5-4 kHz pair detuned 5-15 Hz; damaru pulse ≈ 4-6 strokes/s (theta-range roll) under the 'dissolution' section; a single PHAT-like transient to mark 'cutting'.
- **Notes:** Tingsha Hz are vendor claims (anecdotal); dungchen 58 Hz from a secondary summary of the acoustic paper.
- **Source:** 'Acoustics of the Tibetan long trumpet' https://www.researchgate.net/publication/272529513_Acoustics_of_the_Tibetan_long_trumpet ; dungchen organology https://organology.net/instrument/dungchen/ ; Chöd https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ch%C3%B6d ; Tsultrim Allione, Feeding Your Demons (2008) https://www.lionsroar.com/feeding-demons-tsultrim-allione-on-joyful-giving/ ; tingsha https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tingsha

### Tibetan Buddhist — Tummo/'vase breathing' (Kozhevnikov et al. 2013)

- **Evidence level:** moderate
- **Finding:** PLoS ONE 2013: in expert nuns in eastern Tibet, g-tummo (forceful 'vase' breathing: inhale, hold with abdominal contraction and pelvic lift, visualize flames in the spine) raised axillary temperature, with core rises up to 38.3 °C reported; Western non-meditators using vase breathing alone (without visualization) also raised temperature modestly. EEG alpha/beta increases accompanied the forceful-breath phases. The 'wet sheets' legend is NOT evidence (sheets dry in cold air anyway).
- **Usable parameters:** If a heat/energy segment is wanted: cycles of inhale 5 s → hold 10-15 s (vase) → slow exhale 8 s, 5-7 rounds, with cue 'flame at the navel rising up the spine'; keep holds short for untrained listeners.
- **Notes:** Small n; temperature effect is real but modest; breath-holds contraindicated in pregnancy, cardiovascular disease.
- **Source:** Kozhevnikov M, Elliott J, Shephard J, Gramann K. PLoS ONE 8(3):e58244 (2013) https://dash.harvard.edu/bitstreams/7312037c-dd26-6bd4-e053-0100007fdf3b/download ; summary https://www.sciencedaily.com/releases/2013/04/130408084858.htm

### Tibetan Buddhist — Bardo Thödol: the sounds of the Chönyid bardo ('other realms')

- **Evidence level:** traditional (a lineage's own teaching, not a tested claim)
- **Finding:** In the bardo of dharmatā (chönyid), after the clear light, consciousness meets 'sounds, lights and rays' together; the innate sound of reality 'reverberates like a thousand thunders simultaneously' (Evans-Wentz 1927; Fremantle & Trungpa 1975 'the natural sound of dharmata'); the instruction repeated at every stage is 'recognize these as the projections of your own mind; do not fear them; they cannot harm a mental body'. Fear of the bright five-coloured lights pulls one toward dull lights and lower rebirth. The text is read aloud to the dying/dead over 49 days — 'liberation through hearing'.
- **Usable parameters:** Climax design (minutes ~35-42 of a 60-min track): build to a 20-60 Hz rumble + layered thunder transients + five-colour tone cluster (e.g. white = full spectrum, blue 136, yellow 170, red 204, green 227, white-noise shimmer), peaking ≤ 80 dB SPL, with a calm spoken cue 'this is the natural sound of your own mind; recognize it; do not be afraid' — then abrupt return to the 136.1 drone (the 'clear light' return).
- **Notes:** The text presents hearing the sound as liberation only through recognition, never as travel.
- **Source:** Evans-Wentz, The Tibetan Book of the Dead (1927) https://isharonline.org/wp-content/uploads/The-Tibetan-Book-of-the-Dead.pdf ; Chönyid Bardo text https://www.onelittleangel.com/sacred/books/tibetan-book-of-the-dead.asp?mc=2&lg=0 ; Fremantle & Trungpa (Shambhala 1975); overview https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bardo_Thodol

### Tibetan Buddhist/Bön — Dzogchen 'sound, light and rays' and the A-OM-HUM three-syllable scheme

- **Evidence level:** traditional (a lineage's own teaching, not a tested claim)
- **Finding:** Bön Dzogchen (Zhang Zhung Nyan Gyud) and Nyingma thögal describe the base manifesting as sgra 'od zer gsum — sounds, lights, rays — in dark retreat and sky-gazing; practice is to recognize them as one's own energy. Bön and Vajrayana sadhanas place a white A (or OM) at the forehead, red OM (or AH) at the throat, blue HUM at the heart, emitting white/red/blue light; the Bön root mantra is A OM HUM (and the 'A' alone as the sound of the natural state).
- **Usable parameters:** Three-syllable chant block: 'A' (forehead, high/bright, e.g. 272 Hz), 'OM' (throat, mid 204 Hz), 'HUM' (heart, low 136 Hz), 7-21 rounds, each ~6 s; pair with white/red/blue light cues.
- **Notes:** Nyingma order is OM (forehead) AH (throat) HUM (heart); Bön order A OM HUM.
- **Source:** J.M. Reynolds, The Practice of Dzogchen in the Zhang-Zhung Tradition of Tibet (Vajra 2005) https://tibetanbuddhistencyclopedia.com/en/index.php?title=THE_PRACTICE_OF_DZOGCHEN_IN_THE_ZHANG%C2%B7ZHUNG_TRADITION_OF_TIBET ; Bönpo Dzogchen https://yungdrungbon.co.uk/bonpo-dzogchen-base-path-and-fruit/ ; Tögal https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T%C3%B6gal

### Chinese/Taoist — Liu Zi Jue (Six Healing Sounds) with EEG data

- **Evidence level:** moderate
- **Finding:** Earliest source: Tao Hongjing (456-536), 'On Caring for the Health of the Mind and Prolonging the Life Span': one inhalation, six exhalations CHUI, HU, XI, HE, XU, SI. Modern standardized mapping (Chinese Health Qigong Association 2003): XU (shü) → liver/wood, HE (her) → heart/fire, HU (hoo) → spleen/earth, SI (sss) → lungs/metal, CHUI (chway) → kidneys/water, XI (shee) → triple burner; Tao's original mapping differed (Shi et al. 2020). EEG (Henz & Schöllhorn 2018, n=20, 2×15 min): fronto-central theta rose continuously, alpha-1 (8-10 Hz) rose at frontal sites by 15 min, alpha-2 (10-12.5 Hz) rose parietal/occipital by 30 min, gamma rose at 30 min.
- **Usable parameters:** Mid-track vocal block: six sounds × 6 breaths each, exhale 6-8 s (sub-audible 'whispered' pitch), order XU-HE-HU-SI-CHUI-XI; ~6-8 min total; the EEG effects appeared after ≥15 min of practice.
- **Notes:** Organ correspondences are TCM doctrine (traditional); the EEG paper's abstract and results conflict on alpha timing.
- **Source:** Shi J, Wang R-J, Wang F-Y. SAGE Open 2020 https://journals.sagepub.com/doi/10.1177/2158244020927024 ; Henz D, Schöllhorn WI. Front Psychol 2018 https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5766674/ ; practice https://www.egreenway.com/qigong/sixhealingsounds.htm ; https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liu_Zi_Jue

### Chinese — Huangzhong pitch standard, the 12 lü, 64 hexagrams and the calendar

- **Evidence level:** moderate
- **Finding:** Huangzhong ('Yellow Bell') is the fundamental lü, generated from a 9-cun pipe and the 3:2 'sanfen sunyi' cycle to make 12 lü; it is matched to the 11th lunar month (winter solstice) whose sovereign hexagram is Fu (Return, one yang line returning); the 12 sovereign hexagrams (Fu, Lin, Tai, Dazhuang, Guai, Qian, Gou, Dun, Pi, Guan, Bo, Kun) track the yang/yin tide through the 12 months and were paired with the 12 lü in Han correlative cosmology (Meng Xi, Jing Fang). Absolute pitch varied by dynasty: Marquis Yi of Zeng bells (433 BCE) give the bell inscribed 'huangzhong-gong' at ≈ G#4 (~415 Hz) and the set's Guxian-gong standard at 256.4 Hz (A4 ≈ 512.9 Hz).
- **Usable parameters:** Pythagorean-chain tuning: generate pitches by successive 3:2 from 136.10 Hz (136.1 → 204.2 → 306.2/153.1 → 229.7 → 172.3 ...) to get 'lü' consonant with the drone; a 12-section structure (5 min each) mapped to 12 sovereign hexagrams rising yang (Fu→Qian) then returning (Gou→Kun) is a traditional cyclical shape.
- **Notes:** No surviving Hz for ancient huangzhong beyond bell measurements; 415 Hz is a 12-TET conversion of G#4.
- **Source:** Britannica 12 lü https://www.britannica.com/art/12-lu ; Zeng bells measurements https://zh.wikipedia.org/zh-hans/%E6%9B%BE%E4%BE%AF%E4%B9%99%E7%BC%96%E9%92%9F ; huangzhong-gong = G#4 https://zhuanlan.zhihu.com/p/30314819 ; Twelve Sovereign Hexagrams https://baike.baidu.com/en/item/Twelve%20Sovereign%20Hexagrams/1425171 ; McClain/Chinese tone ratios https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/0140175085900077

### Taoist inner alchemy — Upper dantian / Ni Wan / 'Crystal Palace' / heavenly eye

- **Evidence level:** traditional (a lineage's own teaching, not a tested claim)
- **Finding:** Classical neidan: upper dantian = Ni Wan (Muddy Pellet), the innermost of nine palaces in the head, seat of shen; the elixir is moved there in the final stage; 'turning the light around' (Secret of the Golden Flower, tr. Wilhelm 1929/Cleary 1991) directs attention/light between the eyes; 'opening the heavenly eye' (tianmu/tianyan) is a late neidan aim. The equation of Ni Wan/'Crystal Palace' with the pineal-pituitary-thalamus region is a 20th-century gloss (Mantak Chia, Universal Tao; darkness-retreat teaching claiming pineal DMT/melatonin), not in the classical texts.
- **Usable parameters:** Guided cue language: 'gather the light at the point between the eyes, let it sink to the Ni Wan at the centre of the head, then circulate (microcosmic orbit) down the front, up the spine' — orbit cue cycle ≈ 1 breath (8-10 s); no specific pitch is prescribed in these texts.
- **Notes:** The pineal is light-sensitive (via retina-SCN pathway), not sound-sensitive; nothing in Taoist sources assigns a tone to it.
- **Source:** Golden Elixir, Cinnabar Fields https://www.goldenelixir.com/jindan/dantian.html ; Mantak Chia darkness retreat https://www.universal-tao.com/article/ancient.html ; Damo Mitchell on the pineal https://www.thedaobums.com/topic/49917-excerpt-from-the-pineal-gland-by-damo-mitchell/ ; Wilhelm/Jung, The Secret of the Golden Flower (1929)

### Japanese — Shōmyō chant and Shingon Ajikan (A-syllable) meditation

- **Evidence level:** moderate
- **Finding:** Shōmyō (Shingon/Kōyasan and Tendai/Ōhara schools) is monophonic ritual chant on the Yo pentatonic scale (intervals 2-3-2-2-3 semitones), with very long single tones, slow slides (yuri), and ensemble texture from overtone/vowel differences; ceremonies run 60-90 min. Ajikan: gaze at the Siddham 'A' in a white moon disk on a lotus; breathe the sound 'Ah' with every exhalation; expand the moon disk to fill the universe, then contract it into the heart; attributed to Kūkai (774-835), standardized by Jitsue; the 'A' is the unborn (ādi-anutpāda) and Mahāvairocana's seed. Susokukan (breath counting) is taught first.
- **Usable parameters:** Yo-scale drone cluster on C#: C# D# F# G# A# (136.1 / 153.1 / 181.5 / 204.2 / 229.7 Hz, 12-TET); 'Ah' vowel sustained 10-20 s per exhale; moon-disk expansion cue over ~3 min, contraction ~2 min.
- **Notes:** No Hz values are prescribed; pentatonic set is documented.
- **Source:** Monhart M., 'A Fusion of Horizons: Shōmyō in Kogi Shingon Ritual' http://www.shin-ibs.edu/documents/SCEAR%207/SCEAR7aMonhart.pdf ; https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sh%C5%8Dmy%C5%8D ; Ajikan analysis https://www.themathesontrust.org/papers/buddhism/Shingon_Ajikan_Meditation.pdf ; Kōyasan temple https://nckoyasan.org/shingon-teachings/

### Japanese — Bonshō temple bell acoustics

- **Evidence level:** moderate
- **Finding:** Measured Hōjōbō temple bell: lowest (4-0) mode 137.8 Hz, then 301.8 and 378.6 Hz — inharmonic, with slow decay and a slow beat (several Hz) between split modes; typical large bonshō hum 130-150 Hz; struck from outside with a wooden shumoku; 108 strikes at New Year (Joya no Kane) to dispel the 108 defilements; the lingering sound (yoin) is itself the object of contemplation.
- **Usable parameters:** Transition marker: bonshō-type strike with partials 137.8 / 301.8 / 378.6 Hz (nearly C#3 — fits the 136.1 drone), decay 30-60 s, 2-4 Hz amplitude beat; strike at each section boundary.
- **Notes:** The 137.8 Hz coincidence with 136.1 Hz is useful for design, not meaningful.
- **Source:** 'Influences of the shapes of a temple bell's parts on acoustic characteristics', Acoust. Sci. & Tech. 25(5) 2004 https://www.jstage.jst.go.jp/article/ast/25/5/25_5_340/_pdf/-char/ja ; physical model https://www.researchgate.net/publication/370440266 ; bell culture https://bethreiber.com/if-japanese-buddhist-temple-bells-could-talk/

### Japanese Shinto — Norito and kotodama

- **Evidence level:** traditional (a lineage's own teaching, not a tested claim)
- **Finding:** Norito are formal prayers in archaic Japanese recited by priests; efficacy rests on kotodama, the spirit-power held to reside in correctly pronounced words — beautiful/correct words bring good, coarse words harm; texts were transcribed in special script to fix pronunciation; the intoned delivery (slow, falling cadence) matters as much as the text.
- **Usable parameters:** Spoken-word delivery guideline: slow monotone recitation with long falling cadences; purification (harae) section at the very start of the track.
- **Notes:** No pitch or tempo specifications exist beyond performance custom.
- **Source:** Britannica 'norito' https://www.britannica.com/topic/norito ; Green Shinto https://www.greenshinto.com/2014/04/16/word-power-kotodama/ ; kotodama paper https://www.shs-conferences.org/articles/shsconf/pdf/2022/04/shsconf_eac-law2021_00157.pdf

### Korean and Vietnamese Buddhist chant

- **Evidence level:** moderate
- **Finding:** Korea: beompae (범패) has hossori (short/simple) and jissori (long, extremely slow melismatic) styles, paced by the moktak wooden fish; Yeongsanjae (Lotus Sutra re-enactment, UNESCO 2009) combines chant, cymbal dance (barachum) and butterfly dance. Vietnam (Huế tradition): three vocal modes — tụng (sutra cantillation on equal beats of the mõ wood-block, sometimes 2-4 syllables/s), tán (hymns of praise, melismatic), xướng/thỉnh (responsorial invitations); services opened by the great drum (đại cổ) and great bell (đại hồng chung); styles related to Nguyen court music (1802-1945).
- **Usable parameters:** Wood-block pulse under recitation: steady 120-180 BPM (2-3 Hz) mõ/moktak clicks (an isochronous pulse in the delta/low-theta range if used as entrainment); jissori-style: one syllable stretched over 20-30 s.
- **Notes:** No Hz data for these instruments were found.
- **Source:** Beompae https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beompae ; Yeongsanjae https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yeongsanjae ; Powell, Buddhist Music of Vietnam http://www.personal.utulsa.edu/~john-powell/Buddhist_Music/Vietnam.html ; Huế chant program note https://artsandculture.google.com/asset/chants-et-musiques-bouddhiques-de-hu%C3%AA/sQEND2bXjMpBmg

### Sufi — Mevlevi semā (whirling) and the ney

- **Evidence level:** moderate
- **Finding:** The Mevlevi ayin: Nat-i Şerif (eulogy), kudüm drum strokes, ney taksim (improvised solo on the reed flute = Rumi's 'listen to the reed... cut from the reed-bed', Masnavi I:1-18), Sultan Veled circling (3 rounds), then four selams of turning (each ~15 min with brief pauses; the sheikh turns in the 4th), total 45-90 min. Rotation is anticlockwise on the left foot, right foot driving; right palm up (receiving), left down (giving); speed follows the music and accelerates in the second selam. Purpose stated by UNESCO and the order: the soul's journey to perfection and union (fana). No measured RPM exists; the 2017 Frontiers study found trained whirlers spin ~1 h without vertigo (cross-sectional, small n).
- **Usable parameters:** Session arc: 4 'selams' of ~10-15 min, each a plateau with a brief rest; ney-style breathy flute solo (ney in A/'Mansur' ≈ 440 Hz-based or 'Kız' in B) over the drone; kudüm pulse ~60-70 BPM rising to ~80 in selam 2; Devr-i Kebir and other usul cycles are long (28 beats).
- **Notes:** RPM figures do not exist in the literature; 'accelerates' is descriptive.
- **Source:** UNESCO https://en.unesco.org/silkroad/silk-road-themes/intangible-cultural-heritage/mevlevi-sema-ceremony ; https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sufi_whirling ; Frontiers Hum Neurosci 2017 https://www.frontiersin.org/journals/human-neuroscience/articles/10.3389/fnhum.2017.00003/full ; Masnavi reed song https://www.dar-al-masnavi.org/reedsong.html

### Sufi — Dhikr breath cycles (Naqshbandi, Qadiri/Rifai hadra, Chishti samā)

- **Evidence level:** traditional (a lineage's own teaching, not a tested claim)
- **Finding:** Naqshbandi principle 1 hosh dar dam: awareness on every breath; silent dhikr of 'La ilaha illallah' done with eyes/mouth closed, tongue on palate, breath held, 'La' drawn from below the navel up to the brain, 'illallah' struck into the heart; daily counts of 5,000-10,000; pairing 'Allah' on inhale / 'Hu' on exhale. Loud hadra (Qadiri, Rifai, Shadhili): ostinato repetition of 'Allah'/'Hu'/'Hayy' with bending forward on the exhale and straightening on the inhale, tempo accelerating toward trance. Chishti samā/qawwali: listening to sung poetry with handclaps and dholak, instruments originally disallowed, aim = wajd (ecstasy) and remembrance; 'Hu' regarded as the most sacred sound (Inayat Khan).
- **Usable parameters:** Breath-linked chant: inhale 'Al-lah' 4 s, exhale 'Hu' 6-8 s; hadra-style block: 'Hu' on each exhale at 60 BPM rising to 100-120 BPM over 6-8 min (exhale-bend / inhale-straighten cue); keep the held-breath 'negation-affirmation' as an optional advanced cue only.
- **Notes:** Tempo acceleration is customary, not quantified in sources.
- **Source:** Eleven Naqshbandi principles https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eleven_Naqshbandi_principles ; silent dhikr method https://nurmuhammad.com/wp-content/uploads/MindBodySoulSufiMeditation_mobile.pdf ; Hadra https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ha%E1%B8%8Dra ; Chishti samā https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chishti_Order ; Qawwali https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qawwali

### Sufi — Hazrat Inayat Khan's doctrine of vibration (The Mysticism of Sound, 1923)

- **Evidence level:** none (no evidence)
- **Finding:** Inayat Khan taught that the universe is vibration; the 'abstract sound' Saut-e Sarmad pervades all space and is heard in meditation as (in ascending subtlety) bells, running water, buzzing bees, bird song, the flute, the conch and finally 'Hu'; 'whoever understands the mystery of sound understands the mystery of the whole universe'; those who meditate on Saut-e Sarmad are 'freed from worries, fears and diseases'. Hu is the origin and end of all sound and the name of God behind all names. These are devotional assertions, not tested claims.
- **Usable parameters:** 'Hu' drone chant: long breathy 'Huuu' on the exhale, 8-12 s, pitched at 136/272 Hz; his listed sound sequence (bell→water→bee→bird→flute→conch→Hu) can be a second timbre arc paralleling the Hindu nada list.
- **Notes:** Inayat Khan was a trained Indian classical musician; his sound list is a Sufi restatement of nada yoga.
- **Source:** Hazrat Inayat Khan, The Mysticism of Sound and Music (Sufi Message Vol. II, 1923/1991) https://breath4balance.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/10/mysticism_sound_music.pdf ; study database http://www.hazrat-inayat-khan.org/php/views.php?h1=11&h2=8

### Islamic esoteric — Hurufi letter mysticism

- **Evidence level:** traditional (a lineage's own teaching, not a tested claim)
- **Finding:** Fazlallah Astarabadi (1340-1394, Jāwidān-nāma) taught that the Divine Word is the 28 Arabic / 32 Persian letters, that God is manifest in them, and that the human face shows the 28/32 letters completely (hairlines, brows, lashes etc. counted as letters) — the human being is the Qur'an made flesh; the movement survived in Bektashi Sufism. No sound practice parameters are specified beyond recitation.
- **Usable parameters:** Conceptual only: a 28- or 32-step letter-sequence (one per breath) could structure a recitation section; no pitch/tempo prescribed.
- **Notes:** Included for completeness; no usable acoustic parameters in the tradition.
- **Source:** https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurufism ; https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fazlallah_Astarabadi ; Usluer, 'Hurufism among Albanian Bektashis' https://www.sosyalarastirmalar.com/articles/hurufism-among-albanian-bektashis.pdf

### Jewish Kabbalah — Abraham Abulafia's letter-permutation (tzeruf) with breath and head movements

- **Evidence level:** traditional (a lineage's own teaching, not a tested claim)
- **Finding:** In Hayyei ha-Olam ha-Ba (c. 1280) and Or ha-Sekhel, Abulafia prescribes, in a quiet room at night wrapped in tallit and tefillin, permuting the letters of the 72-letter Name with the five vowels; each letter is pronounced on ONE full breath (inhale, pronounce on exhale, pause before the next), and the head moves with the vowel: holam (o) — head tilts up to the sky; qamatz (a) — head traced left to right; tzere (e) — right to left; hiriq (i) — downward; shuruq (u) — forward/straight. Scholem and Idel document the expected signs (trembling, warmth, inner light, hearing a voice) and the goal: prophecy (nevuah) and the 'untying of the knots' of the soul.
- **Usable parameters:** One letter-vowel per breath (≈ 8-12 s cycles); Hebrew vowel series o-a-e-i-u as a five-vowel chant cycle with head-movement cues; done at night, in darkness; Abulafia's own count is the 72 triplets × 5 vowels.
- **Notes:** Head-movement mapping varies between Abulafia's manuals; Idel notes parallels to yogic/hesychast breath but no proven borrowing.
- **Source:** Moshe Idel, The Mystical Experience in Abraham Abulafia (SUNY 1988) https://www.academia.edu/1823911/ ; Idel, Language, Torah and Hermeneutics in Abraham Abulafia (SUNY 1989); Scholem, Major Trends in Jewish Mysticism (1941) ch. 4; overview https://itsfilipholm.substack.com/p/abraham-abulafia-and-ecstatic-kabbalah ; https://satyori.com/kabbalah/practices/tzeruf-ha-otiyot/

### Jewish Kabbalah — Sefer Yetzirah letters as vibrational forces and the 72 Names

- **Evidence level:** traditional (a lineage's own teaching, not a tested claim)
- **Finding:** Sefer Yetzirah (3rd-6th c. CE) says God 'engraved, carved, weighed, permuted and combined' 22 letters to create: 3 mothers Aleph-Mem-Shin (air, water, fire), 7 doubles B-G-D-K-P-R-T (the 7 planets, 7 days, 7 gates of the head), 12 simples (12 zodiac signs, months, organs). The 72 Names (Shem ha-Mephorash) are 72 three-letter triplets read boustrophedon from Exodus 14:19-21 (3 verses × 72 letters = 216 letters); Abulafia used them for prophecy; practical kabbalists for amulets; the Zohar ties the three verses to Chesed, Gevurah, Tiferet.
- **Usable parameters:** Three-mother vowel/consonant breaths: 'A' (open, air), 'M' (hum, water), 'Sh' (unvoiced hiss, fire) as a 3-element breath cycle; seven 'double' letters mapped to seven planetary tones if the Cousto set is used (Saturn 147.85, Jupiter 183.58, Mars 144.72, Sun 126.22, Venus 221.23, Mercury 141.27, Moon 210.42 Hz).
- **Notes:** Planet-to-double-letter assignment differs among manuscripts (Kaplan tabulates several).
- **Source:** Sefer Yetzirah tr. Westcott https://sacred-texts.com/jud/yetzirah.htm ; Aryeh Kaplan, Sefer Yetzirah: The Book of Creation (Weiser 1990); https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sefer_Yetzirah ; 72 Names https://www.emol.org/kabbalah/secretcircles/72names.html ; https://www.hebrew4christians.com/Names_of_G-d/Esoteric/esoteric.html

### Jewish — Merkabah/Hekhalot mysticism ('descent to the Chariot') and the shofar

- **Evidence level:** traditional (a lineage's own teaching, not a tested claim)
- **Finding:** Hekhalot Rabbati (c. 3rd-7th c.) describes the yored merkavah ascending/descending through seven palaces guarded by angels, showing seals (divine names) at each gate, reciting hymns (the Qedushah 'Holy, holy, holy' sung with the angels) — the posture (per Hai Gaon, 11th c.) is fasting, head between the knees, whispering hymns; the 6th palace is lethal to the unprepared. The shofar (ram's horn, Rosh Hashanah): tekiah (long), shevarim (3 broken), teruah (9 staccato), 100 blasts in all; Maimonides (Hilchot Teshuvah 3:4): though a scriptural decree, it hints 'Awake, sleepers, from your sleep... examine your deeds, return'; it also recalls Sinai, where the shofar sounded before revelation.
- **Usable parameters:** Seven-stage ascent structure (7 'palaces' × ~5-6 min), each gated by a hymn/'seal' cue and a 'Holy, holy, holy' triple chant; shofar-type horn call (natural horn, fundamental ~ 300-500 Hz with octave/fifth harmonics) as the 'awaken' opener: tekiah 3 s, shevarim 3×1 s, teruah 9×0.3 s, tekiah gedolah 6-10 s.
- **Notes:** Hekhalot practice was for the circle of adepts; not a listening practice.
- **Source:** Hekhalot Rabbati https://en.zoharonline.org/Heikhalot-Rabbati ; Blumenthal, 'Secrets of Hekhalot Rabbati' https://www.scribd.com/document/367978966/ ; Gruenwald, Apocalyptic and Merkavah Mysticism (Brill 1980); Maimonides Hilchot Teshuvah 3:4 https://prod.sefaria.org.il/sheets/15039 ; shofar blowing https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shofar_blowing

### Christian — Alfred Tomatis, Gregorian chant and 'charging' high frequencies

- **Evidence level:** anecdotal
- **Finding:** Tomatis (ENT, 1920-2001) claimed in a 1978 documentary that after a Benedictine abbey (unnamed, post-Vatican II, 1960s) stopped its 6-8 h/day of chant the monks became exhausted and depressed, and recovered when chant resumed; his theory: sounds rich in 3,000-8,000 Hz 'charge' the cortex via the ear (he called them 'charging sounds', low frequencies 'discharging'), delivered with his filtered 'Electronic Ear'. No name, date or record of the abbey, no controlled study, and the frequency band varies (2-8 kHz) across his own accounts.
- **Usable parameters:** If used: add a high-frequency 'air' layer (chant harmonics / filtered choir, 3-8 kHz shelf +3-6 dB) in the 'illumination' section; keep total SPL moderate.
- **Notes:** Treat as a story that motivates a mixing choice, not as evidence.
- **Source:** Tomatis, The Conscious Ear (1991); story summary https://susanbensen.com/keep-coming-back-story-monks/ ; forum trace to 1978 'Chant' documentary https://forum.musicasacra.com/forum/discussion/16770/ ; Spirit of Change on Tomatis harmonics https://www.spiritofchange.org/harmonics-and-listening-as-transformation/

### Christian — Byzantine ison drone, Hildegard of Bingen, Gregorian modes

- **Evidence level:** moderate
- **Finding:** Byzantine chant: a sustained ison (isokratima) drone on the mode's base note under the melody; positively datable only to the 16th c.; theology reads it as the unchanging divine ground / the Logos over 'the waters'; it moves only at structural points. Hildegard of Bingen (1098-1179, Symphonia armonie celestium revelationum): chants with an ambitus of 2 to 2.5 octaves (Newman), leaps of a fifth, built on long melismas; she framed music as recovering the 'symphonia' of paradise and the Holy Spirit's viriditas (greening power); 'O vis aeternitatis' is her programmatic opener. Gregorian chant itself is unmetered, syllabic-to-melismatic, 8 church modes, ambitus usually ≤ an octave.
- **Usable parameters:** Keep an unbroken vocal ison on C#3 (138.6) / C#2 (69.3) under all melodic material; Hildegard-style soaring female line spanning C#4-C#6 for the 'illumination' passage; modal (Dorian/Phrygian-on-C#) melodies, free rhythm, phrases 10-20 s.
- **Notes:** No Hz standards existed in medieval chant; pitch was relative.
- **Source:** Ison https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ison_(music) ; symbolism https://orthodox.de/en/heiligergesang/symbolik-des-gesangs-ueber-ison/ ; Barbara Newman (ed.), Symphonia (Cornell 2nd ed. 1998); Hildegard Society https://www.hildegard-society.org/p/music.html ; 'Hooked on Ecstasy' https://www.academia.edu/228180/

### Christian — Hesychast Jesus Prayer breath cycle

- **Evidence level:** traditional (a lineage's own teaching, not a tested claim)
- **Finding:** 'Lord Jesus Christ, Son of God, have mercy on me (a sinner)': Nicephorus the Solitary (13th c.) taught collecting the mind into the heart with the breath, slowing respiration BEFORE the prayer; Ps.-Symeon adds chin on chest, gaze toward the navel; Kallistos & Ignatios Xanthopoulos (late 14th c.) first explicitly tie the words to the breath; modern Athonite practice: first half on the inhale, second half on the exhale, with a slight breath-suspension between (Ware). Goal: the uncreated light (Palamas) and ceaseless prayer — not powers.
- **Usable parameters:** Breath-prayer pacing: inhale 4-5 s ('Lord Jesus Christ, Son of God'), brief pause 1 s, exhale 5-6 s ('have mercy on me') ≈ 5-6 breaths/min — the HRV-resonance band; repeat 10-15 min.
- **Notes:** The 6/min rate coincides with the physiological resonance frequency (0.1 Hz) found in HRV-biofeedback research.
- **Source:** Kallistos Ware, '14th-c. Hesychasts' https://www.ldysinger.com/@texts2/1980_kal-ware/06_hesyc-14c.htm ; Philokalia vol. 4 (Faber); Nicephorus text http://www.fryuhanna.com/2017/07/14/nicephorus-the-solitary-on-the-jesus-prayer/ ; https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hesychasm

### Greek — Pythagoras, Plato's Timaeus, Boethius, Kepler: the 'harmony of the spheres' ratios

- **Evidence level:** **strong**
- **Finding:** Pythagorean consonances from the monochord: 2:1 octave, 3:2 fifth, 4:3 fourth, 9:8 tone, 256:243 leimma (the hammer legend of Nicomachus is false physics but the ratios are real); the tetractys 1+2+3+4=10 encodes them. Plato Timaeus 35b-36b: the Demiurge divides the World-Soul into portions 1, 2, 3, 4, 9, 8, 27, fills the double/triple intervals with harmonic and arithmetic means (giving 4:3, 3:2, 9:8) and leaves 256:243 — the Pythagorean diatonic scale as the soul of the cosmos. Boethius De institutione musica I.2 (c. 500 CE): musica mundana (cosmos, inaudible), humana (soul-body), instrumentalis (sounding). Kepler Harmonices Mundi V (1619): angular-speed ratios at perihelion/aphelion — Saturn 4:5 (maj 3rd), Jupiter 5:6 (min 3rd), Mars 2:3 (fifth), Earth 15:16 (semitone, 'mi-fa, misery-famine'), Venus 24:25 (diesis), Mercury 5:12 (octave + minor third; some tables give 1:4).
- **Usable parameters:** Tune all intervals in the track by pure ratios from 136.10 Hz: 181.47 (4:3), 204.15 (3:2), 153.11 (9:8), 272.2 (2:1), 170.1 (5:4), 163.3 (6:5); 'planetary' glides: Saturn 136.1→170.1 (4:5), Jupiter 136.1→163.3 (5:6), Mars 136.1→204.2 (2:3), Earth 136.1→145.2 (15:16), Venus 136.1→141.8 (24:25), Mercury 136.1→326.6 (5:12).
- **Notes:** Strong as history of ideas; no evidence any of it affects listeners beyond consonance preference.
- **Source:** Pythagorean hammers https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pythagorean_hammers ; Timaeus (Jowett) https://hti.osu.edu/sites/hti.osu.edu/files/platotimaeus.pdf ; Proclus commentary (Baltzly) https://philpapers.org/archive/BALPCO ; Boethius SEP https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/hist-westphilmusic-to-1800/ ; Kepler https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harmonice_Mundi ; Kepler music theory https://microcosmos.uchicago.edu/microcosmos_new/kepler/harmonicesmundi/music.html

### Greco-Egyptian / Gnostic / Hermetic — seven-vowel chant for the seven planets

- **Evidence level:** moderate
- **Finding:** Demetrius, On Style §71 (1st c. BCE-1st c. CE): 'in Egypt the priests hymn the gods through the seven vowels, sounding them in succession, and the sound is heard instead of aulos and kithara'. The Greek Magical Papyri (PGM XIII 'Eighth Book of Moses', PGM IV 'Mithras Liturgy') use the vowels Α Ε Η Ι Ο Υ Ω, often in 7×7 wing/pyramid patterns and with instructions to face directions and draw in breath; late-antique sources map vowel→planet (Α Moon, Ε Mercury, Η Venus, Ι Sun, Ο Mars, Υ Jupiter, Ω Saturn — order varies). Nag Hammadi 'Discourse on the Eighth and Ninth' (NHC VI,6, 2nd-3rd c.) has Hermes and the initiate chant long vowel strings and 'IAO' to rise past the seven spheres to the Ogdoad and Ennead — the classic textual model of 'travel between realms' by sound.
- **Usable parameters:** Seven-vowel ladder: A-E-Ē-I-O-U-Ō each sustained 6-8 s, ascending one tone per vowel (e.g. 136.1, 153.1, 170.1, 181.5, 204.2, 229.7, 255.2 Hz); 7 rounds ('seven heavens'); then 'IAŌ' as the capstone; mapped to Cousto planetary tones if the planetary layer is used.
- **Notes:** Historically real practice; 'ascent' is ritual/visionary framing.
- **Source:** Demetrius On Style (Rhys Roberts 1903) https://www.attalus.org/old/demetr2.html ; Betz (ed.), The Greek Magical Papyri in Translation (1986); Discourse on the Eighth and Ninth (Meyer) http://gnosis.org/naghamm/discourse-meyer.html ; 'Monkey Business' https://www.academia.edu/34852327/ ; Cambridge ch. 'Vowel Constellations' https://www.cambridge.org/core/books/nag-hammadi-codices-and-their-ancient-readers/vowel-constellations-and-secret-language/BEE3737EA9753390531B581901AAE65E ; Mithras Liturgy https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mithras_Liturgy

### Hermeticism (modern) — The Kybalion's Principle of Vibration

- **Evidence level:** none (no evidence)
- **Finding:** The Kybalion (1908, 'Three Initiates', almost certainly William Walker Atkinson) states 'Nothing rests; everything moves; everything vibrates' and that 'Mental Transmutation' — changing one's mental vibration by will/attention — is 'the Art of Polarization'; this 1908 New Thought text, not ancient Hermetica, is the direct source of modern 'raise your vibration / law of attraction' language.
- **Usable parameters:** Only scripting language ('Will directs attention; attention changes the vibration'); no acoustic parameters.
- **Notes:** Important for honesty: the 'vibration/frequency/manifestation' vocabulary is 20th-century New Thought.
- **Source:** The Kybalion ch. IX 'Vibration' https://sacred-texts.com/eso/kyb/kyb11.htm ; full text https://selfdefinition.org/new-thought/The-Kybalion-by-Three-Initiates.pdf

### Greco-Egyptian — Orphic Hymns and the Hathor/Isis sistrum

- **Evidence level:** traditional (a lineage's own teaching, not a tested claim)
- **Finding:** The 87 Orphic Hymns (2nd-3rd c. CE, Asia Minor) are short invocations sung at night by initiates, each headed with its incense (frankincense, myrrh, storax ×13, aromatic herbs; Hymn 87 'To Death' prescribes powdered frankincense). Sistrum (Egyptian sesheshet, sacred to Hathor then Isis): Plutarch, Isis and Osiris 63 — 'all things must be shaken and never cease from motion'; the sistrum's rattle 'averts and repels Typhon' (Seth, decay); its arch holds the four shaken elements, the cat on top the Moon; rattling the sistrum is the Egyptian cult's sonic emblem of vibration overcoming stagnation.
- **Usable parameters:** Sistrum-type bronze rattle at 4-6 shakes/s (theta-range pulse) in the 'activation' sections; Orphic-style night-invocation delivery (short hymn, 10-20 lines, intoned on 2-3 pitches).
- **Notes:** No pitch/tempo prescriptions survive for either; parameters are modern performance choices.
- **Source:** Athanassakis & Wolkow, The Orphic Hymns (Johns Hopkins 2013); hymn list https://sartrix.miraheze.org/wiki/Orphic_Hymns ; Plutarch Isis & Osiris (Loeb, Babbitt) §63 https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Plutarch's_Moralia_(Loeb)/Isis_and_Osiris ; https://topostext.org/work/274

### Zoroastrian — Ahuna Vairya (Yatha Ahu Vairyo) manthra

- **Evidence level:** traditional (a lineage's own teaching, not a tested claim)
- **Finding:** Yasna 27.13, the most sacred Gathic manthra, 21 words (Yasna 19 says the 21 words correspond to the 21 nasks of the Avesta); recited by Zarathustra to repel Angra Mainyu (Vendidad 19), prescribed as purification and protection, recited multiple times in every ritual; the word 'manthra' (= Sanskrit mantra) is the Avestan term for sacred formula, and the Yasna liturgy is chanted in a monotone with prolonged final vowels by mobeds.
- **Usable parameters:** 21-word recitation ≈ 15-20 s; repeat 3× (opening) / 4× / 10× as in ritual counts; near-monotone intonation (reciting tone + lower cadence note).
- **Notes:** The 21-word count is standard in Zoroastrian sources; not independently verified here.
- **Source:** https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ahuna_Vairya ; http://www.zoroaster.com/avesta/Ahuanavar.htm

### Sikh — Naam Simran, Anhad Shabad and the Dasam Duar

- **Evidence level:** traditional (a lineage's own teaching, not a tested claim)
- **Finding:** Naam Simran = sustained repetition of 'Waheguru' (or the Mul Mantar), ideally with every breath and in the ambrosial hours (amrit vela, ~3-6 a.m.); Gurbani (Guru Granth Sahib) repeatedly describes the anhad shabad (unstruck sound/'five-instrument' celestial music) heard when the dasam duar (tenth gate, crown) opens, with amrit (nectar) trickling — Bhai Randhir Singh's Anhad Shabad Dasam Duar gives these as the two signs; the Sikh tradition insists this is God's kirtan, not the yogic nada of instruments.
- **Usable parameters:** 'Wa-he-gu-ru' as a four-syllable breath mantra: 'Wahe' on inhale, 'Guru' on exhale, 4-6 s cycle, 10-30 min; kirtan-style harmonium drone on C#/G# behind it.
- **Notes:** Sikh theology rejects 'powers' as the aim; the sound is grace, not technique.
- **Source:** Bhai Randhir Singh, Anhad Shabad Dasam Duar (English) https://www.akj.org/Media/Books/AnhadShabdDasamDuar_Engish.pdf ; Naam https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N%C4%81ma ; https://www.basicsofsikhi.com/post/why-chant-vaheguru-mantra

### Jain and Bahá'í mantra counts

- **Evidence level:** traditional (a lineage's own teaching, not a tested claim)
- **Finding:** Jain Navkar (Namokar) Mantra: nine lines, 68 letters, salutation to the five supreme beings (Arihant, Siddha, Acharya, Upadhyaya, Sadhu) — the oldest continuously used Jain mantra, recited 108× on a mala, the first five lines in Prakrit; purpose: destruction of karma, auspiciousness (no deity invoked). Bahá'í: Kitáb-i-Aqdas ¶18 — each believer repeats 'Alláh-u-Abhá' (God is Most Glorious) 95 times daily after ablutions, seated facing the Qiblih; 95 = numerical value of 'lillah' (for God).
- **Usable parameters:** Repetition-count structures usable for the track: 95 × 'Allah-u-Abha' at ~3 s each ≈ 5 min; 108 × Navkar at ~15 s each ≈ 27 min (or 9 lines once ≈ 30 s).
- **Notes:** Both traditions treat the practice as devotion, not frequency work.
- **Source:** https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Namokar_Mantra ; https://jainworld.com/education/jain-education-material/beginner-level/namokar-mantra/ ; Kitáb-i-Aqdas https://reference.bahai.org/en/t/b/KA/ka-50.html ; Bahaipedia https://bahaipedia.org/All%C3%A1h-u-Abh%C3%A1

### Design recommendations from this lens

- Tonal centre: build the whole track on 136.10 Hz (C#, Cousto 'Earth-year'/'Om') with sub-octaves 68.05 and 34.03 Hz and a vocal ison at 138.6/69.3 Hz; it is the one pitch that most practitioner traditions (Cousto forks, planet gongs, 'Om tuning' tanpuras) share, and the bonshō (137.8 Hz) and Gyuto sub-fundamentals (61.7-68 Hz) sit near it. State honestly in the guide that the choice is conventional, not ancient.
- Use pure-ratio intervals from that centre (3:2 = 204.15, 4:3 = 181.47, 9:8 = 153.11, 5:4 = 170.13, 2:1 = 272.2 Hz) so Pythagorean/Timaeus/Kepler material, the Chinese 3:2 lü chain, and shōmyō pentatonics all stay consonant; avoid the Solfeggio set, which clashes with everything else.
- Session architecture (45-60 min): fuse Yoga Nidra's 8 stages with the Mevlevi four-selam plateau shape and the Hekhalot seven-gate ascent: (1) harae/shofar-call awakening 0-3 min; (2) sankalpa/intention 3-5; (3) 61-point body rotation 5-16 with 4-s breath (So'ham 15/min); (4) breath-prayer slowdown to 6/min (Jesus Prayer / Allah-Hu split) 16-26; (5) Six Healing Sounds or Bhramari vocal block 26-33; (6) seven-vowel/seven-planet ascent (Greek vowels on Cousto or ratio tones) 33-40; (7) Bardo 'thousand thunders' sound-light-rays climax with recognition cue 40-44; (8) return to clear-light drone, anhad/nada subtle sounds (bell→flute→silence) 44-52; (9) sankalpa repeated, externalization 52-56; (10) 108-second silence tail.
- Carry the beat frequency acoustically as well as binaurally: layer singing-bowl-style near-degenerate mode pairs split 3-8 Hz (e.g. 136.1/142.5 Hz; 272.2/278.9 Hz) and a damaru/sistrum pulse at 4-6 Hz, so the theta-range modulation survives speaker playback and matches the one EEG result on bowls (6.68 Hz beat → delta/theta rise over 5 min).
- Timbre arc from the Hamsa Upanishad / Inayat Khan nada lists: tinkling → bell → conch → tanpura/vina → cymbal (tingsha) → flute (ney/shakuhachi) → kettledrum → mridanga → thunder, then reverse to subtle; pan inner-sound cues slightly right (HYP right-ear instruction) and let plucked tones decay fully into silence (Vijnana Bhairava v.41).
- Breath pacing is where the real, measurable effects live: 15/min (4 s) early → 6/min (10 s) for the middle 20 minutes (Hesychast/dhikr split: ~4-5 s in, 5-6 s out) → optional short vase-breath holds (5 in / 10-15 hold / 8 out) → Bhramari hums (10-15 s exhale). Give the listener audible cues to hum/chant OM and the bija sequence themselves, because the fMRI/NO/EEG findings are for self-produced sound, not listening.
- Vocal material: a Gyuto-style low drone (61.7 or 68 Hz with boosted 5th and 10th harmonics), a continuous Byzantine ison, A-OM-HUM three-point chant (272/204/136 Hz), LAM-VAM-RAM-YAM-HAM-OM ascending ladder, a seven-vowel ladder (A E Ē I O U Ō), and a Hildegard-style soaring line (C#4-C#6) for the 'illumination' passage; keep all chant in modes on C# (Dorian/Phrygian/Yo pentatonic).
- Mark section boundaries with a bonshō-type strike (137.8 / 301.8 / 378.6 Hz partials, 30-60 s decay) and a tingsha pair; use a dungchen-like 58-68 Hz swell under the deep section; keep the Bardo climax ≤ 80 dB SPL with a calm spoken recognition cue immediately before and after.
- Add a 3-8 kHz 'air' layer (filtered choir harmonics, à la Tomatis) only in the illumination section, +3-6 dB shelf, and label it in the guide as Tomatis's untested hypothesis.
- Guide text: present ajna/pineal, Ni Wan/Crystal Palace, dasam duar, Ogdoad and bardo language as the traditions' own frames; describe sankalpa (Yoga Nidra) as the historical 'manifestation' technique; attribute the vibration/frequency vocabulary to the Kybalion (1908) and Cousto (1978); mark every Hz table as modern (Goldman 1980s-90s, Solfeggio 1999, Cousto 1978).

### Cautions from this lens

- No tradition examined assigns a frequency to the pineal gland, and no study shows sound opening or stimulating it; the pineal responds to light via the retina-SCN pathway, not to acoustic vibration. The ajna→pineal identification is 20th-century (Satyananda 1984, Yogananda, Mantak Chia).
- All chakra-Hz tables are modern inventions: the 256-480 Hz just-intonation set (Goldman), the Solfeggio set (Puleo/Horowitz 1999, numerology from Numbers 7), and Cousto's planetary assignments (1978, with chakra pairings added by vendors). None has scriptural or experimental support.
- '136.1 Hz = Om' is Cousto's octave calculation from the sidereal year; Indian Sa is not fixed and ancient texts give no Hz. The 'Sadja = exactly 136.10 Hz' claim has no primary source.
- The human evidence is small and modest: OM fMRI n=12; singing-bowl EEG n=17 with one bowl; Liu Zi Jue EEG n=20; Bhramari/humming NO n=10; tummo n≈10. Effects are relaxation-range (theta/alpha, parasympathetic shift), not 'inhuman levels'; binaural-beat literature generally shows small, inconsistent effects. Nothing supports immediate or guaranteed results, travel between dimensions/times, or control of external 'energy'.
- Tomatis's Benedictine-monastery story is unverifiable (no abbey named, no record, inconsistent frequency bands 2-8 kHz); treat as anecdote.
- Safety: keep peak SPL under ~80-85 dB, especially for the low-frequency thunder climax; avoid rhythmic pulses in the 3-30 Hz range for listeners with photosensitive/audiogenic epilepsy without a warning; no breath-holds (vase breathing, Naqshbandi held-breath dhikr) for pregnancy, cardiovascular or respiratory conditions; the Bardo/Chöd imagery of death, demons and 'thousand thunders' can distress listeners with trauma, psychosis history or panic — give an opt-out cue and never present the climax as literal contact with other beings.
- Cultural/sacred-use issues: Gyuto dbyangs is deity-yoga voice not meant for entertainment, the 72 Names and Shofar are sacred within Jewish practice, Chöd requires empowerment in its tradition, and Sikh teaching explicitly rejects pursuing powers; sample or imitate styles rather than lifting liturgical recordings, and credit traditions in the guide.
- Inharmonic bowls/bells will beat unpleasantly against a sine drone unless their fundamentals are chosen near C#/G#/F#; pick samples (or synthesize modes) within ±10 Hz of 136/204/272 Hz for the main drone, and keep deliberately detuned pairs separate from the tonal bed.
- The Gyuto fundamental (60-75 Hz), dungchen 58 Hz and tingsha '>2 kHz' are from secondary summaries and vendors, not from the primary acoustic papers (which are paywalled or withdrawn); Mevlevi rotation speed has never been measured in RPM; huangzhong's absolute pitch is reconstructed only from the Zeng bells (≈415 Hz).

### Skeptic fact-check of this lens

*Overall reliability:* Moderate-to-good. The history-of-ideas material (Vedic, Kashmir Shaiva, Tibetan, Taoist, Shingon, Sufi, Kabbalistic, Hesychast, Pythagorean/Kepler, PGM/Nag Hammadi, Kybalion) is largely accurate, with correct dates, text citations and appropriately sceptical evidence labels; the dossier commendably debunks 136.1 Hz 'Om', Solfeggio and chakra-Hz tables rather than repeating them. Errors found are mostly second-order: one wrong verse number (HYP 4.29 → 4.94), garbled Surya-Siddhanta time units, three misattributions (Goldman for Beaulieu's C256 set; Satyananda for Swami Rama's 61 points; Frontiers Hum Neurosci for Front Neurosci), one false 'withdrawn' label (Terwagne & Bush), a wrong ney transposition, an inconsistent Zeng-bell pitch, an over-long Solfeggio set, and the pineal-ajna genealogy stopping at 1984 instead of Theosophy/Descartes. Several acoustic numbers (dungchen 58 Hz, singing-bowl EEG parameters, Bhramari EEG, Gyuto Hz) rest on secondary or unconsulted sources and could not be verified because the web-search budget for this turn was exhausted before any query ran; treat those as unverified. The biggest gaps are not errors but omissions: Mandukya Upanishad, Sant Mat/Surat Shabd Yoga, Bernardi 2001, Kirtan Kriya, Huangdi Neijing five tones, the Hemi-Sync/binaural evidence base, and the 432 Hz/Tesla memes the user's request implicitly invokes.

| Original claim | Verdict | Corrected statement | Source |
|---|---|---|---|

The Hamsa Upanishad simply states 21,600 (Dhyanabindu: 21,606). | Surya Siddhanta 1.11-12 (tr. Burgess 1860); Hamsa Upanishad v. 2; Dhyanabindu Upanishad vv. 61-62 |

'Rotation of consciousness'; Swami Rama, Exercise Without Movement (Himalayan Institute 1984), '61 points' |

The fundamental '60-75 Hz' and the 61.7 Hz figure come from Katy Butler's account of Huston Smith's recording, not from the JASA paper, and I could not verify the paper's own Hz values this turn. | Smith H, Stevens KN, Tomlinson RS. No rotation speed is measured, as the dossier correctly says. | Cakmak et al., Front Neurosci 2017;11:3, doi:10.3389/fnins.2017.00003 |

Neither is 'in B'. For a C#-centred track the closest standard ney would be a Şah (Rast = F#) or Müstahsen (Rast = B) ney, or simply a synthesized/retuned reed timbre. | Turkish ney ahenk tables (e.g., Neyzen Süleyman Erguner, Ney Metodu, 2002; ITU Turkish Music Conservatory teaching materials) |
| Marquis Yi of Zeng bells (433 BCE) give the bell inscribed 'huangzhong-gong' at ≈ G#4 (~415 Hz) and the set's Guxian-gong standard at 256.4 Hz | **overstated** | Huang Xiangpeng's analysis gives Zeng's guxian ≈ C (≈256 Hz, the equivalent of Zhou huangzhong) and Zeng's own huangzhong four semitones lower, ≈ G#; but with guxian at C4 = 256 Hz that G# is ≈ 204 Hz (G#3) or 407 Hz an octave up, not 415 Hz. '415 Hz' is just 12-TET G#4 at A440, not a bell measurement; cite the specific bell and its measured frequency or give only the note name. | Huang Xiangpeng, 'Zenghouyi zhongqing mingwen yuexue tixi chutan' (Yinyue Yanjiu 1981/1); Lothar von Falkenhausen, Suspended Music: Chime-Bells in the Culture of Bronze Age China (UC Press 1993), ch. 7-8 |
| Dungchen (1.3->3 m) play 2-3 pedal notes; a 1.7 m horn has fundamental ≈ 58 Hz (B♭1) | **unverifiable** | Could not verify the 58 Hz/1.7 m pairing this turn. Physically, 58 Hz (B♭1) is the pedal of a ~2.7-3 m air column (trombone length); a 1.7 m horn would have its lowest practical note nearer 90-110 Hz. Treat 58 Hz as plausible only for the ~3 m instruments and cite Kemp et al. directly before using the number. | Kemp J, et al., 'Acoustics of the Tibetan long trumpet' (conference paper, c. 2015); standard brass-acoustics length-to-pedal relations (Fletcher & Rossing, The Physics of Musical Instruments, 2nd ed. 1998, ch. 14) |

The '1:4' alternative for Mercury is not Kepler's; drop it. | Kepler, Harmonices Mundi (1619) Book V ch. 4-6; tr. Aiton, Duncan & Field, American Philosophical Society 1997 |

*Items the lens missed, added by the skeptic:*

- Mandukya Upanishad (the foundational AUM text: A-U-M = waking/dream/deep sleep, the silent fourth = turiya) and Chandogya Upanishad 1.1 on the udgitha — the two primary scriptural sources for OM meditation are absent while secondary sources are cited.
- Surat Shabd Yoga / Sant Mat (Radhasoami: Shiv Dayal Singh, Sawan Singh; Kirpal Singh; Eckankar derivative) — the one living tradition whose explicit method is 'inner sound current' + 'soul travel' through named inner regions (Sahasdal Kanwal, Trikuti, Daswan Dwar, Bhanwar Gupha, Sach Khand); it is the closest textual match to the user's 'travel between realms by sound' request and is the missing bridge between Sikh dasam duar and Nada Yoga.
- Bernardi L et al., BMJ 2001;323:1446-1449 ('Effect of rosary prayer and yoga mantras on autonomic cardiovascular rhythms'): Ave Maria and 'om mani padme hum' both slow breathing to ~6/min and enhance HRV/baroreflex — the key empirical support for the dossier's Jesus-Prayer/dhikr 6-breaths-per-minute design choice.
- Kirtan Kriya (Sa-Ta-Na-Ma, Kundalini Yoga/Yogi Bhajan): Newberg et al. 2010 (J Alzheimers Dis 20:517-526, SPECT) and Khalsa/Lavretsky 2013 (Int J Geriatr Psychiatry) — the most-studied chant protocol in the biomedical literature, with a 12-minute fixed structure (2 min aloud, 2 whisper, 4 silent, 2 whisper, 2 aloud) directly usable as a session template.
- Vajra recitation of OM AH HUM with the breath (inhale OM, hold AH, exhale HUM) from the Six Yogas of Naropa / Kagyu-Gelug completion-stage texts — the breath-linked form of the three-syllable practice the dossier gives only as a chant block.
- Huangdi Neijing five-tone/five-organ correspondence (gong-shang-jue-zhi-yu ↔ spleen-lung-liver-heart-kidney, Suwen ch. 5 and 22) and modern Chinese 'five-element music therapy' trials — the actual classical Chinese sound-body doctrine, more relevant than the Zeng-bell pitch question.
- Shakuhachi suizen ('blowing Zen') of the Fuke sect and honkyoku repertoire (e.g., 'Kyorei', 'Koku'): breath-length phrasing, 'ichi-on jōbutsu' (enlightenment in a single tone), and the ro-buki long-tone practice — a Japanese breath-sound discipline with explicit session parameters.
- Theosophical/Western genealogy of the 'pineal = third eye' and 'raise your vibration' ideas: Blavatsky (1888), Leadbeater (1927), New Thought (Atkinson/Kybalion 1908) — plus the pineal-DMT hypothesis (Strassman, DMT: The Spirit Molecule 2001), its partial animal support (Barker et al. 2013, Dean et al. 2019 in rat brain) and the critique that pineal DMT levels are far too low (Nichols 2018, J Psychopharmacol 32:30-36). Needed for honest guide text on the pineal.
- Binaural-beat lineage the user is actually invoking: Dove 1839, Oster 1973 (Sci Am 229:94-102), Robert Monroe/Hemi-Sync and the Gateway Voyage (Journeys Out of the Body 1971; the 1983 US Army 'Analysis and Assessment of Gateway Process' memo by W. McDonnell), and the evidence reviews — Garcia-Argibay et al. 2019 (Psychol Res 83:357-372, small positive meta-effect) vs Ingendoh et al. 2023 (PLoS ONE 18(5):e0286023, 'no consistent entrainment'). If another lens covers this, cross-reference it; if not it is the largest gap.
- 432 Hz / 256 Hz 'scientific pitch' history (Sauveur 1713, Chladni, Verdi's 1884 letter proposing A=432, Schiller Institute 1988 campaign) and the absence of any ancient or physiological basis — necessary because Cousto's A=432.1 tuning will be read by listeners as the '432 Hz' meme.
- Apocryphal Tesla quotes ('If you want to find the secrets of the universe, think in terms of energy, frequency and vibration'; '3, 6, 9') — no primary Tesla source exists; both should be flagged in the guide since they anchor the user's framing.
- Tanpura/jivari acoustics (C.V. Raman 1921, Proc Indian Assoc Cultiv Sci 7:29-33) and the 22-shruti system of Bharata's Natyashastra ch. 28 — the actual Indian sources on drone timbre and microtonal pitch, more defensible than the 136.1 Hz 'Om' figure.
- Greek therapeutic-music sources beyond Pythagorean ratios: Aristotle Politics VIII.7 (katharsis via the Phrygian aulos in Corybantic rites), Plato Republic III.398-399 (ethos of modes), Iamblichus Life of Pythagoras 25 (musical 'medicine' and evening/morning songs) — the classical rationale for mode choice and session timing.
- Genuine Hermetica vs the Kybalion: Corpus Hermeticum I (Poimandres: ascent through the seven planetary spheres shedding faculties) and CH XIII (hymn of rebirth sung in silence) — the authentic late-antique 'ascent through the spheres' texts that pair with the Nag Hammadi Discourse on the Eighth and Ninth.
- Al-Ghazali, Ihya 'Ulum al-Din Book 18 ('On the proprieties of listening and ecstasy') — the classic Islamic ruling on when sama' is lawful, and the Rifa'i 'dhikr al-minshari' (saw-like exhaled 'Hu') and Alevi/Bektashi semah — needed for a complete Sufi section.
- Hasidic nigun (wordless melody; Chabad 'dveikus' nigunim; Rebbe Nachman of Breslov on song and 'hitbodedut') and the Shema/Torah cantillation (te'amim) as the mainstream Jewish sound practices, as distinct from the elite Hekhalot/Abulafian material.
- Mongolian/Tuvan khöömei overtone singing and its acoustic relation to Gyuto chant (both reinforce selected harmonics via vocal-tract formants; Levin & Edgerton, Sci Am 1999) — if the shamanic lens does not cover it, the acoustic method for synthesizing the 'chord' voice is missing.
- Systematic reviews of mantra/chant meditation: Lynch et al. 2018 (Eur J Integr Med 23:101-108) and Álvarez-Pérez et al. 2022 (Int J Environ Res Public Health 19:3380) — needed to set the overall evidence level honestly (small-to-moderate effects on anxiety/stress, no cognitive 'enhancement').

## 3. Indigenous, shamanic and ancestral sound technologies

*Lens scope:* INDIGENOUS, SHAMANIC AND ANCESTRAL SOUND TECHNOLOGIES for trance, soul-flight, healing and contact with other worlds — specific peoples, instruments, documented tempi/pitches, session structures, cosmologies and sources. Evidence levels: strong = instrumented/peer-reviewed measurement; moderate = peer-reviewed ethnography or replicated secondary measurement; weak = single small study or secondary report; anecdotal = practitioner/blog report; traditional = well-documented practice, no claim of measured effect; none = no evidence located.

### Core-shamanism standard (Harner / Foundation for Shamanic Studies) drum tempo and session length

- **Evidence level:** moderate
- **Finding:** The FSS journey standard is a monotonous frame-drum pulse at ~4-5 beats/sec for 15 or 30 minutes, ending with a fast 'callback'. FSS's own page states 'approximately 5 beats per second' (=300 bpm); an instrumented measurement of Harner's 1997 'Shamanic Journey Solo and Double Drumming' CD track 2 found a mean of 254.7 bpm (4.2 Hz) for 14 min 58 s, followed by a ~30 s callback. Rattle alternative also ~5 cycles/sec.
- **Usable parameters:** Pulse 4.2-5.0 Hz (252-300 bpm), isochronous, unaccented, single low frame drum (fundamental ~80-120 Hz, decay <200 ms); journey block 15 min (daily) or 30 min (deep); callback = ~30 s of markedly faster drumming or rattle (~7-10 Hz), then 1-2 min of slow re-grounding beats; listener lies down, eyes covered, headphones at comfortable volume, intention + fixed 'departure point' set before start.
- **Notes:** The often-repeated callback 'four groups of seven beats, then ~1 min rapid drumming, then four groups of seven again' could NOT be confirmed in any FSS primary source; it is a practitioner convention (Ingerman-lineage workshops) — label it as such if used. FSS recordings: 15-min and 30-min solo drum, 'Multiple Drumming' (4 drums in unison), didgeridoo+clapstick, berimbau, rattle versions.
- **Source:** shamanism.org/drumming (FSS official); Gingras, Pohler & Fitch 2014, PLOS ONE 9(7):e102103, doi:10.1371/journal.pone.0102103; Harner 1980 'The Way of the Shaman' (Harper), ch. 'Journeying'; shamanism.eu/en/ressources/drum-rhythms

### Auditory driving / theta entrainment research base behind shamanic drumming

- **Evidence level:** moderate
- **Finding:** Neher (1961 EEG Clin Neurophysiol; 1962 Human Biology 34:151-160) demonstrated 'auditory driving' at 3, 4, 6, 8 Hz; Maxfield (1990, n=12) reported increased theta/high-alpha power with drumming at 4-4.5 Hz; Gingras et al. 2014 (n=39) found 4.2 Hz drumming produced heaviness, dreamlike imagery and perceived heart-rate drop ONLY when paired with shamanic instructions (10/10 vs 2/8), and no larger cortisol decrease than meditation music; Gordon, Karvat, Dagan & Landau 2026 (n=40, 64-ch EEG, 10-min blocks at 1.5/4/9.5 bps) found individual 4 Hz frontocentral neural tracking correlated with altered-experience scores (rho=0.39, p=0.01) while at group level 9.5 bps produced higher altered-experience ratings than 1.5 bps and 4 bps was not significantly different from either.
- **Usable parameters:** 4.0-4.5 Hz pulse is the best-supported single rate; 10-15 min minimum exposure; pair with explicit verbal/written journey instructions (expectation is a large part of the effect); a 9.5 Hz alpha-rate section may raise subjective 'altered' ratings in naive listeners.
- **Notes:** The frequently cited 'Harner & Tryon 1996' EEG study could not be located in primary form; Sandra Harner's documented work is the sIgA (salivary immunoglobulin) increase in experienced journeyers. Treat the '4-7 Hz = theta = trance' formula as plausible but not robustly replicated at the group level.
- **Source:** Neher A. 1962 'A physiological explanation of unusual behavior in ceremonies involving drums', Human Biology 34(2):151-160; Maxfield M. 1990 PhD diss. 'Effects of rhythmic drumming on EEG and subjective experience' (ITP); Gingras et al. 2014 PLOS ONE; Gordon et al. 2026 Scientific Reports 16:10204, doi:10.1038/s41598-026-37700-x; review: Aparicio-Terrés et al. 2025 Ann NY Acad Sci doi:10.1111/nyas.15403; Hove et al. 2016 Cereb Cortex (fMRI of 8-min 4 Hz drumming); Flor-Henry 2017

### Coast Salish winter spirit dance (Pacific Northwest) — Jilek's clinical observations

- **Evidence level:** weak
- **Finding:** Wolfgang Jilek (psychiatrist) documented Salish spirit-dance initiation ('grabbing', seclusion in the smokehouse for days, sensory deprivation, fasting, then dancing to deer-hide drums); secondary sources report he and Ormestad found drum-beat rates concentrated in the 4-7 Hz range during initiation, which he considered the range 'most effective in the production of trance'. A separate ethnographic description gives ~3 beats/sec (180 bpm) for spirit-dance drumming. Jilek also found the altered states of the Salish spirit dance and Plains Sun Dance to be physiologically similar.
- **Usable parameters:** Multiple deer-hide frame drums in unison, 3-7 Hz; initiation sequence = days of darkness/fasting then hours of drumming+dancing; song 'comes' to initiate as a personal spirit song (syəwən).
- **Notes:** The Hz figure is reported via secondary pages (not verified in Jilek's text in this pass). Spirit songs are personal property and must not be reproduced.
- **Source:** Jilek W.G. 1982 'Indian Healing: Shamanic Ceremonialism in the Pacific Northwest Today' (Hancock House); Jilek 1982 'Altered states of consciousness in North American Indian ceremonials', Ethos 10(4):326-343; Jilek & Jilek-Aall 2000, Shaman 8(1); Jilek 1974 'Salish Indian Mental Health and Culture Change'

### Tuvan throat singing (khöömei, sygyt, kargyraa) — measured acoustics

- **Evidence level:** **strong**
- **Finding:** Measured sygyt fundamentals 140-164 Hz (typical ~150 Hz); singers isolate harmonics up to ~H12 by merging two vocal-tract formants into a single narrow filter at 1-2 kHz ('overtone focusing'); kargyraa adds a period-doubling (ventricular folds) giving an undertone one octave below the glottal pitch (e.g., 176 -> 88 Hz; low kargyraa reaches ~55-70 Hz) and can reinforce harmonics as high as the 43rd. Traditionally the styles imitate wind, water, birds and animals and are tied to spirit-of-place (cher eezi) animism rather than formal shamanic séance.
- **Usable parameters:** Drone fundamental 140-165 Hz (male), undertone layer at exactly half (70-82 Hz); melodic overtone line selected from harmonics 6-12 (840-1980 Hz at F0=165); formant 'spotlight' bandwidth ~100 Hz swept slowly; kargyraa timbre = period-doubled sub-octave with strong odd/even harmonics.
- **Notes:** Tuvan shamans (Tos Deer society, Kenin-Lopsan) use the düngür drum and kuzungu mirror; throat singing itself is herder/landscape music, not séance music — don't conflate.
- **Source:** Bergevin, Narayan, Williams et al. 2020 'Overtone focusing in biphonic Tuvan throat singing', eLife 9:e50476 (PMC7064340); Levin & Edgerton 1999 'The Throat Singers of Tuva', Scientific American 281(3); Levin & Süzükei 2006 'Where Rivers and Mountains Sing' (Indiana UP, with CD); Lindestad et al. 2001 J Voice 15(1):78-85 (kargyraa/Mongolian high-speed imaging); Smithsonian Folkways SFW40017 'Tuva: Voices from the Center of Asia'

### Mongolian höömii, kharkhiraa, morin khuur and böö (shaman) drum

- **Evidence level:** traditional (a lineage's own teaching, not a tested claim)
- **Finding:** UNESCO (2010) lists two main Mongolian styles: kharkhiraa (deep: undertone/subharmonic one octave below the drone) and isgeree (whistled overtone). Mongolian tradition says khöömei was learned from birds, whose spirits are central to shamanism. Mongolian böö use a single-headed frame drum (khengereg/khets) with jingling pendants, a horse-headed staff and a chanting/calling ('duudlaga') style; the morin khuur (horsehead fiddle, strings tuned a fourth apart, typically F-B♭ or similar, drone-rich) is a secular epic/long-song instrument, not a séance instrument.
- **Usable parameters:** Kharkhiraa: glottal F0 ~110-130 Hz with sub-octave at 55-65 Hz; isgeree: whistle line 1.5-2.5 kHz; shaman drum pulse accelerating from ~2 Hz (calling) to 4-6 Hz (ongod arrival) with jingles.
- **Notes:** Mongolian shamanic songs call spirit-ancestors (ongod) into the shaman's body (possession-type) rather than only soul-flight.
- **Source:** UNESCO ICH 00396 'Mongolian traditional art of Khöömei' (2010); Pegg C. 2001 'Mongolian Music, Dance and Oral Narrative' (Univ. Washington Press); Humphrey & Onon 1996 'Shamans and Elders'; Heissig 1980 'The Religions of Mongolia'

### Yakut/Sakha khomus (jaw harp) and other Siberian trance instruments (Altai kai, Chukchi yarar, Nenets penzer, Khanty)

- **Evidence level:** traditional (a lineage's own teaching, not a tested claim)
- **Finding:** The Sakha khomus (metal lamellophone, UNESCO-recognised; Britannica: 'once an accompaniment to shamanic ritual') produces a fixed low fundamental with the mouth selecting harmonics: classic Churapcha-type instruments have base frequencies of 50-70 Hz, most tuned models 60-100 Hz. Altai kai is low-register guttural epic singing with topshur lute (epic sessions lasting all night); Chukchi shamans use the yarar frame drum (struck on the rim with a whalebone stick) and Nenets the penzer; Khanty bear-feast songs run for 4-7 nights.
- **Usable parameters:** Khomus layer: fixed fundamental 60-80 Hz, player-swept resonance 500-2500 Hz, pulse at 2-4 plucks/sec; usable as a 'melodic harmonic rattle' over the drum spine.
- **Notes:** Khomus is also a women's and lyrical instrument; its shamanic role is attested but secondary to the drum in Sakha oyuun practice.
- **Source:** Britannica 'khomus'; Oberton.pro instrument measurements (shop data, indicative only); Simon Li 2015 'Jaw Harp: An Acoustic Study' (UIUC Phys 406); Eliade 1964 'Shamanism' ch. 5 (Siberian drums); Hoppál 2007 'Shamans and Traditions'; Smithsonian Folkways 'Tuva, Among the Spirits' (SFW40452); Alekseev 1997 on Sakha

### Evenki/Tungus — origin of the word 'shaman' and the three-world cosmology/drum

- **Evidence level:** traditional (a lineage's own teaching, not a tested claim)
- **Finding:** 'Šaman' entered European languages from Evenki (Tungusic) via 17th-c. Russian reports. Evenki cosmology: Ugu Buga (upper world, at sunrise), Dulin Buga (middle), Hergu Buga (lower, at sunset), linked by a world tree / Pole Star 'world centre' (buyadulin) and, in some groups, a cosmic river (Engdekit). Séance (kamlanie) verbs distinguish ascent (uyisk'i-) and descent (örgisk'i-); drum (untuvun/ungtuvun) cross-ties symbolize the 7 tiers of the upper world; receiving the first drum = admission to the upper world; the drum is the shaman's 'horse/boat'.
- **Usable parameters:** Use as the track's narrative architecture: descent (lower world, sub-80 Hz register), middle (100-200 Hz), ascent through 7 (Evenki) or 9 (Altaic/Eliade) 'heavens' = 7-9 stepped sections; drum tempo rises with each tier in Altaic horse-sacrifice ascent accounts (Eliade ch. 6).
- **Notes:** Shirokogoroff described the shaman as a 'master of spirits' whose séance is a controlled possession/ecstasy; the modern 'journey' is a reconstruction.
- **Source:** Shirokogoroff S.M. 1935 'Psychomental Complex of the Tungus' (Kegan Paul); Anisimov A.F. 1963 'The Shaman's Tent of the Evenks' in Michael (ed.) 'Studies in Siberian Shamanism'; Eliade 1964 'Shamanism: Archaic Techniques of Ecstasy' (Princeton/Bollingen) chs. 1, 5, 8; Hoppál 2013 Shaman vol. 21 (ISARS); Pitt Rivers Museum 'Wandering in Other Worlds' (Evenki)

### Sámi noaidi: goavddis/runebomme drum and joik (luohti)

- **Evidence level:** traditional (a lineage's own teaching, not a tested claim)
- **Finding:** The Sámi drum (goavddis, gievrie, meavrresgárri) head is painted as a cosmic map (sun-centred or zoned into upper/middle/lower worlds); the noaidi struck it while joiking to fall into trance so his free-soul could travel to saivo/Jábmeáibmu (the dead) or to the gods, with helpers joiking to call him back; a bone/brass pointer (vuorbi/árpa) moved on the head for divination. Joik is short, cyclic, often pentatonic, with no end, and 'is' its subject rather than being 'about' it. Drums were confiscated and burned in 17th-18th-c. witch trials (c. 70 survive).
- **Usable parameters:** Drum: large oval frame/bowl drum, pulse 3-5 Hz; joik: 2-4 bar cyclic motif repeated indefinitely, small range (4th-5th), vocables, slight tempo increase over minutes; 'helper' voices calling back = the Sámi callback analogue.
- **Notes:** Joiks are personal/family property; do not use real joiks. Sámi do not describe the noaidi's trip as 'interdimensional' — it is to known places in the landscape and the land of the dead.
- **Source:** Ahlbäck & Bergman (eds) 1991 'The Saami Shaman Drum' (Scripta Instituti Donneriani Aboensis 14); Joy F. 2015 'Sami Shamanism, fishing magic and drum symbolism', Shaman 23; Manker E. 1938/1950 'Die lappische Zaubertrommel'; Rydving H. 1993 'The End of Drum-Time'; Arnberg, Ruong & Unsgaard 1997 'Jojk' (Swedish Radio recordings)

### Lakota: yuwipi, lowanpi and hanbleceya (vision quest)

- **Evidence level:** traditional (a lineage's own teaching, not a tested claim)
- **Finding:** Yuwipi: the medicine man is bound in a star quilt in total darkness; a sequence of songs (opening, calling-the-spirits, prayer round, healing round, closing) is sung continuously with a single drum and rattles for roughly 2 hours; spirits are said to manifest as flying lights/rattles; the room is opened and the ceremony ends with a shared meal. Hanbleceya: 1-4 days alone on a hill without food/water inside a tobacco-tie square, preceded and followed by inipi (sweat lodge, four 'doors'/rounds of songs).
- **Usable parameters:** Darkness + continuous song; Lakota song form: high opening phrase descending (terraced descent), drum ~2-2.5 Hz (120-150 bpm) in ceremonial songs with honor beats; rattle (wagmuha) tremolo ~8-12 Hz; 4-part structure (4 directions, 4 rounds).
- **Notes:** Yuwipi songs are ceremonial property; do not sample. The number four (directions/rounds) is the structural constant in Lakota ritual.
- **Source:** Powers W.K. 1982 'Yuwipi: Vision and Experience in Oglala Ritual' (Univ. Nebraska Press); Powers 1977 'Oglala Religion'; Lame Deer & Erdoes 1972 'Lame Deer, Seeker of Visions'; Black Elk/Brown 1953 'The Sacred Pipe'

### Navajo (Diné): Nightway (Yeibichai) and Blessingway chants

- **Evidence level:** traditional (a lineage's own teaching, not a tested claim)
- **Finding:** The Nightway is a nine-night healing chantway; the ninth night is sung continuously from dark to dawn; Yeibichai songs are distinguished by alternation between normal voice and extreme falsetto (rare in Native North America), steady tempo, 16-beat phrases, form A A A B A A A A B A, accompanied by rattle (and in Enemy Way, a pottery water drum). Blessingway (Hózhǫ́ǫ́jí) restores hózhǫ́ (balance/beauty) with unaccompanied or rattle-accompanied song cycles over 2 nights.
- **Usable parameters:** Falsetto/chest alternation as a timbral marker of 'holy people'; 16-beat phrases at ~2 Hz pulse; nine-night macro-structure can inspire a 9-section arc.
