Theta/delta beats measurably impair vigilance: in a 30-min visual vigilance task, theta/delta beats (1.5 and 4 Hz) produced fewer correct detections, more false alarms and more negative mood than beta beats (16 and 24 Hz).
- **Usable parameters:** Guide: never while driving, cycling, operating tools, cooking on a hob, in a bath/hot tub/pool, or supervising children; allow 10-15 min of re-orientation before driving after a session; do not use while walking outdoors (traffic awareness).
- **Notes:** The Lane study is the clearest experimental evidence that a theta/delta track is not safe background audio for tasks.
- **Source:** Hemi-Sync Listening Tips https://hemi-sync.com/help-center/category/listening-tips ; iAwake disclaimer https://www.iawaketechnologies.com/disclaimer/ ; Lane J.D. et al., Physiology & Behavior 1998;63(2):249-252, PMID 9423966, doi:10.1016/S0031-9384(97)00436-8

### Drugs, alcohol, cannabis and psychedelics

- **Evidence level:** anecdotal
- **Finding:** There is no pharmacological interaction (audio is not a drug), and no controlled study of binaural beats combined with cannabis or psychedelics exists. Producers nonetheless prohibit combination: iAwake 'not to be used while under the influence of alcohol or other mood altering substances' and 'consult a physician ... if taking medication or drugs'. Clinically, cannabis and classic psychedelics increase suggestibility, hypnagogic imagery, time distortion and dissociation, and sedatives/alcohol deepen delta-stage drowsiness, so the combination amplifies exactly the experiences (vibrations, imagery, derealisation) the safety protocol is designed to keep bounded.
- **Usable parameters:** Guide wording: 'Use this track sober. Do not combine with alcohol, cannabis, psychedelics or sedatives. If you take prescribed psychiatric, anticonvulsant or sleep medication, ask your prescriber before using deep-trance audio; do not change any medication because of experiences on the track.'
- **Notes:** Say honestly that this is a precaution, not a documented interaction.
- **Source:** iAwake disclaimer https://www.iawaketechnologies.com/disclaimer/ ; absence of literature noted from Garcia-Argibay 2019 review scope (PMID 30073406)

### Children and adolescents

- **Evidence level:** moderate
- **Finding:** Hemi-Sync states 'All nonverbal recordings are fine for children' and advises parents to preview verbally guided titles; iAwake requires under-18s to be examined for epilepsy/seizure-prone conditions before use; WHO advises keeping children's device volume below 60% and preferring speakers or headphones with parental loudness control. There is no study of deep theta/delta entrainment with OBE/'inter-dimensional' intent in minors, and a track built around dissociative-type states is unsuitable for them.
- **Usable parameters:** Label the track 18+. For any family use, only a short (<=15 min) relaxation segment at <=60% volume, with a parent present.
- **Source:** Hemi-Sync FAQs https://hemi-sync.com/help-center/category/hemi-sync-faqs/ ; iAwake disclaimer https://www.iawaketechnologies.com/disclaimer/ ; WHO Safe Listening Q&A https://www.who.int/news-room/questions-and-answers/item/deafness-and-hearing-loss-safe-listening

### Tinnitus and hyperacusis

- **Evidence level:** weak
- **Finding:** No evidence shows binaural beats worsen tinnitus, and Hemi-Sync notes its low-frequency content means mid/high-frequency hearing loss 'would not interfere'; but iAwake lists 'a history of tinnitus' as a reason to consult a physician, and people with hyperacusis or sound sensitivity commonly find steady pure-tone carriers aversive. Tinnitus pitch is most often in the 3-8 kHz region, far above entrainment carriers, so carrier choice can avoid it.
- **Usable parameters:** Design: keep carriers 100-400 Hz, no spectral content above ~8 kHz louder than -30 dBFS, slow 20-30 s fade-ins, no clicks/transients, a pink-noise or drone bed so pure tones are never exposed. Guide: start at 40% volume if you have tinnitus/hyperacusis; stop if ringing, fullness or muffled hearing persists >1 h after a session (that is a sign the level was too high).
- **Notes:** Post-session ringing or muffling is a temporary-threshold-shift warning sign and should be stated as a stop criterion.
- **Source:** iAwake disclaimer https://www.iawaketechnologies.com/disclaimer/ ; Hemi-Sync FAQs https://hemi-sync.com/help-center/category/hemi-sync-faqs/

### Expected experiences: hypnagogic imagery, 'vibrational state', sleep-paralysis-like phenomena

- **Evidence level:** moderate
- **Finding:** Robert Monroe described a reproducible sequence when lying relaxed in darkness at the sleep border: deepening 'Conditions A-D' of hypnagogic relaxation, then the 'vibrational state' (surging, buzzing, electric-like roaring, pressure) which he treated as the gateway to separation, and a 'fear barrier' (fear of dying, of not returning, of the unknown) that must be passed by relaxing and letting the vibrations run rather than fighting them. These sensations overlap heavily with clinical sleep paralysis / hypnagogic hallucination (buzzing, pressure, sensed presence, inability to move), whose lifetime prevalence is 7.6% in the general population, 28.3% in students and 31.9% in psychiatric patients (34.6% with panic disorder). Anyone doing sleep-border practice with a theta/delta track should expect these and be told they are time-limited (seconds to a few minutes) and harmless.
- **Usable parameters:** Guide script for the vibrational/paralysis state: 'Do not struggle. Slow the out-breath. Move your eyes, then wiggle a finger or toe, then roll onto your side; it passes within a minute or two.' Monroe's own advice: relax, let the vibrations spread, you can return instantly by thinking of moving your physical hand. Expect the fear response to fade over repeated sessions (Monroe) and tell users they may stop at any point by opening their eyes and sitting up.
- **Notes:** Monroe's claim that the vibrational state leads to literal out-of-body travel is anecdotal; the sensations themselves are well documented in sleep science. Monroe's idiosyncratic 'head to magnetic north' instruction has no evidence and can be omitted.
- **Source:** Monroe R.A., Journeys Out of the Body (Doubleday 1971), chapters 'Preliminary Exercises' and 'Separation Techniques' (prior knowledge; text not fetched); Sharpless B.A. & Barber J.P., Sleep Medicine Reviews 2011;15(5):311-315, PMID 21571556, doi:10.1016/j.smrv.2011.01.007 (35 studies, N=36,533)

### Expected experiences: time distortion, emotional release, body sensations

- **Evidence level:** moderate
- **Finding:** Time distortion (sessions feeling much shorter or longer), spontaneous emotional release (tears, laughter, grief), body heat/cold, twitches (hypnic jerks), tingling and 'energy' sensations are routinely reported in deep relaxation, hypnosis, Monroe sessions and meditation; Lindahl's taxonomy groups them as somatic (energy surges, involuntary movements reported after strong 'somatic energy', 63%), affective (positive affect 75%, fear/anxiety 82%) and perceptual changes. They are not signs of success or damage; only persistence beyond the session or escalation across sessions warrants concern.
- **Usable parameters:** Guide: normalise these in one paragraph; give a 'pause' instruction (open eyes, breathe out slowly, name five things you can see, resume or stop); recommend a light blanket (core temperature drops in deep relaxation) and an empty bladder beforehand.
- **Notes:** Avoid interpreting hypnic jerks or 'energy surges' as kundalini/pineal activation in the guide; keep the language descriptive.
- **Source:** Lindahl 2017 PLOS ONE https://journals.plos.org/plosone/article?id=10.1371/journal.pone.0176239 ; Hemi-Sync Listening Tips (discontinue on 'unusual physical or mental discomfort') https://hemi-sync.com/help-center/category/listening-tips

### Realistic single-session outcomes (what one listen can do)

- **Evidence level:** moderate
- **Finding:** Documented single-session effects are modest and specific: (1) a 6 Hz beat on 250/256 Hz carriers raised scalp theta power 'at nearly all positions' within 10 minutes (the authors call 10 min the most effective duration), with frontal-midline theta and a drop in the BRUMS 'tension' factor, but some negative mood items (bad-tempered, confused) rose; n=28. (2) ~30 min of binaural-beat audio before day-case surgery cut STAI state anxiety by 26.3% vs 11.1% for the same music without beats and 3.8% for no intervention. (3) The 2019 meta-analysis of 22 studies (35 effect sizes) gives an overall effect of g = 0.45 (medium) on anxiety, memory, attention and pain, larger when exposure begins BEFORE the task and when exposure is longer; masking noise was not necessary. Nothing in this literature documents 'divine wisdom', manifestation or travel; it documents calmer mood, drowsiness and small cognitive shifts.
- **Usable parameters:** Set the user's expectation: 'first noticeable effect (calm, heaviness, drowsiness, slower sense of time) within 10-15 minutes; state-anxiety reduction of roughly a quarter on a standard scale is the best-documented single-session result.' Design: at least 10 min of stable theta before any transition; 30-min minimum for the main plateau.
- **Notes:** Even the entrainment claim itself is contested: Lopez-Caballero & Escera (Frontiers in Human Neuroscience 2017) found no EEG power enhancement or arousal change from binaural beats, and Orozco Perez et al. (eNeuro 2020) found weaker entrainment than monaural beats and no mood effect (both from prior knowledge, not fetched). Honest guide language: 'may support', 'designed to', never 'will'.
- **Source:** Jirakittayakorn N. & Wongsawat Y., Frontiers in Neuroscience 2017;11:365, https://www.frontiersin.org/articles/10.3389/fnins.2017.00365/full ; Padmanabhan R., Hildreth A.J., Laws D., Anaesthesia 2005;60(9):874-877, doi:10.1111/j.1365-2044.2005.04287.x ; Garcia-Argibay M., Santed M.A., Reales J.M., Psychological Research 2019, PMID 30073406

### Outcomes that accrue over weeks (what daily use can do)

- **Evidence level:** weak
- **Finding:** The multi-week evidence is small and mostly uncontrolled: 8 adults using delta binaural beats daily for 60 days showed reduced trait anxiety (p=0.004) and increased quality of life (p=0.03), with decreases in IGF-1 and dopamine; 15 mildly anxious adults using delta/theta tapes ~30 min/session, 10-17 sessions over 4 weeks, showed reduced diary anxiety but non-significant STAI change. Lavallee et al. found 7 Hz beats 'facilitative' and 15 Hz 'hindering' for meditation EEG, and that only experienced meditators (mean 18 years) could hold theta/delta under a hindering beat while novices (mean 8 months) could not, i.e., the audio does not replace practice. Holosync's 'years of meditation in 30 minutes a day' has no published support.
- **Usable parameters:** Honest timeline for the guide: days 1-7 adaptation (drowsiness, vivid dreams, novelty), weeks 2-4 more reliable state access and lower baseline anxiety, 4-8 weeks before judging trait-level change; evaluate with a simple weekly 0-10 anxiety/sleep/clarity log rather than expecting dramatic events. Daily 30-60 min is the dose used in every study.
- **Notes:** The dopamine and IGF-1 decreases in Wahbeh are unexplained and from n=8; do not cite them as benefits.
- **Source:** Wahbeh H., Calabrese C., Zwickey H., J Altern Complement Med 2007, PMID 17309374 ; Le Scouarnec R.P. et al., Altern Ther Health Med 2001;7(1):58-63, PMID 11191043 ; Lavallee C.F., Koren S.A., Persinger M.A., J Altern Complement Med 2011, PMID 21480784 ; Centerpointe claims https://www.centerpointe.com/

### Honest limit: what sound cannot do (pineal, manifestation, dimensional travel)

- **Evidence level:** none (no evidence)
- **Finding:** No scientific evidence shows that any sound frequency opens, activates or 'decalcifies' the pineal gland, confers manifestation ability, or enables travel between dimensions, realms or times. Headphone audio is converted to neural signals in the cochlea; it does not reach the pineal (a ~8 mm structure at the brain's centre) as vibration. What the literature supports is that structured sound can shift mood, arousal and attention and help people enter absorbed hypnagogic states in which vivid subjective experiences (including felt 'travel') occur; these are experiences, not verified events.
- **Usable parameters:** Guide phrasing that stays honest without dismissing the user: 'This track is a tool for entering deep, absorbed states in which many people report meaningful imagery, insight and a sense of expanded awareness. It does not physically change your pineal gland, and no audio can; treat what you experience as inner experience to reflect on, not as proof of external events.'
- **Notes:** Even the Monroe Institute, the most experience-oriented producer, states OBEs cannot be guaranteed.
- **Source:** Garcia-Argibay 2019 meta-analysis scope (PMID 30073406) ; Tan D.X. et al., Molecules 2018;23(2):301 (pineal physiology review, PMID 29385085) ; Hemi-Sync FAQ statement that 'An OBE cannot be guaranteed' https://hemi-sync.com/help-center/category/hemi-sync-faqs/

### Protocol: time of day and Wake-Back-To-Bed (WBTB) for hypnagogic/lucid use

- **Evidence level:** moderate
- **Finding:** The most-used timing for sleep-border work is Wake-Back-To-Bed: sleep at least 5 hours, wake, stay up 30 minutes to 2 hours (research conflicts on the ideal; some find ~1 h, others find a quick return to sleep after a cognitive technique such as MILD works better), then lie back down for the session. Holosync's variant is 'Wake up one hour before you want to get up'. The 2012 systematic review of 35 lucid-dream induction studies (11 lab, 24 field) rated methodological quality 'relatively low' and found no technique reliably verified, so WBTB is best-practice tradition rather than proven method. WBTB costs sleep; the Sleep Foundation warns of sleep deprivation, sleep-paralysis episodes and dream/reality confusion.
- **Usable parameters:** Guide: Option A (default) morning, within 30 min of waking, seated or semi-reclined, 45-60 min. Option B (hypnagogic/lucid) WBTB: 5-6 h sleep, 30-60 min awake with dim light and dream journaling, then the track lying down; use at most 2-3 nights/week to protect sleep. Option C (sleep) evening delta-only segment; expect to fall asleep (Hemi-Sync: 'That's fine').
- **Notes:** Do not recommend WBTB nightly; sleep fragmentation undermines mood and is itself a risk factor for the dissociative experiences the protocol tries to manage.
- **Source:** Sleep Foundation, Lucid Dreams: https://www.sleepfoundation.org/dreams/lucid-dreams ; Stumbrys T. et al., Consciousness and Cognition 2012;21(3):1456-1475, PMID 22841958 ; Centerpointe FAQ https://www.centerpointe.com/faq/ ; iAwake 'preferably in the morning' https://www.iawaketechnologies.com/faq/beginners-listening-schedule/

### Protocol: posture, environment, body preparation

- **Evidence level:** traditional (a lineage's own teaching, not a tested claim)
- **Finding:** Convergent practice across traditions: Monroe lay supine in a darkened, quiet space (later formalised as the Monroe Institute's bedded, blacked-out CHEC units) with the body relaxed and loose clothing; Harner's core shamanic journey is done lying on the floor in a darkened room with the eyes covered by the forearm or a cloth; Hemi-Sync says 'Sit or lay comfortably in a distraction-free environment' with enclosed headphones; iAwake prefers 'eyes closed and in a sitting position' at the 'highest COMFORTABLE volume'. Sitting reduces sleep onset; lying down favours hypnagogic depth. No tradition specifies temperature or meals, but deep relaxation lowers core temperature and a full stomach increases drowsiness and reflux when supine.
- **Usable parameters:** Guide: lie supine with head raised ~10-15 degrees on a pillow (or semi-reclined chair) to reduce sleep onset and reflux; eye mask or blacked-out room; room 18-22 C with a light blanket; loose clothing, no belt/watch; last meal 2-3 h before, empty bladder, 250 ml water before; phone on airplane mode; for sitting sessions, back supported, hands open on thighs.
- **Notes:** The 'head slightly elevated' detail attributed to Monroe in the brief could not be verified in a fetched source; it is a reasonable practical recommendation on its own merits.
- **Source:** Monroe, Journeys Out of the Body (1971) and Monroe Institute CHEC-unit practice (prior knowledge); Harner M., The Way of the Shaman (1980), ch. 3 'Journey to the Lowerworld' (prior knowledge); Hemi-Sync Listening Tips https://hemi-sync.com/help-center/category/listening-tips ; iAwake volume FAQ https://www.iawaketechnologies.com/faq/what-should-i-set-my-volume-level-at-when-listening-to-these-tracks/

### Protocol: pre-session intention statements (Monroe affirmation, Neville's SATS, shamanic stated intention)

- **Evidence level:** traditional (a lineage's own teaching, not a tested claim)
- **Finding:** Three lineages open sessions with an explicit statement of purpose. Monroe Institute Gateway Affirmation (used before every Hemi-Sync exercise): 'I am more than my physical body. Because I am more than physical matter, I can perceive that which is greater than the physical world. Therefore, I deeply desire to Expand, to Experience; to Know, to Understand; to Control, to Use such greater energies and energy systems as may be beneficial and constructive to me and to those who follow me. Also, I deeply desire the help and cooperation, the assistance, the understanding of those individuals whose wisdom, development and experience are equal or greater than my own. I ask their guidance and protection from any influence or any source that might provide me with less than my stated desires.' Neville Goddard's State Akin To Sleep (SATS): in the drowsy pre-sleep state, construct a brief first-person scene that implies the wish already fulfilled, with sensory detail and the feeling of it being done, and loop it until sleep. Harner's core method: before the drum starts, state a single clear question or intention for the journey, and plan to return at the call-back.
- **Usable parameters:** Track design: reserve 60-90 s of near-silence or soft drone after the opening for the affirmation/intention (spoken or silent); reserve 3-5 min at the 'floor' of the session for a Neville-style imaginal scene (user supplied, one scene, looped); provide in the guide a one-sentence intention template: 'In this session I intend to ___ and I will return fully when the call-back sounds.'
- **Notes:** The Monroe affirmation's last line is itself a safety device (asking 'guidance and protection'); keep it whole if used. Attribute SATS correctly to Neville rather than to 'manifestation science'.
- **Source:** Monroe Institute Gateway Affirmation (text from prior knowledge; monroeinstitute.org returned HTTP 429/404 this session); Neville Goddard, Feeling Is the Secret (1944) and The Power of Awareness (1952); Harner, The Way of the Shaman (1980)

### Protocol: the call-back, return sequence and grounding after

- **Evidence level:** traditional (a lineage's own teaching, not a tested claim)
- **Finding:** Every mature altered-state system has a formal return: Harner's drum 'call-back' is a sharp tempo change (conventionally four groups of seven beats, a half-minute of very rapid drumming, then the four-by-seven again) signalling the journeyer to retrace the route and re-enter the body; Monroe's exercises end with a counted return to ordinary waking consciousness ('C-1'); Hemi-Sync's practical grounding advice is to 'count down slowly from 10 to 1 or splash cool water on your face'. Clinical grounding for dissociation uses the 5-4-3-2-1 sensory check (5 things seen, 4 felt, 3 heard, 2 smelled, 1 tasted), feet pressed to the floor, cold water, and eating something.
- **Usable parameters:** Track design: a clearly audible, pre-announced return cue 5-7 min before the end (tempo/pitch change or voice), followed by a rising beat sweep (e.g., 4 Hz -> 10-12 Hz over 3-4 min) and a counted ascent; end on silence, not a fade into sleep (except the sleep version). Guide after-care: sit up slowly, 2 min feet-on-floor breathing, 250-500 ml water, light snack if light-headed, 5-4-3-2-1 if spacey, no driving for 10-15 min.
- **Notes:** Users in the WBTB/sleep variant should be told the call-back may wake them; that is intended.
- **Source:** Harner, The Way of the Shaman (1980) (call-back pattern from prior knowledge); Hemi-Sync Listening Tips https://hemi-sync.com/help-center/category/listening-tips ; standard clinical grounding (prior knowledge)

### Protocol: journaling and record-keeping

- **Evidence level:** moderate
- **Finding:** Hypnagogic and dream content decays within minutes of waking (the basis of every dream-recall and lucid-dreaming protocol, which instruct immediate dream journaling), so experiences from the session must be recorded before any other activity. Hemi-Sync and lucid-dream guidance both treat a journal as the primary way to track progress and to notice adaptation or adverse patterns across weeks.
- **Usable parameters:** Guide: keep notebook or voice recorder within reach; write within 2 minutes of the call-back: date/time, version used, volume %, 3 lines of content, 0-10 ratings for calm, clarity, discomfort, and sleep quality that night. Review weekly; two consecutive weeks of rising discomfort or falling sleep quality = reduce dose or stop.
- **Notes:** The log is also the mechanism for the honest-expectations section: it shows the user what is actually changing.
- **Source:** Sleep Foundation, Lucid Dreams (dream journaling within MILD/WBTB): https://www.sleepfoundation.org/dreams/lucid-dreams ; iAwake listening schedule (progression tracked over weeks) https://www.iawaketechnologies.com/faq/beginners-listening-schedule/

### Protocol: dose, frequency of use and adaptation period

- **Evidence level:** moderate
- **Finding:** Producers and trials converge on 30-60 min once daily: iAwake builds beginners to '1 hour daily, preferably in the morning' on a 3-days-on/1-day-off cycle; Holosync uses about 30 min/day; trials used ~30 min sessions (Padmanabhan, Le Scouarnec, Lane) or daily use for 60 days (Wahbeh); theta induction is measurable by 10 min and its spread across scalp sites peaked at 10 min and thinned thereafter (Jirakittayakorn). Hemi-Sync says training products 'work best when you listen multiple times'. No producer page fetched states a maximum sessions-per-day figure.
- **Usable parameters:** Guide: full track (45-60 min) once daily, or the 15-min daily version up to twice daily; cap 90 min/day; one rest day per week; days 1-3 start at 50% volume and may stop at 20-30 min; expect 1-2 weeks of adaptation (drowsiness, vivid dreams, occasional headache from clenching/posture), re-assess at 4 and 8 weeks.
- **Notes:** The '2-3 sessions/day' Hemi-Sync figure mentioned in the brief could not be verified and should not be attributed to Hemi-Sync in the guide.
- **Source:** iAwake beginner schedule https://www.iawaketechnologies.com/faq/beginners-listening-schedule/ ; Centerpointe FAQ https://www.centerpointe.com/faq/ ; Jirakittayakorn 2017 https://www.frontiersin.org/articles/10.3389/fnins.2017.00365/full ; Wahbeh 2007 PMID 17309374

### Pineal health, honestly: light hygiene and melatonin

- **Evidence level:** **strong**
- **Finding:** The pineal gland's measurable function is melatonin secretion gated by light reaching the retina. Harvard-reported research: 6.5 h of blue light suppressed melatonin about twice as long as green light of equal brightness and shifted circadian rhythm by 3 h versus 1.5 h; practical advice is to avoid bright screens 2-3 h before bed, use dim red light at night, and get bright light during the day. These are the only interventions with evidence for 'supporting the pineal gland'.
- **Usable parameters:** Guide: evening sessions in a dark room with an eye mask; no phone screen for 2-3 h before an evening/WBTB session (use dim red light to find the player); morning sessions followed by 10-30 min outdoor daylight; keep a regular sleep schedule. Present these as the honest 'pineal support' practices.
- **Notes:** Avoid claims that darkness during the session 'activates the third eye'; the mechanism is simply not suppressing melatonin.
- **Source:** Harvard Health Publishing, 'Blue light has a dark side': https://www.health.harvard.edu/staying-healthy/blue-light-has-a-dark-side

### Pineal calcification and the fluoride debate

- **Evidence level:** weak
- **Finding:** The pineal is the most calcification-prone tissue in the body and calcification increases with age (common on adult CT scans). One 2001 study of 11 aged cadavers found pineal fluoride of 297 +/- 257 mg F/kg wet weight (vs 0.5 mg/kg in muscle) correlating with pineal calcium (16,000 +/- 11,070 mg Ca/kg; r = 0.73, p < 0.02) and concluded the aged pineal 'readily accumulated F'. This is a single small post-mortem study; it does not show fluoride causes calcification, that calcification impairs spiritual perception, or that any diet, supplement (e.g., iodine, boron, tamarind, 'activator' products) or sound 'decalcifies' the pineal; the 2018 review proposing that calcification is an active, bone-like process also notes rejuvenation remains a hypothesis. The relationship between calcification degree and melatonin output is itself inconsistent across studies.
- **Usable parameters:** Guide language: 'Pineal calcification is common and age-related; one small study found fluoride concentrates there. No proven way to reverse it exists, and sound certainly does not. What you can control is light exposure and sleep.' Do not recommend fluoride avoidance, chelation or supplements as part of the protocol.
- **Notes:** State the Luke figures accurately (n=11, aged cadavers) to be honest without dismissing the user's interest.
- **Source:** Luke J., 'Fluoride deposition in the aged human pineal gland', Caries Research 2001;35(2):125-128, PMID 11275672 ; Tan D.X. et al., 'Pineal Calcification, Melatonin Production, Aging...', Molecules 2018;23(2):301, PMID 29385085, https://www.mdpi.com/1420-3049/23/2/301

### Mental-health framing: stop rules and when to seek help

- **Evidence level:** moderate
- **Finding:** Producer rule ('immediately discontinue use' on 'unusual physical or mental discomfort') plus clinical adverse-event data support explicit, non-alarmist stop criteria. Red flags from the meditation adverse-event literature are anxiety/panic, depressive shifts, cognitive anomalies, re-experiencing of trauma, derealisation, involuntary movements and suicidality; 88% of affected practitioners in Lindahl's sample said difficulties extended into daily life, which is the hallmark distinguishing a passing session effect from a problem.
- **Usable parameters:** STOP THE SESSION NOW if: chest pain, severe headache, seizure-like symptoms, panic that does not ease within 5 min of opening eyes and sitting up, or hearing ringing/muffling. PAUSE THE PRACTICE (days to weeks) if: unreality/detachment lasting more than a few hours, intrusive trauma memories, insomnia or sleep paralysis most nights, mood swings, grandiosity or feeling 'chosen'. SEEK PROFESSIONAL HELP if any of those persist beyond 1-2 weeks, impair work or relationships, or include thoughts of self-harm (US: call or text 988; elsewhere local emergency number); Cheetah House for meditation-specific difficulties. Suggested guide wording: 'Most people find these sessions calming. A small number of people, including some with no prior history, find deep-state practices unsettling. That is not a failure or a sign of anything mystical going wrong; it is a signal to ease off and, if it persists, to talk with someone qualified.'
- **Notes:** Keep the tone matter-of-fact; avoid both 'completely safe' and fear-laden wording.
- **Source:** Hemi-Sync Listening Tips https://hemi-sync.com/help-center/category/listening-tips ; Farias 2020 doi:10.1111/acps.13225 ; Lindahl 2017 https://journals.plos.org/plosone/article?id=10.1371/journal.pone.0176239 ; Cheetah House https://www.cheetahhouse.org/

### Combined disclaimer boilerplate (synthesised from Hemi-Sync, iAwake, Brain.fm, Centerpointe)

- **Evidence level:** **strong**
- **Finding:** Reputable producers' legal text shares nine elements: (1) educational/relaxation purpose, not intended to diagnose, treat, cure or prevent any disease and not a substitute for medical or psychological care; (2) do not use while driving or operating machinery; (3) consult a physician before use if you have epilepsy or a tendency toward seizures, auditory disorders/tinnitus, adverse mental or psychiatric conditions, a pacemaker, are pregnant, or take medication; (4) do not use under the influence of alcohol or mood-altering substances; (5) discontinue immediately on unusual physical or mental discomfort; (6) not for children without physician/parent review; (7) no warranty of results or efficacy, results vary and specific experiences 'cannot be guaranteed'; (8) user assumes all risk and waives claims for injury; (9) do not combine with other brain-stimulation devices. Brain.fm omits elements 2-6 and 9 entirely; iAwake includes all nine; Hemi-Sync includes 1-3, 5, 7, 9.
- **Usable parameters:** Ready-to-use wording: 'This recording is provided for relaxation, meditation and personal exploration. It is not a medical device and is not intended to diagnose, treat, cure or prevent any condition, nor to replace professional medical or psychological care. Never listen while driving, operating machinery, or in water. If you have epilepsy or a history of seizures, a hearing disorder or tinnitus, a psychiatric condition, a pacemaker or other implanted device, are pregnant, or take medication, consult a qualified professional before use. Do not use under the influence of alcohol or other mood-altering substances, or together with any other brain-stimulation device. If you experience unusual physical or mental discomfort, stop immediately. Not intended for persons under 18. Individual experiences vary and no particular outcome can be guaranteed. By using this recording you accept responsibility for your own wellbeing.'
- **Notes:** Sacred Acoustics' pages (sacredacoustics.com) returned 404/429 for every URL tried this session, so its wording is not included; from prior knowledge its guidance (headphones, lie down or recline, eye mask, set an intention, not while driving) is consistent with the above but is unverified here.
- **Source:** Hemi-Sync Listening Tips https://hemi-sync.com/help-center/category/listening-tips ; iAwake Disclaimer https://www.iawaketechnologies.com/disclaimer/ ; Brain.fm Terms https://www.brain.fm/terms ; Centerpointe https://www.centerpointe.com/faq/

### Do not combine with light-flicker or electrical stimulation

- **Evidence level:** **strong**
- **Finding:** Audio-visual entrainment (AVE) glasses, cranial electrotherapy (CES), tDCS and PEMF devices carry genuine contraindications (photosensitive seizure risk from 3-30 Hz flicker; pacemaker and pregnancy exclusions for electrical/magnetic stimulation) that audio alone does not; Hemi-Sync explicitly recommends against combining its audio with 'other devices or technologies that influence brain activity'. Pairing this track with a strobe/flicker video or 'light machine' would import the real seizure risk into an otherwise low-risk practice.
- **Usable parameters:** Deliver the track as audio only; any accompanying visual (if a video is ever made) must be static or slow-fading (<1 Hz change), never pulsing in the 3-30 Hz band. Guide: do not use with AVE glasses, CES, tDCS, PEMF or 'frequency' devices.
- **Notes:** Mind Alive's (AVE manufacturer) contraindication page was rate-limited (HTTP 429) this session; its standard warnings (epilepsy, photosensitivity, pacemaker for CES, pregnancy) are from prior knowledge.
- **Source:** Hemi-Sync FAQs https://hemi-sync.com/help-center/category/hemi-sync-faqs/ ; Epilepsy Society UK photosensitivity figures https://epilepsysociety.org.uk/about-epilepsy/epileptic-seizures/seizure-triggers/photosensitive-epilepsy

### Design recommendations from this lens

- Audio-only deliverable; no strobing or 3-30 Hz pulsing visuals ever bundled; state this in the guide (seizure risk is light-based, not sound-based).
- Carriers 100-400 Hz (Oster: beats strongest below ~500 Hz, imperceptible above ~1 kHz), beat frequencies <= 30 Hz, always embedded in a pink-noise/drone bed so pure tones are never exposed; nothing above ~8 kHz louder than -30 dBFS to protect tinnitus/hyperacusis listeners.
- Master with headroom: about -18 to -16 LUFS integrated, true peak <= -1 dBTP; put a 1 kHz, -20 dBFS, 5 s calibration tone at the start so the guide can say 'set this tone to a comfortable speaking level, then do not touch the volume'. Target playback 55-70 dBA, never >75 dBA for 60 min.
- Slow fades everywhere: 20-30 s fade-in, no transients/clicks, no sudden level or pitch jumps; every beat-frequency transition ramps over >= 60 s.
- Session shape for safety: (1) 60-90 s intention slot after a brief settling segment; (2) >= 10 min stable theta before any deeper transition (theta effects appear by ~10 min); (3) main plateau >= 30 min; (4) a pre-announced call-back cue 5-7 min before the end, then a rising sweep (e.g., 4 Hz -> 10-12 Hz over 3-4 min) with a counted ascent, ending on silence. The 15-min daily version keeps the same intention/call-back bookends with a shorter plateau.
- Provide two endings: 'return' (default, ends alert) and 'sleep' (delta-only tail, no call-back) so the WBTB/evening user does not get an alerting sweep by accident.
- Guide must specify: stereo air-conduction headphones (closed over-ear or in-ear best; sleep-band earphones acceptable lying down), no bone-conduction, no speakers, no spatial/3D/crossfeed/EQ DSP, check L/R.
- Guide protocol: supine with head raised ~10-15 degrees or semi-reclined; eye mask or dark room; 18-22 C with a light blanket; 2-3 h after a meal; empty bladder; 250 ml water before; airplane mode; journal within 2 min of the call-back; 10-15 min before driving.
- Timing options in the guide: A) morning default; B) WBTB (>= 5 h sleep, 30-60 min awake in dim/red light, then lie down) limited to 2-3 nights/week; C) evening sleep version in darkness with no screens for 2-3 h before.
- Dose: one full session/day or the short version up to twice/day, cap 90 min/day, one rest day/week, first 3 days at 50% volume and optional early stop; re-assess at 4 and 8 weeks with a weekly 0-10 log of calm, clarity, discomfort, sleep.
- Honest-expectations section: single session = calmer mood, heaviness/drowsiness, slower sense of time, roughly a quarter reduction in state anxiety on standard scales (best-documented); weeks = lower trait anxiety and better sleep/quality of life in small studies; no evidence for pineal activation, manifestation, or literal inter-dimensional/time travel; even EEG entrainment by binaural beats is contested. Use 'designed to', 'may support', never 'will'.
- Include the synthesised nine-element disclaimer verbatim, the three-tier stop rules (stop now / pause practice / seek help), crisis-line pointer (US 988) and Cheetah House as the meditation-difficulty resource.
- Label 18+; pregnancy advice = semi-reclined or left side, <= 60% volume; pacemaker advice = headphones on head only, keep >15 cm from the device.
- Attribute traditional elements correctly (Monroe Gateway Affirmation in full if used; Neville Goddard for SATS; Harner for stated intention and call-back) and present them as practice frames, not mechanisms.

### Cautions from this lens

- The web-search budget for this turn was exhausted before any search ran; all verification was done by fetching primary pages and NCBI E-utilities records directly. Items marked 'prior knowledge' (Monroe book chapters and Gateway Affirmation text, Harner call-back pattern, Neville Goddard texts, Lee 2009 headphone-magnet study, Lopez-Caballero 2017 and Orozco Perez 2020 null-entrainment papers, Mind Alive and Sacred Acoustics guidance, obstetric supine advice, clinical interaural-attenuation values) were not re-verified this session and should be spot-checked before the final guide cites them.
- monroeinstitute.org returned HTTP 429 on every attempt and sacredacoustics.com returned 404/429/400 on every URL tried; the Monroe Institute's own FAQ wording and Sacred Acoustics' listening instructions are therefore NOT quoted. Hemi-Sync (hemi-sync.com, the Monroe product company) wording is verified and used instead.
- The brief's statement that Hemi-Sync advises 'not more than 2-3 sessions per day' could not be found on any fetched page and should not be attributed to Hemi-Sync; the brief's 'Monroe recommends head slightly elevated' detail was likewise unverifiable.
- Several binaural-beat trials are small (n = 8 to 29), mostly uncontrolled or single-blind, and the 2019 meta-analysis (g = 0.45) pools heterogeneous outcomes; independent EEG studies have failed to find entrainment at all. Present effect sizes as 'modest and preliminary'.
- The meditation adverse-event data (Farias 8.3%; Lindahl's severe sample) come from meditation, not binaural audio; extrapolation is reasonable for a track whose explicit aim is deep trance and dissociative-type 'travel', but it is an extrapolation and the guide should say so.
- Pineal claims: the only fluoride-pineal study is n = 11 aged cadavers; there is no evidence that calcification affects spiritual perception, that it can be reversed, or that sound touches the pineal. Any guide text implying otherwise would be false.
- Legal: none of the fetched producer disclaimers claim regulatory clearance; the track must not be described as treating any condition. The combined boilerplate provided is a synthesis of producer practice, not legal advice.
- Sleep-border (WBTB) use trades sleep for access; nightly use risks sleep deprivation and increased sleep-paralysis episodes, which are the very experiences most likely to frighten a user. Limit to 2-3 nights/week and foreground the 'it passes in a minute or two' script.

### Skeptic fact-check of this lens

*Overall reliability:* High. Every numerical claim I could check against a primary source was correct (Farias 2020, Lindahl 2017, Sharpless & Barber 2011, Luke 2001, Wahbeh 2007, Lane 1998, Garcia-Argibay 2019, Epilepsy Society figures, WHO allowance table, Brain.fm Terms), and the dossier is unusually candid about what it could not fetch. Three substantive slips: (1) the headphone-magnet distance is misattributed - Lee 2009 found no interference beyond 3 cm and recommended 3 cm, while 15 cm/6 in is the FDA's generic smartphone/accessory rule; (2) the Jirakittayakorn mood result is reported more favourably than the data (only 'Worried' fell; irritability, low mood, sleepiness rose and vigor fell); (3) Lavallee's 'facilitative/hindering' are condition labels, not findings. The Harner call-back count pattern is unverified and should be softened. Web search was unavailable this turn (budget exhausted) so verification used NCBI E-utilities and direct page fetches; the AHA device page, PubMed HTML pages and Brain.fm's help centre were inaccessible. The main gaps are not errors but omissions: sensory-deprivation hallucination data, meditation-induced psychosis/mania reports and bipolar/sleep-loss risk for WBTB, specific sleep-disorder and vestibular contraindications, the Cheyne sleep-paralysis model that maps onto Monroe's vibrational state, and the current systematic review on entrainment (Ingendoh 2023). With those additions and the three corrections, the lens is fit to source a listening guide.

| Original claim | Verdict | Corrected statement | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Pacemakers: Lee et al. 2009 (Heart Rhythm) found clinically significant interference when headphones were placed over implanted devices, 'and the Heart Rhythm Society/AHA advise keeping headphones at least 15 cm (6 inches) from the device'; guide wording 'keep >15 cm from the device'. | **misattributed** | Lee S, Fu K, Kohno T, Ransford B, Maisel WH, Heart Rhythm 2009;6(10):1432-6 (PMID 19968922) tested 8 headphone models in 100 PM/ICD patients: clinically relevant magnetic interference occurred in 30/100 (38.2% of ICD vs 20.0% of PM patients) when headphones were placed on the chest; removal restored normal function in all but one case; interference was NOT observed at >=3 cm from the skin, and the authors recommend keeping headphones at least 3 cm from the device. The '6 inches / 15 cm' figure is the FDA's generic recommendation for smartphones and accessories with magnets (see Nadeem et al., Curr Cardiol Rep 2022;24:347-353, PMID 35084671), not a finding of Lee 2009. Using 15 cm in the guide is fine as a conservative rule, but cite it to the FDA, not to Lee/HRS. The AHA device page returned HTTP 403 this session and could not be checked. | NCBI E-utilities abstracts: PMID 19968922; PMID 35084671 |
| Jirakittayakorn & Wongsawat 2017: 6 Hz beat raised theta within 10 min 'with frontal-midline theta and a drop in the BRUMS tension factor, but some negative mood items (bad-tempered, confused) rose'. | **overstated** | n=28 (17 experimental, 11 control), 250 Hz left / 256 Hz right. Theta increases vs baseline were widest at 10 min (18 sites) and thinned to 1 site (Cz) at 30 min; the authors state 'the suitable exposure duration was 10 min'. Mood: only the single BRUMS item 'Worried' decreased; 'Annoyed', 'Bad tempered', 'Depressed', 'Exhausted' and 'Sleepy' increased strongly, 'Worn out', 'Tired', 'Confused', 'Mixed up' increased, and all vigor items ('Lively', 'Energetic', 'Active', 'Alert') decreased. Versus control, 'Bad tempered', 'Depressed', 'Confused' and 'Mixed up' were higher in the beat group. The honest summary is 'drowsier and somewhat more irritable/low, slightly less worried', not 'reduced tension'. This strengthens the dossier's own warnings about driving and mood monitoring. | https://www.frontiersin.org/articles/10.3389/fnins.2017.00365/full (fetched) |
| Lavallee et al. 2011 'found 7 Hz beats facilitative and 15 Hz hindering for meditation EEG, and that only experienced meditators (mean 18 years) could hold theta/delta under a hindering beat while novices (mean 8 months) could not'. | **overstated** | 'Facilitative (7 Hz)' and 'hindering (15 Hz)' were the authors' a-priori labels for the conditions, not results. Findings (J Altern Complement Med 2011;17(4):351-5, PMID 21480784): experienced meditators showed increased left-temporal delta/theta power with 7 Hz beats (novices did not); with 15 Hz beats novices showed more gamma than experienced meditators and could not maintain occipital theta. It is a small Persinger-lab study (Laurentian University) with no control for expectancy; cite it as suggestive only. The dossier's conclusion 'the audio does not replace practice' is a fair reading. | NCBI E-utilities abstract PMID 21480784 |
| WHO Safe Listening Q&A page is cited for: device volume 'no more than 60% of maximum' and 'Over 1 billion people aged 12-35 are at risk of hearing loss from recreational sound'. | **misattributed** | The Q&A page's wording is 'Keep the volume below 60% of maximum on your device'; the weekly allowance table (80 dB 40 h; 85 dB 12 h 30; 90 dB 4 h; 95 dB 1 h 15; 100 dB 20 min; 10-60 dB unlimited), the 'well-fitted and noise-cancelling headphones' advice and the 10-minute rest per hour (for loud venues) are verified on that page. The 'over 1 billion young people (12-35)' figure is not on the Q&A page; it comes from the WHO 'Deafness and hearing loss' fact sheet / Make Listening Safe materials. The underlying standard for the 80 dB/40 h weekly dose (and 75 dB/40 h for children) is ITU-T H.870 'Guidelines for safe listening devices/systems' (WHO-ITU, 2018, rev. 2022), which the dossier does not cite. | https://www.who.int/news-room/questions-and-answers/item/deafness-and-hearing-loss-safe-listening (fetched); ITU-T H.870 (prior knowledge) |
| Harner's drum 'call-back' is 'conventionally four groups of seven beats, a half-minute of very rapid drumming, then the four-by-seven again'. | **unverifiable** | The Foundation for Shamanic Studies site only states that its recordings 'conclude with a fast callback rhythm to bring the journeyer back to ordinary reality' and that the journey drum is 'played in a steady rhythm at approximately 5 beats per second'; the specific 4x7 / 30 s / 4x7 pattern could not be confirmed from any primary source this session (it circulates in FSS workshop practice and Harner's 1980 text describes a sharp change of rhythm followed by rapid drumming, but the exact counts should be stated as 'a common FSS convention' rather than quoted as Harner's prescription). Note that the verified ~5 beats/s tempo (about 200-220 bpm, i.e., 4-7 Hz pulse) is the one parameter the dossier omits. | https://www.shamanism.org/drumming/ (fetched) |
| Lindahl et al. 2017 figures: 60 practitioners; 82% fear/anxiety/panic/paranoia; 43% re-experienced traumatic memories; 73% moderate-to-severe impairment; 17% suicidality; 17% hospitalised; median duration 1-3 years; 41% practising 10+ h/day; 72% onset during/just after a retreat; somatic energy 63%; positive affect 75%; 88% difficulties extended into daily life. | **accurate** | All figures verified against the PLOS ONE text (60 practitioners after excluding 13 of 73 interviewed; 32 experts also interviewed). Confirm the dossier's caveat with the paper's own words: 'the 100% frequency of challenging experiences is an artifact of sampling' and 'purposive sampling does not grant the ability to generalize any findings'. One nit: the paper gives no percentage for 'involuntary movements' as such; the 63% is for the broader 'somatic energy' category. | https://journals.plos.org/plosone/article?id=10.1371/journal.pone.0176239 (fetched) |
| Farias et al. 2020: 83 studies, 6,703 participants, 8.3% overall adverse-event prevalence (3.7% experimental, 33.2% observational), anxiety 33%, depression 27%, cognitive anomalies 25%. | **accurate** | Verified: Acta Psychiatr Scand 2020;142(5):374-393, PMID 32820538; 55 of 83 studies (65%) reported at least one adverse event; 8.3% (95% CI 0.05-0.12); 3.7% experimental, 33.2% observational; anxiety 33%, depression 27%, cognitive anomalies 25%; suicidal behaviours and GI problems 11% each (least frequent). Add PMID to the citation. | NCBI E-utilities abstract PMID 32820538 |
| Sharpless & Barber 2011: 35 studies, N=36,533; lifetime sleep paralysis 7.6% general population, 28.3% students, 31.9% psychiatric patients, 34.6% panic disorder. | **accurate** | Verified verbatim (Sleep Med Rev 2011;15(5):311-5, PMID 21571556, PMCID PMC3156892). | NCBI E-utilities abstract PMID 21571556 |
| Luke 2001: 11 aged cadavers; pineal fluoride 297 +/- 257 mg F/kg wet weight vs 0.5 mg/kg muscle; pineal calcium 16,000 +/- 11,070 mg/kg; r = 0.73, p < 0.02. | **accurate** | Verified (Caries Res 2001;35(2):125-8, PMID 11275672). Muscle value is 0.5 +/- 0.4 mg/kg; bone 2,037 +/- 1,095 mg F/kg ash; no correlation between pineal F and bone F. | NCBI E-utilities abstract PMID 11275672 |
| Wahbeh 2007 (n=8, 60 days daily delta beats): trait anxiety down p=0.004, QoL up p=0.03, IGF-1 and dopamine decreased; Lane 1998 (theta/delta 1.5 and 4 Hz vs beta 16 and 24 Hz, 30-min vigilance); Garcia-Argibay 2019 (22 studies, 35 effect sizes, g = 0.45, exposure before task and longer exposure better, masking noise unnecessary). | **accurate** | All verified. Add details: Wahbeh is an uncontrolled pilot using delta (0-4 Hz) beats (J Altern Complement Med 2007;13(1):25-32, PMID 17309374; IGF-1 p=0.01, dopamine p=0.02); Lane et al. n=29, beats embedded in pink noise, participants blind to the beats (Physiol Behav 1998;63(2):249-52, PMID 9423966); Garcia-Argibay is Psychol Res 2019;83(2):357-372 (PMID 30073406). | NCBI E-utilities abstracts PMIDs 17309374, 9423966, 30073406 |
| Epilepsy Society UK: about 3% of people with epilepsy are photosensitive; 3-30 Hz is the usual trigger range; 16-25 Hz most provocative. | **accurate** | Verified verbatim ('around 3% have photosensitive epilepsy'; 'Between 3-30 hertz ... are the common rates'; 'between 16 and 25 times a second are the most likely to trigger'). The page adds that some people are sensitive up to 60 Hz, so a visual deliverable should avoid any periodic flicker up to 60 Hz, not just 3-30 Hz. | https://epilepsysociety.org.uk/about-epilepsy/epileptic-seizures/seizure-triggers/photosensitive-epilepsy (fetched) |
| Brain.fm's Terms contain NO medical, seizure, driving or 'not a medical device' language; only an efficacy warranty disclaimer and a 'physical harm, gross negligence and willful misconduct' liability carve-out; 'Brain.fm omits elements 2-6 and 9 entirely'. | **accurate** | Verified for brain.fm/terms and brain.fm/legal (no epilepsy/seizure/driving/medical-device language; 'efficacy' and 'physical harm' phrases present; users must be 18+).
