| There are no published case reports of binaural beats or audio-only entrainment provoking seizures. | **accurate** | A PubMed search for 'binaural beats seizure' returns a single paper, and it points the other way: Becher et al., Eur J Neurosci 2015;41(2):254-63 (PMID 25345689) applied monaural and binaural beats at 5, 10, 40 and 80 Hz (60 dB SPL, 5-s bursts) to presurgical epilepsy patients with intracranial electrodes, reporting EEG modulation (mostly power decreases) and no provoked seizures; the authors even raise beat stimulation as a possible seizure-control tool. Cite this as positive safety evidence rather than relying on absence of reports alone; note the stimulation was brief, so it does not test 45-60 min exposure. | NCBI E-utilities esearch 'binaural beats seizure' and abstract PMID 25345689 |

*Items the lens missed, added by the skeptic:*

- Becher et al. 2015 (Eur J Neurosci, PMID 25345689) and the Bonn group's review Chaieb, Wilpert, Reber & Fell 2015, 'Auditory beat stimulation and its effects on cognition and mood states', Frontiers in Psychiatry 6:70 - the only direct evidence of beat stimulation in people with epilepsy; belongs in the seizure finding.
- Ingendoh, Posny & Heine 2023, 'Binaural beats to entrain the brain? A systematic review of the effects of binaural beat stimulation on brain oscillatory activity', PLOS ONE 18(5):e0286023 - the current systematic review (14 studies, inconsistent evidence for entrainment); should replace 'prior knowledge' citations as the primary source for 'entrainment is contested'. Also Lopez-Caballero & Escera 2017 is Front Hum Neurosci 11:557 and Orozco Perez, Dumas & Lehmann 2020 is eNeuro 7(2) - give the exact references.
- Sensory-deprivation hallucination risk from the prescribed set-up (eye mask + darkness + headphones is Ganzfeld-like): Mason & Brady 2009, J Nerv Ment Dis 197(10):783-5, found 15 min of light/sound deprivation produced perceptual distortions, paranoia and hallucinations, especially in hallucination-prone healthy volunteers; Wackermann, Putz & Allefeld 2008 (Cortex) on Ganzfeld-induced hypnagogic imagery. The guide should say this is a known, normal effect of sensory restriction and screen for psychosis-proneness.
- Meditation-induced psychosis/mania case literature to support the psychiatric screen: Kuijpers et al. 2007 'Meditation-induced psychosis' (Psychopathology 40:461-464); Yorston 2001 'Mania precipitated by meditation' (Mental Health, Religion & Culture); Sethi & Bhargava 2003 (Aust N Z J Psychiatry). Plus the fact that sleep loss itself triggers mania in bipolar disorder (Wehr, Sack & Rosenthal 1987; Plante & Winkelman 2008), which makes the WBTB option a specific contraindication for bipolar disorder, not just a general 'sleep deprivation' caution.
- Spiritual-emergency framework that clinicians actually use for 'pineal/dimensional' experiences: Grof & Grof 1989 'Spiritual Emergency'; Lukoff, Lu & Turner 1992 and the DSM-IV/DSM-5 V-code 'Religious or Spiritual Problem' (V62.89 / ICD-10 Z65.8); Sannella 1976 'Kundalini - Psychosis or Transcendence?'; the Spiritual Emergence Network / Spiritual Crisis Network UK as additional referral resources alongside Cheetah House.
- Sleep-paralysis phenomenology that maps directly onto Monroe's 'vibrational state': Cheyne, Rueffer & Newby-Clark 1999 (Consciousness and Cognition 8:319-337) three-factor model - Intruder (sensed presence), Incubus (pressure/breathing difficulty) and 'Unusual Bodily Experiences' (floating, OBE, vestibular-motor sensations); Cheyne 2003 on vibrations/buzzing; Sharpless' CBT protocol for recurrent isolated sleep paralysis (Sharpless & Doghramji 2015, Oxford UP; Sharpless 2016 Nat Sci Sleep) which supplies an evidence-based version of the 'do not struggle, move a finger' script. Also exploding head syndrome (Sharpless 2014/2015: ~18% of students) as another benign sleep-border event the user may encounter.
- Specific sleep-disorder contraindications for the WBTB/sleep-border option that the dossier never lists: narcolepsy, REM sleep behaviour disorder, recurrent isolated sleep paralysis, nightmare disorder, untreated obstructive sleep apnoea (supine lying worsens it), and bipolar disorder (see above).
- Vestibular, migraine and sound-sensitivity conditions beyond hyperacusis/tinnitus: vestibular migraine and Meniere's disease (low-frequency drones and slow amplitude modulation can provoke dizziness/nausea), migraine with aura, misophonia (steady tones/breath sounds), and autistic sensory sensitivity - all deserve a 'start short, start quiet' line.
- Physical hazards of in-ear/sleep headphones worn lying down for 45-60 min: otitis externa, cerumen impaction, pressure sores of the pinna/ear canal, and Bluetooth battery heat; recommend flat sleep-band speakers or over-ear only when lying on the back, and cleaning/rotation.
- Equal-loudness physics the calibration-tone recommendation depends on: ISO 226:2003 equal-loudness contours show a 100-250 Hz carrier needs roughly 10-20 dB more SPL than 1 kHz to sound equally loud, so A-weighted meters and phone 'dB apps' under-read the hearing dose of low-frequency carriers; and ITU-T H.870 (WHO/ITU safe listening device standard) with its 80 dBA/40 h adult and 75 dBA/40 h child weekly dose, which is the authoritative version of the 'WHO allowance'.
- Binaural-beat perception limits with primary sources, not just Oster: Licklider, Webster & Hedlun 1950 (JASA) for the ~1 kHz carrier ceiling; Perrott & Nelson 1969 (JASA) showing the beat ceases to be heard above roughly 30-35 Hz difference at a 500 Hz carrier; Oster's own observation that the beat is best heard near 440 Hz. These justify the 100-400 Hz / <=30 Hz design rule the dossier gives.
- Drumming parameters for the 'call-back' design: FSS journey drumming is ~5 beats/s (~205-220 bpm), i.e., in the theta band; Neher 1961/1962 ('auditory driving' of EEG by drumming) and Maxfield 1990/1994 EEG study of shamanic drumming are the only data; Rouget 1985 'Music and Trance' is the standard ethnomusicological critique that trance is culturally framed, not acoustically forced - useful for the honest-expectations section.
- The 1983 US Army/CIA 'Analysis and Assessment of Gateway Process' memo (declassified 2003) - the user will almost certainly have seen it; the guide should state plainly that it is a one-officer literature review, not an experimental validation of Hemi-Sync or dimensional travel.
- Monroe Institute residential-program screening (Gateway Voyage applications ask about psychiatric history and current medication, and the Institute advises against attendance during acute mental-health crisis) - this is the most relevant producer precedent for a psychiatric screen but could not be verified this session (monroeinstitute.org rate-limited); flag as to-be-checked rather than omitting.
- Padmanabhan 2005 details that help the expectation-setting: it was a randomised trial of 108 day-surgery patients, 30 min pre-operative listening; effect was on state anxiety only, no physiological endpoint. Add n to the citation.

## 7. Design panel

Three designers each proposed a full architecture from a different school; two judges scored them. The shipped session is a synthesis (see DESIGN.md).

### Design 0: Threshold: an evidence-first 60-minute binaural/isochronic entrainment session (alpha settle → 6 Hz theta → 4.2 Hz drum journey → 40 Hz integration → 14 Hz return) (60 min)

*Rationale:* Design logic, element by element, with its evidence level: (1) Carriers 250 and 400 Hz (strong): the beat percept is strongest at 350-500 Hz (Oster 1973; Licklider et al. 1950) and the only measurable binaural-beat ASSR was at ~400 Hz (Schwarz & Taylor 2005); 250/256 Hz is the exact stimulus that raised theta in the one controlled time-course study (Jirakittayakorn & Wongsawat 2017); carriers above ~500-1000 Hz give almost nothing (Grose & Mamo 2012: 1 of 10 at 810/850 Hz), so 963/852/528/432 Hz 'Solfeggio'/'pineal' tones are excluded as carriers (they are 1999 Puleo/Horowitz numerology with no pineal evidence, and Wahbeh 2007 found no melatonin change after 60 days of beats). (2) Beat range 4.2-14 Hz only (strong): beats are salient below ~20 Hz with a ~30 Hz ceiling (Perrott & Nelson 1969; Ingendoh 2023), are perceived as a rotating image below ~2-3 Hz (Perrott & Musicant 1977), and delta/theta beats degrade vigilance and mood (Lane 1998), so there is no delta or 'epsilon' binaural segment and the session ends at 14 Hz, not in deep delta. (3) Ramps <= 1-1.4 Hz/min as smoothstep (practitioner convention, anecdotal) with fixed carriers so nothing jumps. (4) Theta hold of 12 min at 6 Hz (moderate): the effect appears by 10 min, peaks 10-15 min and habituates by 25-30 min, so each block is 10-16 min and something changes at every boundary; the mood caveat from that study is weak because the silent control showed the same drowsiness. (5) Isochronic/monaural reinforcement on separate carriers (strong for mechanism): acoustic AM gives a larger steady-state response than binaural beats and survives speakers/leakage (Schwarz & Taylor 2005; Orozco Perez 2020; Picton 2003), so every beat rate is delivered twice, and the 40 Hz block is monaural + click train (Galambos 1981; Chan 2022 dosing and safety) rather than a binaural pair. (6) Drum journey at 4.5 -> 4.2 Hz for 13+ min (moderate/weak): the only instrumented tradition tempo (Gingras 2014: 254.7 bpm) with EEG support (Maxfield 1990; Gordon 2026 individual 4 Hz tracking) and the explicit finding that the instructions, not the drum, create the journey phenomenology, which is why the track carries a journey instruction and a callback. (7) 0.1 Hz breath pacer on the bed (strong for the breathing effect itself): 5.5-6 breaths/min is the HRV resonance frequency (Lehrer), auditory pacing demonstrably slows breathing (RESPeRATE trials; Bernardi 2001), and this is the single most reliable way for a listener to feel a change on the first session; humming adds a nitric-oxide and vagal lever (Weitzberg & Lundberg 2002). (8) 40 Hz for 15 min then SMR/beta return (moderate): the best-dosed gamma protocol in humans and the only safe way to end alert. (9) Mix: noise bed -25 dB and no music under the beats (Monroe patent >= 10 dB; Grose 2012 shallow salience), reverb only on drum/bowl buses, zero ILD and no processing on the binaural bus, every tonal component >= 1 ERB from every other so no unintended beats arise. (10) Level: ~60-65 dB SPL via a calibration tone (research used 65-70; hearing-safe under WHO/ITU 80 dBA/40 h). What the track honestly targets: relaxation and state-anxiety reduction (the most replicated effect, g ~0.4-0.45), slower breathing with higher HRV, hypnagogic absorption and imagery in the drum block, and a crisp alert finish; it does not and cannot open the pineal gland, alter 'vibration', or move anyone between dimensions or times, and the guide must say that entrainment of the whole cortex by binaural beats is itself unproven (Ingendoh 2023: 5 for, 8 against, 1 mixed). Safety built into the architecture: headphones required for the binaural bus only; no listening while driving; no sub-20 Hz content; no flicker ever bundled (seizure risk is light-based; Becher 2015 found no provoked seizures with beats in epilepsy patients); 40 Hz layers quiet, brief and never in sleep use; mandatory return segment; nine-element producer disclaimer and the stop/pause/seek-help rules from the safety lens in the guide; 18+.

| Stage | Minutes | Carrier(s) | Beat Hz | Layers | Sources |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Stage 1 - Arrival (alpha pre-exposure, calibration, breath, hum, intention) | 0–5 | Pair A: L 400.0 / R 410.0 Hz (0 dB reference). Pair B: L 250.0 / R 260.0 Hz (-3 dB float, which is roughly equal loudness after ISO 226 correction at ~60 phon). WHY: 400 Hz sits in the perceptual sweet spot for binaural beats (Oster 1973 best near 440 Hz; Licklider, Webster & Hedlun 1950 widest beat range at 350-500 Hz carriers; Schwarz & Taylor 2005 binaural-beat ASSR only measurable at a ~400 Hz mean carrier; Orozco Perez 2020 used 396.5/403.5 and 380/420). 250 Hz is the exact carrier that produced the measured theta rise in Jirakittayakorn & Wongsawat 2017 (250/256 Hz, 65 dB SPL). The two pairs are 150 Hz apart (> 2 ERB; ERB = 52 Hz at 250 Hz, 68 Hz at 400 Hz, Glasberg & Moore 1990) so they are resolved in separate auditory filters and do not interact. Same-sign offset on both pairs (right ear always higher) so the two beat images lateralise the same way and never cancel. | 10 → 10 | 0:00-0:05 calibration tone: 1 kHz sine at -20 dBFS, both channels, 20 ms Hann edges; 0:05-0:10 silence; 0:10-0:20 raised-cosine fade-in of all beds (10 s).; Binaural bus (pairs A and B) at 10 Hz beat, phase-continuous, perfectly centred, ILD 0.00 dB, dry, no processing.; Isochronic reinforcement: 170 Hz tone, Hann-gated pulses at 10 Hz, 50% duty (period 100 ms, on-time 50 ms), identical in both channels, enters 3:00 with 8 s fade, level -9 dB. 170 Hz is > 1 ERB from both 250 and 400 Hz (ERB at 210 Hz = 47 Hz) so it adds no roughness; it is a true amplitude-modulated stimulus of the kind that drives the auditory steady-state response (Picton 2003; Chaieb 2017 monaural beats on 107-223 Hz carriers reduced state anxiety in 5-min blocks).; Pink-noise bed: pk3 (Kellet) pink noise, -25 dB relative to pair A RMS, 0.7 shared + 0.3 independent per channel (IACC ~0.7), notched -6 dB in 198-302 Hz and 332-468 Hz (+/-1 ERB around each binaural carrier) so the carriers stay clear.; Breath pacer on the noise bed and the drone ONLY: raised-cosine gain swell +/-3 dB with a matching low-pass sweep 2 kHz (exhale) -> 6 kHz (inhale); cycle starts at 7.5 s (8 breaths/min, 3.4 s rise / 4.1 s fall) and lengthens linearly to 10.0 s (6/min, 4.5 s rise / 5.5 s fall, 0.1 Hz) by 4:00, then holds. 0.1 Hz is the HRV resonance band (Lehrer & Gevirtz 2014; Lehrer, Vaschillo & Vaschillo 2000 protocol range 4.5-6.5/min); guide offers a 12 s variant for tall/male listeners.; Drone: 110 Hz sine (A2) with two unison copies detuned +/-0.15% (0.3 Hz shimmer) and a slow +/-0.05% random walk, no upper harmonics (keeps it > 1 ERB from 170 Hz), enters 1:00 with 8 s fade, -10 dB float (equal-loudness note: needs ~10 dB more than a 400 Hz tone to sound as loud; set by phon, not dBFS). It is the humming reference pitch (A2 for men, A3 220 Hz implied octave for women). Its 'archaeoacoustic' lineage (Jahn, Devereux & Ibison 1996 95-120 Hz chamber resonances; Cook 2008 n=30) is weak and must be labelled symbolic/aesthetic in the guide.; Voice cues (optional voiced version; a voice-free render is also produced): 0:20 'settle, set the tone to a quiet speaking level, then leave the volume'; 1:30 'from the next bell, hum gently on every out-breath, any comfortable pitch'; 4:00 'in one sentence, silently say what you intend, and that you will return when the drum calls'; voice mono-centred, -6 dB, band-limited 120 Hz-6 kHz, dry, each cue <= 12 s.; Bowl boundary cue: single struck bowl at 4:55, synthesized fundamental 600 Hz (> 1 ERB above 410 Hz; ERB at 500 Hz = 79 Hz), partials 1 : 2.83 : 5.42 : 8.77 (Terwagne & Bush 2011), doublet split 2 Hz on the fundamental, T60 20 s / 12 s / 7 s / 3 s, -12 dB peak, reverb return -15 dB (bowl bus only). | Garcia-Argibay, Santed & Reales 2019 Psychol Res 83(2):357-372 (pre-exposure and longer exposure moderate the effect; noise not necessary); Oster 1973 Sci Am 229(4):94-102; Licklider, Webster & Hedlun 1950 JASA 22:468-473; Schwarz & Taylor 2005 Clin Neurophysiol 116:658-668 (carrier sweet spot); Lehrer & Gevirtz 2014 Front Psychol 5:756; Lehrer, Vaschillo & Vaschillo 2000 Appl Psychophysiol Biofeedback 25:177-191 (0.1 Hz resonance breathing, 4.5-6.5/min range); Bernardi et al. 2001 BMJ 323:1446-1449 (mantra/rosary recitation slows breathing to ~6/min and raises baroreflex sensitivity); Weitzberg & Lundberg 2002 Am J Respir Crit Care Med 166:144-145 (humming raises nasal nitric oxide ~15-fold, n=10); Hatha Yoga Pradipika 2.68 Bhramari (traditional); Monroe Gateway 'Resonant Tuning' hum (practitioner lore); Harner 1980 The Way of the Shaman: stated intention and commitment to return before the journey (traditional); Gingras, Pohler & Fitch 2014 PLoS ONE 9(7):e102103 (instructions, not sound, drive journey phenomenology); WHO/ITU-T H.870 safe listening (80 dBA/40 h); ISO 226:2003 equal-loudness correction for low carriers |
| Stage 2 - Descent (alpha to theta ramp) | 5–13 | Unchanged: L 400.0 / R (400 + f_beat) Hz at 0 dB and L 250.0 / R (250 + f_beat) Hz at -3 dB. The carriers never move; only the right-ear offset follows f_beat(t). WHY: a fixed carrier keeps the percept stable and avoids any pitch-glide cue (Grose, Buss & Hall 2012: the binaural percept is only as salient as a shallow AM, so anything moving in the carrier band masks it). | 10 → 6 | Binaural bus: f_beat(t) smoothstep 10 -> 6 Hz over 5:00-13:00 (0.5 Hz/min, within the <= 1 Hz/min practitioner convention of SBaGen/Gnaural/Atwater 1997); phase-continuous accumulators, no crossfades.; Isochronic 170 Hz: pulse rate = f_beat(t) (same shared schedule), 50% Hann duty, level rises -9 -> -6 dB over the stage (8 s-slewed gain) so the acoustic AM percept strengthens as the beat slows.; Pink-noise bed -25 dB with 10.0 s breath swell +/-3 dB (unchanged).; 110 Hz drone -10 dB, breath-swelled (unchanged).; No drum, no bowl, no voice in this stage (silence from cues = fewer attention captures during induction).; Bowl boundary cue at 12:55 (same 600 Hz bowl, -12 dB). | Jirakittayakorn & Wongsawat 2017 Front Neurosci 11:365 (6 Hz is the best-documented theta recipe; theta rises by 10 min); SBaGen (Jim Peters), Gnaural, Atwater 1997 J Sci Exploration 11(3):263-274 (ramp-rate convention; practitioner, not controlled evidence); Grose, Buss & Hall 2012 Hear Res 285:40-45 (binaural salience is shallow, keep the carrier band clean); Chaieb, Wilpert, Reber & Fell 2015 Front Psychiatry 6:70 (theta-alpha beats 5-10 Hz for anxiolysis; noise bed acceptable); Lane et al. 1998 Physiol Behav 63:249-252 (theta beats impair vigilance: this is why the guide forbids driving/tasks) |
| Stage 3 - Theta core (6 Hz hold, the measured-effect block) | 13–25 | L 250.0 / R 256.0 Hz (now raised to 0 dB, the lab recipe) and L 400.0 / R 406.0 Hz (now -3 dB). The level roles of the two pairs cross over 13:00-14:00 with 60-s linear (correlated-signal) gain ramps; frequencies untouched. WHY: 250/256 at ~65 dB SPL is the exact Jirakittayakorn & Wongsawat 2017 stimulus; 400 Hz stays as the perceptually strongest beat carrier (Oster, Licklider). | 6 → 6 | Binaural bus: 6.00 Hz hold, both pairs, same-sign offset.; Isochronic 170 Hz at 6.00 Hz (period 166.7 ms, on-time 83.3 ms, Hann rise/fall 41.7 ms each), -6 dB, both channels. This is the acoustic (monaural) AM reinforcement: monaural/AM beats give a larger steady-state response than binaural beats (Schwarz & Taylor 2005; Orozco Perez 2020) and work even if the listener's headphones leak; it also makes the 6 Hz pulse something the listener can feel on first listen.; Pink-noise bed -25 dB, breath swell 10 s +/-3 dB.; 110 Hz drone -10 dB, breath-swelled.; No drum yet (the drum is reserved for the 4.2-4.5 Hz journey so two near-rate pulse streams never fight).; Voice cue 24:15-24:40 (journey instruction, <= 25 s, -6 dB) in the voiced render; bowl boundary cue at 24:55. | Jirakittayakorn & Wongsawat 2017 Front Neurosci 11:365 (250/256 Hz, 65 dB SPL, theta at 10-15 min; mood items in the beat group matched the silent control, so the '20-min theta cap' is a comfort choice, not a controlled finding); Orozco Perez, Dumas & Lehmann 2020 eNeuro 7(2) (7 Hz 396.5/403.5; 8-min blocks give a measurable SSR; monaural stronger; binaural theta drove cross-frequency 40 Hz activity); Karino et al. 2006 J Neurophysiol 96:1927-1938 (MEG responses to 4 and 6.66 Hz binaural beats at 240/480 Hz carriers); Lavallee, Koren & Persinger 2011 JACM 17:351-355 (7 Hz labelled facilitative; small Persinger-lab study, suggestive only); Padmanabhan, Hildreth & Laws 2005 Anaesthesia 60:874-877 (n=108; ~30 min of theta/delta-range beats cut state anxiety 26.3% vs 11.1% same audio without beats); Ingendoh, Posny & Heine 2023 PLoS ONE 18(5):e0286023 (honesty anchor: 5 of 14 EEG studies support entrainment, 8 contradict) |
| Stage 4 - Journey (4.5 -> 4.2 Hz frame drum, isochronous, with callback) | 25–39.5 | L 250.0 / R (250 + f_beat) Hz at 0 dB and L 400.0 / R (400 + f_beat) Hz at -3 dB, unchanged. WHY: the binaural beat tracks the drum rate (6 -> 4.5 -> 4.2 Hz) so the two periodic stimuli reinforce instead of fighting (keep drum and beat within +/-0.5 Hz); the beat never goes below 4.0 Hz because below ~2-3 Hz a binaural beat is heard as a rotating image rather than a pulse (Perrott & Musicant 1977; Ross 2014) and delta beats measurably degrade vigilance and mood (Lane 1998). | 6 → 4.2 | Binaural bus: smoothstep 6.0 -> 4.5 Hz over 25:00-28:00 (0.5 Hz/min), then linear 4.5 -> 4.2 Hz over 28:00-38:00, hold 4.2 Hz to 39:30.; Frame drum (physically modelled, see synthesis): f0 72 Hz, modes 1 : 1.59 : 2.14 at 0 / -8 / -14 dB, pitch envelope +12% tau 35 ms, amplitude tau 220 ms (f0) / 90 ms (modes), skin-slap noise burst 300-3000 Hz tau 10 ms, 2.5 ms Hann attack, body low-pass 2 kHz; rate = f_beat(t) from 25:30 (fade-in 8 s) i.e. 4.5 Hz (270 bpm) easing to 4.2 Hz (252 bpm) by 38:00, isochronous and unaccented apart from a +8% velocity every 4th hit, timing jitter sigma 4 ms, velocity +/-10%, alternating +/-0.35 equal-power pan, reverb return -15 dB (drum bus only). Level -6 dB RMS, peaks -3 dB. Sustained 13 min continuously (Maxfield's minimum).; Callback 38:30-39:30 (labelled in the guide as Harner's core-shamanism convention, not an ethnographic universal, and not confirmed on the FSS page which only says 'fast callback rhythm'): 38:00-38:30 drum eases 4.2 -> 3.8 Hz (binaural stays 4.2); 38:30 four groups of seven sharp beats at 2 Hz with 1.5 s gaps (~14 s), then 30 s of rapid drumming at 8 Hz (480 bpm), then four groups of seven at 2 Hz again (~14 s), ending 39:28; voice cue at 38:25 'the drum is calling you back; retrace your path' (voiced render).; Isochronic 170 Hz: rate = f_beat(t), -6 dB until 25:30 then -9 dB once the drum is in (drum takes over as the dominant AM stimulus; two equal-level pulse streams at the same rate but different phase would blur the pulse).; Pink-noise bed -25 dB with breath swell reduced to +/-1.5 dB (free breathing during the journey; the pacer is a background cue only).; 110 Hz drone -10 dB (unchanged; the 72 Hz drum fundamental and 110 Hz drone are 38 Hz apart, > 1 ERB at 90 Hz = 34 Hz).; Optional 'unseen presence' texture is deliberately omitted (no sub-20 Hz content; no bullroarer/infrasound, which is not reproducible on headphones and is linked to unease: Tandy & Lawrence 1998).; Bowl boundary cue at 39:25 (overlaps the final callback group, -12 dB). | Foundation for Shamanic Studies, shamanism.org/drumming ('approximately 5 beats per second', 15/30-min recordings ending in a 'fast callback rhythm'); Harner 1980 The Way of the Shaman ch. 3 (205-220 bpm text figure; 4x7 / rapid / 4x7 callback convention); Gingras, Pohler & Fitch 2014 PLoS ONE 9(7):e102103 (Harner CD measured 254.7 bpm = 4.2 Hz; dreamlike imagery 10/10 vs 2/8 with vs without journey instructions; cortisol fell equally in all conditions); Maxfield 1990 PhD, Institute of Transpersonal Psychology (4-4.5 bps 'most inducting for theta gain', pattern sustained >= 13-15 min; n=12, unpublished); Gordon, Karvat, Dagan & Landau 2026 Sci Rep 16:10204 (n=39, 10-min blocks at 1.5/4/9.5 bps; 4 Hz neural tracking vs altered-experience rho=0.39, p=0.01; group effect weak); Neher 1961 EEG Clin Neurophysiol 13:449-451 and 1962 Human Biology 34:151-160 (auditory driving at 3-8 beats/s); Will & Berg 2007 Neurosci Lett 424:55-60 (EEG entrainment to periodic drum/clicks 1-8 Hz); Aparicio-Terres et al. 2025 Ann NY Acad Sci 1550:55-70 (rhythmic sound mainly fosters absorption and relaxation; thalamocortical entrainment is a hypothesis); Rouget 1985 Music and Trance (trance is culturally framed, not acoustically forced); Eliade 1964 / Harner three-world journey frame (20th-century synthesis, presented as an inspired composite, not 'the' ancestral method); Lakota/FSS 'return and account' convention (traditional) |
| Stage 5 - Integration (40 Hz monaural/click gamma over an alpha binaural climb) | 39.5–55.5 | Binaural pair B only: L 250.0 / R (250 + f_beat) Hz at 0 dB. Pair A (400/4xx) fades OUT 39:30-40:30 (equal-power, 60 s) and its spectral slot is taken by the monaural gamma pair. WHY: the 40 Hz stimulus is placed at 380 + 420 Hz summed identically in both ears (Orozco Perez 2020's exact gamma carriers; monaural gamma 'peaked highest' there), which is a 40 Hz acoustic beat / 100%-depth AM at 400 Hz. Keeping a binaural pair at 400 Hz simultaneously would put 380/400/410/420 Hz inside one ERB and generate unintended 10/20/30 Hz beats (self-defeating stack), so the binaural beat moves entirely to the 250 Hz carrier, 120 Hz (> 2 ERB) below 380 Hz. | 4.2 → 10 | Binaural bus (250 Hz pair): smoothstep 4.2 -> 10.0 Hz over 39:30-45:30 (~1 Hz/min ascent), hold 10.0 Hz to 55:30 (alpha; Reedijk 2013 alpha 10 Hz on 335/345 and 40 Hz on 320/360 were their two conditions; Cade/Wise 'awakened mind' = alpha present alongside beta/gamma, anecdotal).; Monaural 40 Hz layer: 380.0 Hz + 420.0 Hz sines, equal amplitude, identical in both channels, fade-in 40:00-40:30, fade-out 55:30-56:00, level -9 dB relative (each component -15 dB). This is the physiologically strongest 40 Hz driver (Schwarz & Taylor 2005; Orozco Perez 2020) and works on speakers too. Note: a 40 Hz BINAURAL beat is a subtle percept but does give a reliable, smaller ASSR (Draganova 2008; Grose & Mamo 2012 10/10 young adults at 390/430; Ross 2014 largest cortical response at 40 Hz); we still choose monaural because the response is larger and the 250 Hz binaural pair is already occupied.; 40 Hz click train: 1 ms raised-cosine-shaped band-limited clicks (300 Hz-6 kHz, nothing above 8 kHz louder than -30 dBFS for tinnitus safety) every 25.0 ms (4% duty), identical in both channels, 40:30-55:30 (15 min), -12 dB relative, 20 s fades. Mirrors the Chan 2022 / Martorell 2019 pulse structure; our level (~55-60 dB SPL at the ear) is below the 68-78 dB used in those trials and the guide says so.; Isochronic 170 Hz: rate = f_beat(t) 4.2 -> 10 Hz, -9 dB (kept quiet so the 40 Hz layer dominates the AM percept; 170 Hz and the 380/420 pair are > 2 ERB apart).; Drum: off (last hit at 39:28; its reverb tail carries into 39:35).; Pink-noise bed -25 dB, breath swell restored to +/-3 dB at 10.0 s.; 110 Hz drone -10 dB, breath-swelled.; Voice cue 45:30-45:45 (voiced render): 'eyes still closed, bring your intention back to mind; notice what is different now'. Bowl boundary cue at 55:25. | Jirakittayakorn & Wongsawat 2017 Int J Psychophysiol 120:96-107 (40 Hz beat: gamma within 15 min; 20 min improved working-memory recall); Chan et al. 2022 PLoS ONE 17(12):e0278412 and 2025 Alzheimer's & Dementia doi 10.1002/alz.70792 (1 ms clicks / 25 ms, 68-78 dB, 1 h/day; no adverse events over up to 2 years; cognitive efficacy unproven); Galambos, Makeig & Talmachoff 1981 PNAS 78:2643-2647; Picton et al. 2003 Int J Audiol 42:177-219 (40 Hz ASSR maximal in awake adults); Schwarz & Taylor 2005 Clin Neurophysiol 116:658-668; Orozco Perez, Dumas & Lehmann 2020 eNeuro 7(2) (monaural 40 Hz on 380/420 gives the highest SSR; no mood change); Ross & Lopez 2020 Sci Rep 10:7002; Ross et al. 2014 J Neurophysiol 112:1871-1884; Draganova et al. 2008 Cereb Cortex 18:1193-1200; Grose & Mamo 2012 Ear Hear 33:187-194 (40 Hz binaural response exists but is smaller; older adults respond less, 9/14); Reedijk, Bolders & Hommel 2013 Front Hum Neurosci 7:786 (340 Hz carrier, 10 Hz and 40 Hz beats, 3 min pre-exposure then task; benefit only in low eye-blink-rate individuals, so 'possible for some listeners'); Lutz et al. 2004 PNAS 101:16369-16373 (gamma 25-42 Hz is a correlate of trained meditation, not something a tone creates); Martorell et al. 2019 Cell 177:256-271 (mouse data; Soula et al. 2023 Nat Neurosci 26:570-578 failed to entrain native gamma or reduce amyloid, so no dementia claims); Cade & Coxhead 1979 The Awakened Mind / Wise 1995 (simultaneous alpha + beta/gamma pattern; anecdotal, design metaphor only) |
| Stage 6 - Return (SMR/beta re-alerting, ends in silence) | 55.5–60 | L 250.0 / R (250 + f_beat) Hz at 0 dB, the 400 Hz pair returns at -6 dB from 56:00 with the same offset (gentle widening of the percept for the climb). WHY: same two research carriers; 14 Hz is well inside the binaural detection range (ceiling ~30 Hz, Perrott & Nelson 1969) and inside Sterman's SMR band (12-15 Hz), the practitioner-standard landing zone (Monroe/Hemi-Sync 'return to C-1', Mind Alive SMR sessions). 14.3 Hz would coincide with the second Schumann mode; we use 14.0 and label any Schumann talk as symbolic. | 10 → 14 | Binaural bus: smoothstep 10 -> 14 Hz over 55:30-58:30 (1.33 Hz/min), hold 14 Hz 58:30-59:40, then a 20 s raised-cosine fade to silence 59:40-60:00. The track ENDS IN SILENCE, not in a fade into deep sleep.; Isochronic 170 Hz: from 56:30 the pulse rate decouples from the binaural schedule and ramps 10 -> 16 Hz by 58:30 (Lane 1998's 16 Hz beta rate), -9 dB, Hann 50% duty (at 16 Hz: period 62.5 ms, on-time 31.25 ms); 16 Hz vs 14 Hz are in different stimulus classes (binaural vs acoustic AM) so they do not produce a cancelling interference; fade out with the master.; 40 Hz monaural pair and click train: already faded out by 56:00.; Breath pacer: cycle shortens linearly 10.0 s -> 7.5 s (6 -> 8 breaths/min) over 56:00-58:00 on the noise bed and drone, +/-3 dB; the listener is cued to breathe with it.; 110 Hz drone: fades out 58:30-59:30 (60 s raised-cosine).; Pink-noise bed -25 dB until the master fade.; Voice (voiced render): 58:30-58:50 counted ascent 'one... two... three... four... five, eyes open when you are ready, move your hands and feet'; 59:35 'session complete; write before you do anything else'.; Bowl: final struck bowl at 59:30 (-12 dB) whose 20 s tail is cut by the master fade; no bowl after silence. | Lane, Kasian, Owens & Marsh 1998 Physiol Behav 63(2):249-252 (beta 16/24 Hz beats vs theta/delta 1.5/4 Hz: vigilance and mood; basis of 'no driving'); Sterman & Friar 1972 EEG Clin Neurophysiol 33:89-95 (SMR 12-15 Hz); Monroe/Hemi-Sync return-to-waking convention and Listening Tips (count down 10 to 1, cool water; practitioner); Monroe patent US 5,356,368 wake-up segment on 400/416 Hz (beta range); Harner 1980 / FSS: return and account after the callback (traditional); Hemi-Sync Listening Tips: 10-15 min before driving; Perrott & Nelson 1969 JASA 46:1477-1481 (binaural beat ceiling ~30 Hz, so 14 Hz is safely perceptible) |

*Global layers:* BINAURAL BUS (the only layer that requires headphones): two carrier pairs, 250 Hz and 400 Hz, same-sign right-ear offset = f_beat(t) from ONE shared smoothstep schedule (10 -> 10 -> 6 -> 6 -> 4.5 -> 4.2 -> 10 -> 14 Hz); float amplitudes identical in L and R (ILD 0.00 dB, verified < 0.1 dB narrowband RMS); dry, centred, no reverb, no panning, no delay, no width, no per-channel limiting, no EQ; only the relative gain of the two pairs changes (60 s linear ramps). Never a carrier above 500 Hz, never a beat below 4.0 Hz or above 14 Hz on this bus.; ISOCHRONIC / MONAURAL REINFORCEMENT BUS (speaker-safe, headphone-leak-safe): 170 Hz tone Hann-gated at 50% duty at the current f_beat(t) (decoupled to 16 Hz only in the final 3.5 min), identical in both channels, -6 to -9 dB; plus the Stage-5 40 Hz layer (380 + 420 Hz summed, -9 dB) and 40 Hz click train (1 ms clicks / 25 ms, 300 Hz-6 kHz, -12 dB). Carriers chosen so that every tonal component is >= 1 ERB from every other (170 vs 250: 80 Hz; 250 vs 380: 130 Hz; 110 vs 170: 60 Hz; 72 vs 110: 38 Hz), so no unintended beats or roughness are created.; DRUM BUS (Stage 4 only): physically modelled 72 Hz frame drum at f_beat(t) 4.5 -> 4.2 Hz, humanised (sigma 4 ms, +/-10% velocity), alternating +/-0.35 pan, callback pattern at 38:30-39:30; synthetic chamber reverb return -15 dB (T60 3.6 s, pre-delay 20 ms, HF damping 3.8 kHz) on this bus only.; BOWL BUS: one synthesized 600 Hz bowl (ratios 1 : 2.83 : 5.42 : 8.77, 2 Hz doublet on the fundamental, T60 20/12/7/3 s) struck once at every stage boundary (4:55, 12:55, 24:55, 39:25, 55:25, 59:30) as the listener's map; -12 dB peak; same reverb return; no other musical events.; DRONE: 110 Hz sine with +/-0.15% detuned copies (0.3 Hz shimmer) and no harmonics, -10 dB (phon-corrected: aim for equal loudness with a 400 Hz tone at 60 phon, which is roughly +10 dB SPL), breath-swelled; present 1:00-59:30. Labelled in the guide as an aesthetic/humming-reference tone with a weak archaeoacoustic story (Jahn 1996; Cook 2008 n=30, unreplicated), not a 'brain switch'.; NOISE BED + BREATH PACER: pk3 pink noise at -25 dB relative to the binaural pair, 0.7 shared / 0.3 independent per channel, -6 dB notches at 198-302 Hz and 332-468 Hz; carries the 0.1 Hz coherent-breathing swell (+/-3 dB raised-cosine, 4.5 s rise / 5.5 s fall, with a 2 -> 6 kHz low-pass sweep on the inhale), cycle 7.5 s -> 10 s in Stage 1, 10 s through Stages 2-5 (+/-1.5 dB during the journey), 10 s -> 7.5 s in Stage 6. The swell is applied ONLY to the noise bed and the drone, never to any carrier.; VOICE BUS (two renders: voiced and voice-free): seven short cues (0:20, 1:30, 4:00, 24:15, 38:25, 45:30, 58:30/59:35), each <= 25 s, mono-centred, -6 dB, 120 Hz-6 kHz band-limited, dry, scripted from Gingras 2014 (journey instruction), Harner (intention/return), Hemi-Sync (count-up) and the Sharpless/Cheyne sleep-paralysis script ('do not struggle, slow the out-breath, move a finger'). The voice-free render ships with the same script in the guide.; CALIBRATION TONE: 1 kHz, -20 dBFS, 5 s, both channels, at 0:00 before the fade-in; the guide instructs 'set this to the level of a quiet speaking voice, then do not touch the volume' (target ~60-65 dB SPL at the ear; research range 65-70 dB SPL; well under WHO/ITU-T H.870 80 dBA/40 h).; MASTER: 4th-order Butterworth high-pass at 25 Hz (SOS, state carried across blocks), single scalar gain to -18 LUFS integrated, soft safety limiter at -1 dBFS that must log zero touched samples, 20 s raised-cosine end fade, digital silence after 60:00. No content below 25 Hz, no infrasound claims, nothing above 8 kHz louder than -30 dBFS.

*Mastering:* Sample rate 48,000 Hz; synthesis and mix in float64; master high-pass 25 Hz (4th-order Butterworth SOS); integrated loudness -18.0 LUFS (acceptable -19 to -17; the 15-min daily version may sit at -17), measured per ITU-R BS.1770-4 with K-weighting and -70/-10 gating; short-term loudness never above -12 LUFS-S; loudness range 6-10 LU (do not exceed 11, which keeps Spotify/Apple from applying dynamic processing); true peak <= -1.5 dBTP on a 4x-oversampled measurement (meditation material of sines + noise has a crest factor of ~8-12 dB so peaks land near -6 to -10 dBFS and the limiter must never engage; log zero limited samples); narrowband L/R level match on every binaural carrier < 0.1 dB; DC < 1e-4; no content below 25 Hz; nothing above 8 kHz louder than -30 dBFS; 20 s raised-cosine end fade into true digital silence. Calibration: 1 kHz tone at -20 dBFS for 5 s at 0:00 so the listener sets ~60-65 dB SPL ('quiet speaking voice'); at that setting a 60-min session uses a small fraction of the WHO/ITU-T H.870 80 dBA/40 h weekly allowance. Delivery formats: (a) FLAC 24-bit 48 kHz stereo, ffmpeg -c:a flac -compression_level 8 -sample_fmt s32 (verify ffprobe bits_per_raw_sample=24; expect ~12.4 MB/min, ~745 MB for 60 min, ~187 MB for 15 min); (b) MP3 320 kbps CBR via libmp3lame with joint stereo DISABLED (-joint_stereo 0, -compression_level 0, -ar 48000; LAME equivalent lame -b 320 -m s -q 0) so each channel is quantised independently and the interaural phase relation that carries the beat is preserved (~2.4 MB/min, 144 MB for 60 min); after encoding, decode and re-run the Hilbert beat-trace and ILD checks; (c) optional 16-bit WAV with TPDF dither (+/-1 LSB, no noise shaping) for CD/legacy players. Apply the final gain as a single numpy scalar; if ffmpeg loudnorm is used only as a check, run it two-pass with linear=true and confirm 'Normalization Type: Linear' and force -ar 48000 (loudnorm resamples to 192 kHz internally). Metadata/ID3: title, 'HEADPHONES REQUIRED - do not use while driving' in the comment field, 18+ label. Two renders of each length: voiced and voice-free; no 'sleep' variant is produced from this design because the 40 Hz block and the alerting return are intentional (a sleep variant would be a separate render that stops at Stage 3 and fades at 6 Hz without the 40 Hz layer).

*Daily short version:* THRESHOLD DAILY (15:00, same carriers, same levels, -17 LUFS allowed). 0:00-0:05 1 kHz -20 dBFS calibration tone; 0:05-0:10 silence; 0:10-0:20 fade-in. STAGE A 'Arrive' 0:10-2:00: binaural 10 Hz on L 400/R 410 (0 dB) + L 250/R 260 (-3 dB); isochronic 170 Hz at 10 Hz -9 dB from 1:00; pink bed -25 dB with breath swell 7.5 s -> 10 s cycle by 2:00; 110 Hz drone -10 dB from 0:30; voice (or guide) 0:20 'set the tone to a quiet speaking voice', 1:30 'one sentence of intention; you will return at the count'. STAGE B 'Descend' 2:00-4:00: beat smoothstep 10 -> 6 Hz (1 Hz/min, twice the full-track rate but still gentler than the lab, which started at 6 Hz cold); isochronic rate follows, level -9 -> -6 dB; 250 pair rises to 0 dB, 400 pair to -3 dB over 3:00-4:00. STAGE C 'Theta' 4:00-12:00: 6.00 Hz hold on 250/256 + 400/406 for 8 min (plus the 2-min ramp = ~10 min of theta-range exposure, matching the 10-min effective duration in Jirakittayakorn 2017 and Orozco Perez's 8-min measurable blocks); isochronic 170 Hz at 6 Hz -6 dB; breath 10 s +/-3 dB; no drum (the drum journey needs >= 13 min and belongs to the full track); optional 60 s silent intention-scene window 9:00-10:00 (no voice, bed continues). STAGE D 'Return' 12:00-15:00: beat smoothstep 6 -> 14 Hz over 12:00-14:00 (4 Hz/min; faster than the full track, acceptable because 5-min blocks with abrupt switches were tolerated in Chaieb 2017 and the ascent limit is comfort, not evidence), hold 14 Hz 14:00-14:40; isochronic decouples to 16 Hz from 13:00 (-9 dB); breath cycle 10 -> 7.5 s over 12:30-14:00; drone out 14:00-14:30; voice count 1-5 at 14:00; 20 s master fade 14:40-15:00 to silence. No 40 Hz layer in the daily theta version. ALTERNATE DAILY (optional, for alternate days or mornings when alertness is wanted): identical bookends but Stage C replaced by 4:00-13:00 at 10 Hz binaural on the 250 pair with the 380+420 Hz monaural 40 Hz layer (-9 dB) and the 1 ms/25 ms click train (-12 dB) for 9 min, then the same 14 Hz return; mirrors Reedijk 2013/Ross & Lopez 2020 and keeps total 40 Hz dose well under Chan 2022's 1 h/day. Usage: once or twice daily, cap 90 min/day total across versions, one rest day per week, never while driving, journal within 2 min, 10-15 min before driving.

### Design 1: Gateway Journey: C-1 to Focus 21 and Return (Monroe-school staged consciousness journey, 60 min) (60 min)

*Rationale:* ARC: The track follows the Monroe Gateway ladder literally (C-1 preparation -> count into Focus 10 -> Focus 12 journey -> vibrational state -> Focus 15 'no time' -> Focus 21 'the edge' -> counted return to C-1) because that is the lineage the request invokes, and it fuses it with the one indigenous-derived element that has instrumented measurements (the 4-4.5 Hz journey drum, 13.5 min sustained, with a formal call-back) and with the Eastern/Hermetic sound frames that explicitly describe ascent through realms by sound (Gateway resonant hum, seven-vowel planetary ladder, Gyuto chord voice, bonsho and bowl markers). Every felt 'event' the user is asking for (third-eye/inner focus, cosmic alignment, manifestation window, vibration mastery, travel) has a dedicated stage with an instruction, because the research shows the instructions, not the frequencies, produce the journey phenomenology (Gingras 2014; Rouget 1985).

NUMBERS: Carriers 136.1 / 272.2 / 408.3 Hz are one octave-coherent family built on the Cousto 'Om / Earth-year' tone, chosen so the drone, bowls, bonsho (137.8 Hz), Gyuto voice (68.05 Hz) and vowel ladder stay consonant; the primary 408.3 Hz pair sits in the 400-440 Hz region where binaural beats are most salient and where the only measured binaural ASSRs were found. Beat rates descend 10 -> 7.83 -> 7 -> 4.5-4 -> 3 -> 2.13 -> 1.5 Hz and climb back to 14.3 Hz, each anchored to something specific: alpha pre-exposure (Garcia-Argibay), theta onset/peak timing (Jirakittayakorn: 5-10 min onset, 10-15 min peak), 7 Hz meditation facilitation (Lavallee, weak), 4-4.5 Hz drum theta (Maxfield; Gordon 2026), 2 Hz pulse synchronization (Will & Berg), the unofficial Focus reconstructions (F10 10->7, F12 4-5, F15 2-3, F21 0.5-2), and the SMR/beta re-alerting evidence (Sterman; Lane 1998). Descent never exceeds 0.5 Hz/min, ascent ~1.4-1.7 Hz/min, all phase-continuous. Each plateau lasts 7-13 min and something changes at every plateau to counter habituation. The 40 Hz element uses the click structure proven safe at 1 h/day (Chan 2022) but only for 90 s and at -12 dB because this is a relaxation track, not a GENUS protocol.

REDUNDANCY: the beat is delivered three ways at once (binaural pairs, monaural AM on 340.25 Hz, Hann-gated isochronic on 204.15 Hz, plus the Monroe septon in Focus 12), so the track degrades gracefully on speakers and gives the stronger AM-type steady-state response, with the binaural pairs reserved for the cross-frequency/network effects that only binaural beats showed (Orozco Perez 2020).

WHAT WAS AVOIDED AND WHY: no reverb, panning, width or sweep on any binaural pair (reverb destroys the interaural phase relation); no binaural carrier above 408.3 Hz and nothing near 1 kHz; no beat above 30 Hz on a binaural pair; no step changes; no opposite-sign offsets; the Solfeggio tones (396-963 Hz, 1999 numerology, mutually inharmonic and inharmonic with the C# centre) are omitted; no 'epsilon' sub-sonic tones (implemented as a 0.25 Hz swell); no infrasound; no death-whistle or 15-25 Hz flicker-like pulsing; the track never ends in delta and ends on silence after an SMR close; the deepest (<= 3 Hz) material is held to ~11 min; the single 40 Hz cue is excluded from the sleep render; the 136.1 Hz pair is weighted lower and its sub-3 Hz 'rotation' percept is used deliberately as a body sensation rather than fought.

HONESTY (for the guide): binaural-beat 'entrainment' is unproven (Ingendoh 2023: 5 of 14 EEG studies for, 8 against); the replicated effects are modest anxiety reduction (Padmanabhan 2005, ~26 % state-anxiety drop), small cognitive effects (g ~0.4-0.45), theta increases while the sound plays, and well-documented hypnagogic phenomena (heaviness, vibrations, imagery, time distortion) that are experiences, not verified events. No tradition surveyed assigns a frequency to the pineal gland, no sound changes pineal structure or melatonin (Wahbeh 2007 measured melatonin and found no change), the Focus-level frequencies are reconstructions TMI never published, the 1983 memo is a one-officer literature review, 7.83 Hz 'Earth tuning', 136.1 Hz 'Om', 110 Hz 'brain switch', Thompson epsilon, the Awakened-Mind pattern and the 4x7 call-back are practitioner conventions, and the 'vibration/frequency/manifestation' vocabulary is 20th-century New Thought (Kybalion 1908). The design therefore maximises what sound can honestly do: a well-built, safe, redundant, culturally sourced induction into a deep absorbed state with a clear map, an intention frame, a manifestation window grounded in the yogic sankalpa / Neville SATS tradition, and a reliable return.

SAFETY BUILT IN: calibration tone and quiet master; 18+; headphones only; no driving (theta/delta beats impair vigilance, Lane 1998); physician consult for epilepsy, auditory, psychiatric, pacemaker (keep headphones off the chest), pregnancy (side-lying or semi-reclined); stop rules for persistent unreality, panic, ringing; dose one full session or two short per day, cap 90 min, one rest day a week; WBTB use limited to 2-3 nights/week and contraindicated in bipolar disorder; sensory-restriction imagery and sleep-paralysis-type sensations normalised with the 'slow the out-breath, move a finger' script; Cheetah House / crisis-line pointers; nothing in the track is a medical claim.

The 40 Hz element is NOT a binaural pair: it is a click train identical in both ears (40 Hz binaural beats do give a measurable but smaller ASSR; Draganova 2008, Ross 2014; the click/AM route gives the larger response, Schwarz & Taylor 2005). | 2.127 → 1.5 | f_beat 2.127 -> 1.5 Hz over 46:00-47:00, hold 1.5 Hz 47:00-50:00; Seven-vowel ladder (synthesized formant voice, never a sampled liturgical recording): A E E-long I O U O-long on 136.10 / 153.11 / 170.13 / 181.47 / 204.15 / 229.67 / 255.19 Hz (ratios 1, 9:8, 5:4, 4:3, 3:2, 27:16, 15:8 from the 136.1 Hz centre; Pythagorean/Timaeus intervals), each vowel 7 s with 1 s overlap, 3 rounds 46:30-49:00, capstone 'I-A-O' 49:00-49:20 on 181.47 / 136.10 / 255.19 Hz; -10 dB, equal-power rotation 0.03 Hz, wet -16 dB; 40 Hz click train 48:30-50:00: 1 ms band-limited clicks (2-pole band-pass 300-3000 Hz) every 25 ms (4 % duty, Chan 2022 / Martorell 2019 pulse structure), identical both ears, -12 dB relative, 20 s raised-cosine fade in/out; omitted entirely from any sleep variant; Isochronic 204.15 Hz at 1.5 Hz, -12 dB; monaural AM 340.25 Hz at 1.5 Hz depth 50 %, -12 dB; Chamber drone 110/70 Hz fades out 46:00-47:00; tanpura -10 dB; pink bed -25 dB; breath pacer 12 s +/-3 dB; Tingsha-like accent pair at 2.6 kHz and 2.612 kHz (12 Hz pair detuning), 3 s decay, -14 dB, one at 46:00 and one at 49:20 (vendor-sourced pitch range, decorative marker only); Narration 46:10: 'Focus 21. The edge. Receive.' Silent thereafter | Monroe Focus 21 definition (McDonnell 1983; Gateway Wave VI 'Odyssey' Intro to Focus 21); reconstruction F21 = 0.5-2 Hz (SINE, unofficial); Demetrius On Style 71; Greek Magical Papyri (Betz 1986); Nag Hammadi NHC VI,6 Discourse on the Eighth and Ninth (seven-vowel ascent, 'IAO'); Plato Timaeus 35b-36b; Boethius De institutione musica I.2 (pure-ratio intervals); Chan et al. 2022 PLoS ONE 17:e0278412 (1 ms clicks every 25 ms, 68-78 dB, 1 h/day safe); Martorell et al. 2019 Cell 177:256-271; Galambos, Makeig & Talmachoff 1981 PNAS 78:2643-2647 (40 Hz ASSR); Draganova 2008; Ross & Lopez 2020 Sci Rep 10:7002; Voss et al. 2014 Nat Neurosci 17:810-812 (25/40 Hz tACS and lucidity; electrical, single study); Lutz et al. 2004 PNAS 101:16369 (gamma 25-42 Hz in trained meditators; a correlate, not something a tone creates) |
| Stage 9 - Call-back, Return to C-1 and SMR close | 50–60 | 272.2 Hz pair and 408.3 Hz pair (0 / -3 dB rebalancing to 408.3 at 0 dB by 54:30 since the beat re-enters the 7-15 Hz range where the 400 Hz carrier is most salient), 136.1 Hz pair -6 dB until 57:00. From 57:00 the primary pair glides 408.3 -> 400.0 Hz (left) with right = 400 + f_beat, ending at L 400.0 / R 414.3 Hz = 14.3 Hz (Monroe US 5,356,368 wake-up uses 400/416 Hz; 14.3 Hz chosen as SMR and as the Schumann second mode, symbolic). | 1.5 → 14.3 | Drum call-back 50:00-51:30 (labelled 'FSS core-shamanism convention', not an ethnographic universal and not stated on the FSS site): four groups of seven beats at 1.8 Hz with 1.5 s gaps (~20 s), 30 s of rapid drumming at 8.0 Hz (sigma 2 ms, no accent), four groups of seven again (~20 s); -6 dB RMS, pan alternation off (centred), reverb -14 dB; f_beat: 1.5 -> 8.0 Hz over 50:00-54:30 (1.44 Hz/min smoothstep), 8.0 -> 10.0 Hz over 54:30-57:00 (0.8 Hz/min), 10.0 -> 14.3 Hz over 57:00-59:30 (1.7 Hz/min); phase-continuous throughout, no step changes; Deep layers out: Gyuto voice, 110 Hz drone and vowel ladder all gone by 50:00; monaural AM 340.25 Hz follows f_beat at depth 50 %, -9 dB until 57:00 then fades; Awakened-Mind stack 54:30-57:00 (Cade/Wise simultaneous bands, implemented on separate buses so the binaural bus keeps one rate): binaural primary 8 -> 10 Hz (alpha, 0 dB); monaural AM 340.25 Hz re-tasked to 5.5 Hz (theta, -9 dB); isochronic 204.15 Hz Hann pulses at 15 Hz (light beta, -12 dB); 136.1 Hz binaural pair re-offset to 1.5 Hz (delta, -6 dB); amplitudes decrease upward 0 / -3 / -6 / -12 dB as in the modern-lens recipe (delta here at -6); 57:00-59:30 SMR close: only the primary pair (gliding to 400 / 414.3 Hz) plus isochronic at 14.3 Hz -12 dB and tanpura; 136.1 pair and AM layer fade over 20 s at 57:00; Narration: 52:00 'The call-back has sounded. Retrace your path. Return to the body.'; 55:00-57:30 counted ascent 10 -> 1, one number per 15 s, 'one: fully awake, alert, refreshed, C-1'; 57:40 'Move your fingers and toes. Take a deep breath. Open your eyes.'; Bonsho strike at 54:30 (137.8 / 301.8 / 378.6 Hz) marking the stack entry; bowls off; Breath pacer: 12 s -> 10 s cycle 50:00-54:00, then off; pink bed -25 dB to the end; tanpura -8 dB, fades 58:30-59:30; 59:30-60:00: 30 s raised-cosine fade of everything to digital silence; file ends on silence | Harner 1980 The Way of the Shaman; shamanism.org/drumming ('fast callback rhythm'; 4x7 pattern is workshop convention); Monroe counted return to C-1; Hemi-Sync Listening Tips ('count down slowly from 10 to 1'); Cade & Coxhead 1979 The Awakened Mind; Wise 1995 The High-Performance Mind (pattern described qualitatively, no replication); Sterman & Friar 1972 EEG Clin Neurophysiol 33:89-95 (SMR 12-15 Hz); Lane, Kasian, Owens & Marsh 1998 Physiol Behav 63:249-252 (beta 16/24 Hz beats: better vigilance and mood; theta/delta degrade vigilance); Monroe US 5,356,368 (400/416 Hz wake-up envelope); Schumann second mode ~14.1-14.3 Hz (symbolic); Hemi-Sync / clinical grounding practice (journal, water, no driving 10-15 min) |

*Global layers:* Binaural bus (headphones only): carriers 408.3 / 272.2 / 136.1 Hz (3x, 2x, 1x the 136.10 Hz Cousto 'Earth-year' centre; all <= 500 Hz, primary in the 400-440 Hz salience sweet spot), scheme A offsets (right = left + f_beat on every pair, same sign always), one shared smoothstep f_beat(t) schedule, ILD 0.00 dB, dry, centred, no reverb/pan/width/per-channel processing; per-stage exceptions (2 Hz body pair in Focus 10, 1.5 Hz delta pair in the Awakened-Mind block) are stated explicitly above; carriers at least 1 ERB apart (ERB 39 / 54 / 68 Hz at 136 / 272 / 408 Hz); Monaural AM bus: 340.25 Hz (2.5 x 136.1) sinusoidal AM at f_beat(t), depth 40-90 % by stage, -8 to -12 dB, identical both ears (speaker-safe, larger steady-state response than binaural beats: Orozco Perez 2020, Schwarz & Taylor 2005); replaced by the Monroe septon (200 Hz base, 200+f spacing) during Focus 12; Isochronic bus: 204.15 Hz (1.5 x 136.1) Hann-gated 50 % duty pulses at f_beat(t) (at 6 Hz: period 166.7 ms, on-time 83.3 ms, 41.7 ms rise/fall), -10 to -12 dB, identical both ears; sidebands at 204.15 +/- k f stay inside one ERB; 40 Hz click train (Stage 8 only): 1 ms band-limited clicks every 25 ms, 4 % duty, -12 dB, 90 s, never in a sleep variant; Pink-noise bed: Kellet pk3 pink, -25 dB relative to primary carrier RMS (Monroe patent >= 10 dB below beats; design default -25), two streams mixed 0.7 shared / 0.3 independent (IACC ~0.7), 'phased' by a 6 s raised-cosine crossfade between two pink streams (Monroe US 5,356,368) plus optional comb-filter sweep at 1/8 Hz; -6 dB notch 340-476 Hz (+/-1 ERB around 408 Hz) so the primary carrier stays clear; present from 0:15 to 59:30; Tanpura drone: 204.15 / 272.2 / 272.2 / 136.1 Hz (Pa-Sa-Sa-Sa, 5-8-8-1), 48 harmonics at 1/n, jivari window sweep, +/-0.15 % detuned unison copies (0.2-0.4 Hz shimmer), -8 to -10 dB; the one layer present in every stage; its 3rd harmonic (408.3 Hz) sits 12 dB or more below the primary carrier; Breath pacer: raised-cosine amplitude swell on bed + tanpura/pad buses only, +/-2 to +/-3 dB (never more than +/-6 dB), with a matching low-pass sweep 2 kHz -> 6 kHz on the rise; cycle 4 s (0-3 min, So'ham 15/min) -> 16 s (Bhramari 4 in / 12 hum) -> 10 s (0.1 Hz resonance breathing, 4.5 s rise / 5.5 s fall, from minute 12) -> 12 s (Focus 15/21, 0.083 Hz, inside the 4.5-6.5 breaths/min resonance range) -> off after 54:00; never modulates the binaural carriers; Singing-bowl bus: additive modes 1 : 2.83 : 5.42 : 8.77 : 12.87 with 1-3 Hz doublet splits, T60 30 / 15 / 8 / 4 s, fundamentals 207.7 and 277.2 Hz (G#3, C#4: within 10 Hz of the drone's 204 / 272 Hz so they do not beat harshly against it), strikes every 20-40 s, rim-singing swells 30-60 s with 0.6-1.2 Hz tremolo; reverb (T60 3.6 s) on this bus only; cultural label: the 'Tibetan singing bowl' is a 20th-century market category; Bonsho boundary strikes: partials 137.8 / 301.8 / 378.6 Hz, 2-3 Hz mode beat, 45 s decay, at 39:00 and 54:30 (and optionally at every stage boundary), -6 dB peak; Drum bus: physically modelled frame drum (f0 72 Hz) at 4.5 -> 4.0 Hz locked within +/-0.5 Hz of f_beat during Focus 12 / vibration, 24:00-37:00 (13.5 min), plus the call-back 50:00-51:30; jitter sigma 4 ms, velocity +/-10 %, +/-0.35 pan alternation, reverb -14 dB; Voice/chant bus (all synthesized, no sampled liturgical recordings): hum/choir drone 136.1/272.2/68.05 Hz (Stage 2), Gyuto-style 68.05 Hz chord voice with 5th/10th harmonics boosted (Stages 6-7), seven-vowel ladder on pure-ratio tones (Stage 8); equal-power rotation 0.02-0.03 Hz; reverb -16 dB on this bus only; Pad bus (non-binaural): 136.1 + 204.15 + 272.2 Hz pad carrying the REBAL sweep (5.5 s) and the vibrational-state head-to-feet sweep (5.0 s), -10 dB; Chamber drone (Stage 7): 110 Hz + 70 Hz, synthetic stone-chamber reverb T60 4 s, 0.25 Hz epsilon swell, -9 dB; Narration bus: dry, centred, -10 dB relative, inserts no closer than 40 s apart, silent during the journey, patterning, no-time and edge windows; scripts are Monroe's own preparatory components (box, resonant tuning, affirmation, REBAL, count 1-10, counted return) plus one-line journey/return cues; Mix-bus chain: sum in float64 -> 4th-order Butterworth SOS high-pass at 25 Hz (stateful across chunks) -> single scalar gain to -18 LUFS integrated -> soft safety limiter at -1.0 dBFS (should never engage) -> TPDF dither only for 16-bit exports

*Mastering:* Sample rate 48 kHz throughout (no resampling in the chain except the 4x oversampled true-peak meter). Integrated loudness -18 LUFS (acceptable -20 to -16; the 15 min daily version may sit at -17 LUFS), set by a single scalar gain computed in numpy (BS.1770-4 K-weighting and gating) rather than ffmpeg loudnorm, which silently switches to dynamic (compressing) mode when measured LRA exceeds the target or TP cannot be met linearly and upsamples to 192 kHz internally; if loudnorm is used as a check, run two-pass with linear=true, force -ar 48000, and confirm 'Normalization Type: Linear'. Short-term loudness never above -12 LUFS-S; loudness range 6-10 LU (never above 11 so streaming platforms and loudnorm do not compress it). True peak <= -1.5 dBTP (ITU-R BS.1770-4, 4x oversampling) leaving margin for the MP3 encode; Spotify normalises to -14 LUFS and lifts quiet masters only within their true-peak headroom, Apple Sound Check targets -16 LUFS, so a -18 LUFS / -1.5 dBTP master plays back quietly and unmodified, which is the intent (target listening level 55-70 dB SPL at the ear, the 65-70 dB SPL range used in the EEG studies; a 60 min session at 75 dBA uses under 5 % of the WHO/ITU-T H.870 weekly allowance of 80 dBA for 40 h). High-pass the mix at 25 Hz (4th-order Butterworth SOS); no intentional tone below 55 Hz except the 68.05 Hz voice fundamental and the 70 Hz chamber partner; no content above 8 kHz louder than -30 dBFS (tinnitus/hyperacusis). Binaural bus: left/right carrier RMS within 0.1 dB, no reverb, no stereo widening, no mid/side processing, no per-channel limiting; stereo-linked gain only. Soft safety limiter at -1.0 dBFS ceiling (5 ms look-ahead, 200 ms release) logged, expected to engage on zero samples (sine + noise material has an 8-12 dB crest factor so peaks land near -6 to -10 dBFS). Render float64 -> write float32 WAV (scipy.io.wavfile) -> ffmpeg -i master.wav -c:a pcm_s24le master24.wav for the archival 24-bit WAV (optional 1 LSB TPDF dither at 2^-23). Deliverables: (1) FLAC 24-bit 48 kHz: ffmpeg -i master.wav -c:a flac -compression_level 8 -sample_fmt s32 out.flac, then ffprobe must report bits_per_raw_sample=24 (fallback flac --best --bps 24); expect ~12.4 MB/min (60 min ~745 MB; the noise bed dominates size, lowering it 6 dB saves 10-15 %). (2) MP3 320 kbps CBR with joint stereo OFF so each channel is quantised independently and the interaural relation that carries the beat is never folded into a side channel: ffmpeg -i master.wav -c:a libmp3lame -b:a 320k -joint_stereo 0 -compression_level 0 -ar 48000 -id3v2_version 3 out.mp3 (LAME equivalent: lame -b 320 -m s -q 0 --noreplaygain); never add -q:a VBR with -b:a; expect 2.4 MB/min (60 min ~144 MB, 15 min ~36 MB); after encoding, decode and re-run the Hilbert beat-rate verification. (3) Optional 16-bit WAV/FLAC with TPDF dither (+/-1 LSB, no noise shaping). Metadata: title, stage cue-sheet (CUE/chapters at the nine stage boundaries), and a text note that the file is intentionally NOT mono-compatible and requires stereo headphones with correct L/R placement, no spatial/3D/crossfeed/EQ DSP, no bone conduction. Ship three renders: full 60 min 'return' ending (default), full 'sleep' ending (Stage 7 held at 2.127 Hz, no call-back, no 40 Hz, 60 s fade), and the 15 min daily version. Prepend the 5 s 1 kHz -20 dBFS calibration tone to each so the guide can say 'set this to a comfortable speaking level and leave the volume alone'.

*Daily short version:* DAILY 'FOCUS 10 LITE' - 15:00 total (12-15 min window), -17 LUFS, same carriers and buses, same calibration tone. Structure (times in m:ss): 0:00-0:05 1 kHz -20 dBFS calibration tone; 0:05-1:30 C-1 + Resonant Tuning: bed and tanpura in (8 s fades), binaural 408.3 (0 dB) / 272.2 (-3 dB) pairs at 10.0 Hz from 0:20, hum drone 136.1/272.2/68.05 Hz -8 dB, pacer 16 s (4 in / 12 hum), narration at 0:10 'hum the out-breath' and at 1:10 a 20 s affirmation slot (listener's one-sentence intention). 1:30-4:00 descent 10.0 -> 7.0 Hz (1.2 Hz/min, faster than the full track's 0.5 Hz/min ceiling, stated in the guide as the compromise a 15 min format forces); count 1 -> 10 at 1:30-3:30 (one number per 12 s); isochronic 204.15 Hz at f_beat -10 dB enters 2:00; 136.1 pair with 2.0 Hz 'body' offset at -6 dB enters 2:30; pacer glides to 10 s cycle by 3:00. 4:00-12:00 plateau: hold 7.0 Hz (an 8 min block, the measurable-response minimum; Lavallee 7 Hz facilitation and Jirakittayakorn's 10 min theta peak land here); monaural AM 340.25 Hz at 7 Hz depth 50 % -9 dB; bowl strike (207.7 / 277.2 Hz alternating) every 40 s; pacer 10 s +/-3 dB; silent SATS/patterning window 5:00-11:00 with a soft bowl bell at 7:00 and 8:08 bracketing the 68 s block; no drum (a 4.5 Hz drum would fight a 7 Hz beat; a separate 15 min FSS-style drum-only journey file at 4.2 Hz with call-back can be offered for users who want the Focus 12 journey daily). 12:00-14:30 return: beat 7.0 -> 13.0 Hz (2.4 Hz/min; ascent is less rate-sensitive and this stays under the full version's 2 Hz/min only loosely, stated honestly), 136.1 pair and AM layer fade over 20 s at 12:00, isochronic re-tasked to 13 Hz -12 dB, counted ascent 10 -> 1 at 12:10-13:50 (one per 10 s), bonsho strike at 12:00, narration 14:00 'fingers, toes, deep breath, open your eyes'. 14:30-15:00 30 s raised-cosine fade to silence; ends on silence. Carriers glide 408.3 -> 400 Hz over 12:00-14:30 so the close lands on L 400.0 / R 413.0 Hz. Use up to twice daily (morning default), never while driving; the daily and full versions share the same disclaimer, stop rules and journaling instruction.

### Design 2: Axis Mundi: A World-Traditions Sound Journey (Threshold, Descent, Journey, Bardo, Ascent, Return) (55 min)

*Rationale:* Architecture: the 55-minute arc fuses the session shapes the traditions actually use - Satyananda's eight-stage Yoga Nidra (settle, sankalpa, rotation, breath, visualisation, sankalpa, externalise), the core-shamanism 15-minute drum journey with a formal call-back, the Bardo Thodol's sound-light-rays climax with its own recognition instruction, the Mevlevi/Hekhalot/Evenki stepped ascent, and the Monroe-style counted return - and places them on a beat schedule that respects every psychoacoustic constraint in the dossier: carriers 136.1/272.2/408.3 Hz (<= 500 Hz, strongest-percept region for the top carrier), one offset scheme (same-sign on all carriers, so nothing cancels), ramps <= 0.52 Hz/min down and <= 1.7 Hz/min up with smoothstep phase-continuous frequency changes (no jumps, no amplitude crossfades on the binaural bus), plateaus of 5-7 minutes each with a timbral change at each plateau to counter the habituation seen after ~10-15 min in Jirakittayakorn 2017, a 3.0 Hz floor (binaural beats below 2-3 Hz are heard as motion; delta impairs vigilance), 40 Hz delivered as monaural AM for 3-4 quiet minutes, never as a binaural pair, no reverb or panning on the carriers, and an ending in the 12-15 Hz SMR band on silence rather than a sleep fade. Every acoustic layer doubles the beat as a speaker-safe, physiologically stronger stimulus (monaural/isochronic on 204.15 Hz; bowl doublets tracking f_beat in the ombak manner; the frame drum locked within 0.25 Hz of f_beat), which is also where the better evidence lies (Schwarz & Taylor 2005; Orozco Perez 2020; Will & Berg 2007). Tuning: everything is built in pure ratios on 136.10 Hz (9:8, 5:4, 4:3, 3:2, 27:16, 15:8, 2:1), so the Vedic drone, the Pythagorean/Timaeus ladder, the Chinese 3:2 lu chain, the Bon three syllables and the Greek seven vowels stay consonant with one another and with the bonsho (137.8 Hz) and didgeridoo/Gyuto (68 Hz) registers; the Solfeggio set (Puleo/Horowitz 1999, six tones; 963 Hz added by vendors) is omitted because it is mutually inharmonic, clashes with the C# centre and has no evidence - the guide says so. Why these numbers: 4.25-4.5 Hz for 15 min is the only tradition-derived tempo with instrumented measurement (Harner CD 4.2 Hz; FSS ~5 bps; Maxfield >= 13-15 min); 6.07 Hz is within 0.1 Hz of the 6 Hz beat that raised theta within 10 min; 7.83 and 14.3 Hz are legitimate theta and SMR rates that also carry the Schumann symbolism; 10 Hz alpha is the pre-exposure and creativity rate (Reedijk 2013); 8.51/4.25 Hz are Cousto's own octave reductions of 136.1 Hz, used for internal coherence and labelled numerological; 110/70 Hz is the Neolithic chamber band (weak, one unreplicated study); 300.9-311.6 Hz are the measured Chavin pututu fundamentals. Breath pacing, humming and the listener's own chanting are where the measurable physiology lives (6/min resonance breathing, Bernardi 2001; Bhramari nitric oxide, Weitzberg 2002; OM fMRI, Kalyani 2011), so the track cues them rather than merely playing at the listener. Honesty, to be printed in the guide: binaural-beat 'entrainment' of the cortex is not established (Ingendoh 2023: 5 for, 8 against, 1 mixed); the reliable findings are a weak steady-state response at the beat rate and modest anxiety/mood effects (g ~0.4-0.45; Padmanabhan 2005 ~26 % state-anxiety drop); no study shows any sound frequency affecting the pineal gland, its calcification (present in ~62-72 % of adults) or melatonin (Wahbeh 2007 measured melatonin after 60 days of daily beats: no change); the ajna-pineal identification is Theosophical (Blavatsky 1888, Leadbeater 1927), 'vibration/frequency' vocabulary is Kybalion/New Thought (1906-1908) and Cousto (1978), the Tesla quotes are apocryphal, the CIA Gateway memo is a one-officer literature essay, Rouget 1985 and Gingras 2014 show that journey phenomenology comes from set, setting and culture at least as much as from the sound, and nothing here enables travel between dimensions or times; what the design can honestly deliver is a well-built, safe stimulus for deep relaxation, absorbed hypnagogic imagery, a shamanic-style journey frame with a reliable return, a 'manifestation' window in the tradition's own sense (sankalpa / state akin to sleep), and an alert finish. Safety is built into the structure: calibration tone and 55-70 dBA target, no content that depends on flicker, 3.0 Hz floor, <= 20 min of continuous deep theta before a change, thunder capped at -3 dB re carriers, an opt-out for the Bardo imagery, a mandatory return and externalisation, a separate sleep ending, 18+ labelling, headphone-only instructions (no bone conduction, speakers, spatial/crossfeed DSP), no driving, no alcohol/cannabis/psychedelics, physician consult for epilepsy, psychiatric, pacemaker (keep headphones off the chest), pregnancy (semi-reclined) and tinnitus, the three-tier stop rules, and credit to every tradition with the statement that all vocal and ceremonial material is synthesised, never sampled from owned recordings.

| Stage | Minutes | Carrier(s) | Beat Hz | Layers | Sources |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Stage 1 - Harae / Threshold Bell (shofar call, bonsho, tanpura) | 0–3 | L = {136.10, 272.20, 408.30} Hz; R = L + f_beat(t) (Scheme A: identical same-sign offset on all three carriers, never mixed with ratio-scaling). 408.30 Hz = 3 x 136.10 (perfect twelfth above the Om drone) is the perceptual workhorse because the 350-500 Hz region gives the strongest, widest-range beat percept (Licklider, Webster & Hedlun 1950; Oster 1973; Schwarz & Taylor 2005 ASSR only at ~400 Hz; Grose & Mamo 2012 10/10 listeners at 390/430 Hz vs 1/10 at 810/850). 272.20 Hz = 2 x 136.10 sits near the 250 Hz carrier of Jirakittayakorn & Wongsawat 2017 and the 240 Hz carrier of Karino 2006. 136.10 Hz is Cousto's 1978 sidereal-year octave tone (2^32 / 31,556,926 s), the pitch most practitioner traditions share as 'Om'; its beat percept is weak (ERB 39 Hz) so it functions as the tonal anchor, labelled numerological. All carriers <= 500 Hz; nothing binaural above 1 kHz. | 10 → 10 | 0:00-0:05 calibration tone: 1 kHz sine at -20 dBFS, identical both channels, 20 ms raised-cosine edges, then 1 s silence (listener sets 'comfortable speaking level', target 55-70 dBA).; 0:06 bonsho (temple-bell) strike: additive partials 137.8 / 301.8 / 378.6 Hz (measured Hojobo bell; 137.8 is a happy coincidence with 136.1), T60 45 s on the lowest partial, 2-4 Hz amplitude beat from split modes, 5 ms Hann attack + 10 ms band-passed click, peak -6 dB re binaural pair, on the reverb bus (T60 3.6 s).; 0:10-0:40 shofar-type natural-horn call on a 306.19 Hz fundamental (9:8 above 272.2, i.e. a Pythagorean tone above the drone) with 2nd-5th harmonics at -6/-10/-14/-18 dB and 1 % breath noise: tekiah 3 s, shevarim 3 x 1 s, teruah 9 x 0.3 s, tekiah gedolah 8 s; -9 dB; panned +0.2 right (HYP IV.67 right-ear instruction for inner-sound cues); 15 ms attacks, 300 ms releases.; 0:40 binaural bus fades in over 5 s (raised cosine) at 10.0 Hz: 408.3 Hz pair 0 dB (reference, ~-20 dBFS RMS per ear), 272.2 Hz pair -3 dB, 136.1 Hz pair -3 dB (raised from a naive -6 dB because ISO 226 equal-loudness needs ~8-12 dB more SPL at 136 Hz at 50-60 phon). Dry, centred, ILD 0.00 dB, no reverb, no panning, no limiting on this bus.; 0:45 tanpura/Om drone fades in over 8 s: strings Pa-Sa-Sa-Sa = 204.15 / 272.20 / 272.20 / 136.10 Hz (5-8-8-1 tuning), one pluck every 2.5 s (10 s cycle), 48 harmonics at 1/n with a sweeping jivari window; -8 dB; 2 unison copies detuned +/-0.15 % for 0.2-0.4 Hz shimmer.; 0:45 pink-noise bed enters: -25 dB re carriers, Kellet pk3 pink, decorrelated as 0.7 shared + 0.3 independent per channel (IACC ~0.7), -6 dB notch 340-476 Hz (+/-1 ERB around 408.3) so the main carrier stays clear.; Breath pacer: 4.0 s cycle = 15 breaths/min, the Hamsa/Dhyanabindu Upanishad 21,600-breaths-per-day rate (Surya Siddhanta: 1 prana = 4 s), rendered as a +/-2 dB raised-cosine swell (2 s rise / 2 s fall) on the noise bed and tanpura bus only, with a 2-4 kHz low-pass sweep on the rise; the binaural carriers are never modulated.; Call-back pattern: none in this stage. | Shinto norito/harae (Britannica 'norito'; Green Shinto on kotodama) - purification placed at the very start; Shofar sequence tekiah/shevarim/teruah; Maimonides, Hilchot Teshuvah 3:4 'Awake, sleepers, from your sleep'; Bonsho acoustics: Acoust. Sci. & Tech. 25(5) 2004 (137.8 / 301.8 / 378.6 Hz modes); Joya no Kane practice of the lingering yoin; Hamsa Upanishad v.2 and Dhyanabindu Upanishad vv.61-62 (21,600 breaths/day); Surya Siddhanta 1.11-12 (1 prana = one breath = 4 s); Tanpura: Raman 1921 Proc Indian Assoc Cultiv Sci 7:29-33 (jivari); 5-8-8-1 tuning; Cousto, The Cosmic Octave (1984/1988) 136.10 Hz, labelled numerological; Licklider, Webster & Hedlun 1950 JASA 22:468-473; Oster 1973 Sci Am 229(4):94-102; Schwarz & Taylor 2005 Clin Neurophysiol 116:658-668; Grose & Mamo 2012 Ear Hear 33:187-194 (carrier choice); Garcia-Argibay, Santed & Reales 2019 Psychol Res 83(2):357-372 (pre-exposure before task; g = 0.45) |
| Stage 2 - Sankalpa / Resonant Tuning (Bhramari hum, So'ham, intention) | 3–7 | Same three carriers (136.10 / 272.20 / 408.30 Hz, R = L + f_beat).
